# Fitting and module families

[Documentation](../README.md) / [Protocol index](../protocol.md)

Catalog fitting rules, weapon tiers, ammunition, repairs, propulsion and tackle.

These records preserve milestone evidence and historical observations. Dates, schema versions, test results and live acceptance apply to the recorded change; they do not establish acceptance of later changes.

## Local ancillary armor and shield repairs, November 7, 2026

Milestone 5's first checkpoint enables all **18 ordinary static local ancillary repairers** in build **3662168**: ten shield boosters (group **1156**) and eight armor repairers (**1199**), including the Xarasier variants and capital-size module profiles. The eight published Abyssal mutation templates remain excluded. The production inventory retains **6,079 entries**, **235 admitted hulls** and **76 drones**; module/rig admissions increase from 3,366 to **3,384**, and charge admissions from 800 to **801**. Exactly these 18 modules and Nanite Repair Paste change. Every repairer retains an overheating gap; paste retains its unfinished overheating-repair consumer. This checkpoint does not close advanced defenses or the full completion program.

Shield effect **4936**, `fueledShieldBoosting`, repairs at cycle start. A loaded cycle consumes one capacitor charge and pays no capacitor; an empty cycle pays the effective capacitor cost for the same repair amount. Charge group comes from catalog attribute **605**, `chargeGroup2`, and charge sizes must match exactly. Small boosters use meaningful size **0**, including Cap Booster 25. Larger boosters cannot load smaller-size charges to extend their burst. Actual charge volume determines magazine capacity: for example a small booster holds seven normal 25s or nine Navy 25s, with the same repair per activation.

Armor effect **5275**, `fueledArmorRepair`, always spends capacitor at cycle start and repairs at cycle end. Paste is type **28668**, group **916**, with volume **0.01**. Required paste is the module's integer `chargeRate`; the catalog's charged armor multiplier **1886** is three. If fewer units remain, all are consumed and only the additional paste bonus scales with that fraction. A medium repairer with base amount 207 and rate four repairs 310.5, 414 or 517.5 with one, two or three paste, and 621 with four. Empty operation repairs its base amount. Catalog fitted-group limits enforce one ancillary armor repairer while allowing ordinary repairers alongside it.

The existing fitting graph supplies earned skill, rig and hull modifiers before payment. Shield Compensation reduces the empty shield capacitor cost; charged cycles remain free of capacitor cost. Armor duration follows its prerequisite repair skills. Real-budget Incursus and Breacher tests verify their trained repair bonuses. Full-profile tests use artificially enlarged Ibis fitting/resource budgets to exercise every static module, including capital sizes; they establish module behavior, not capital hull admission or real-budget capital fits. The module graph retains the nominal armor amount: the pinned tooltip applies its own charged multiplier, so projecting the fueled amount into the graph would count that multiplier twice.

Fuel ownership, stack form, positive quantity, exact compatibility and magazine capacity are checked in the same transaction as capacitor, health or pending repair, and cooldown. A rejected request leaves all of them unchanged. Existing loading/unloading checks reject changes during a paid cycle or reload. Reload uses the catalog's **60,000 ms** deadline. Schema remains **104**: existing owned charge stacks, module cooldowns and paid local-repair rows persist the result without new saved entity fields. Restart retains remaining paste, spent capacitor and the exact paid armor amount/deadline; that amount completes once. Shield repair is already committed at cycle start. Restored pending armor cycles use the native ancillary effect identity and do not invent offline repeats.

Native activation dispatch accepts the two fueled effect names and publishes the actual **4936/5275** effect identities through the ordinary HUD and ship-state prime. Quantity changes use the existing magazine adapter, including zero at depletion; observer graphics reuse `effects.ShieldBoosting` and `effects.ArmorRepair`. A fueled chain finishes its last paid interval and stops when its magazine empties. The client may then request an ordinary cargo reload according to its own auto-reload setting. No server-side auto-reload preference is invented. Explicitly activating an empty module supports its unfueled mode and repeats while payment succeeds. Manual stop finishes the paid interval.

Evidence: the pinned client archive SHA-256 is **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**, and catalog fingerprint remains **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**. Inert inspection of `shipmodulebutton.pyj.AutoReload` and `Update` establishes the client-owned setting and inactive-effect/zero-quantity trigger. `clientDogmaLocation.pyj.LoadAmmoTypeToModule` resolves matching cargo stacks and invokes the existing load route. `godma.pyj.GetReloadTimes` reads next activation and reload time; `moduleButtonTooltip.pyj.AddArmorRepairersInfo` applies the charged multiplier when a charge exists. No client Python was executed and no game was launched. Partial-paste tooltip rendering remains unverified.

CCP's [No Brakes balancing record](https://www.eveonline.com/fr/news/view/no-brakes-retribution-1.1-balancing) establishes capacitor use, proportional partial-paste bonuses, reload duration and the armor fitted limit; current amounts come from the pinned catalog rather than that historical balance patch. The open-source EOS [fueled armor](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/blob/c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494/eos/eve_obj/effect/repairs/fueled_armor_repair.py) and [fueled shield](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/blob/c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494/eos/eve_obj/effect/repairs/fueled_shield_boosting.py) calculators at revision **c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494** support fitting interpretation, not an authoritative server lifecycle. No source was copied. This checkpoint changes no Carbon-owned Blue, transport or Destiny simulation rules; earlier engine references remain applicable.

`tools/Export-AncillaryRepairCatalog.py` opens the catalog read-only, rejects input overwrite and exports all **26 published profiles**, fuel, prerequisite skills and relevant hull/effect definitions into the **219-type** `AncillaryRepairCatalog.json` fixture. Regenerate with:

```powershell
$catalog = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Eden\catalog.sqlite'
python tools/Export-AncillaryRepairCatalog.py --catalog $catalog
```

**Automated validation:** Tool/solution restore and solution build passed with zero warnings/errors (1.53 seconds combined). The **448-case affected selection passed** in **154.51 seconds** for the test command, including all **62 new ancillary cases**. After adding explicit unchanged-armor and reopened paid-amount assertions, the seven affected cases passed again (7.73 seconds; build 2.81 seconds). Both Python suites passed (**8 catalog**, **9 formatting** cases, 0.91 seconds combined). Formatting verification checked **1,068 files**, including the 140-column check (14.13 seconds). Documentation checks verified **527 local links and anchors**. Fixture export reproduced byte-for-byte; input overwrite was rejected and source catalog bytes were unchanged (2.70 seconds). Fixture SHA-256: **EB84BAB31954D47E585D72A0F22B4CD41BE86BBE3A3C1B8FEA8DEBAD471903B4**.

Coverage includes all static profiles and mutation exclusions, proportional paste, exact charge sizes and volumes, empty capacitor payment and skills, real-budget hull bonuses, atomic rejection, one-AAR limits, saved paid deadlines and amount, native prime/effects/quantity, cargo reload, depletion/repeats and manual stop. Twelve warmed ancillary-handler frames assert zero SQL commands, opened connections and fitting recalculations for both families; this is a handler check, not a complete session-frame or checkpoint benchmark. Existing ordinary repairs, ammunition, remote repairs/assistance, fitting constraints/snapshots, resident fits and historical migrations passed in the affected selection.

Development started with exact `AncillaryRepairRuleTests.PartialPasteConsumesRemainingFuelAndScalesOnlyTheBonus`, `AncillaryRepairTests.AllStaticTiersConsumeRealFuelAndCommitTheirPublishedRepairAmount` and `AncillaryRepairTests.NativeDepletionFinishesThePaidCycleThenPermitsARealClientReload` filters. The final affected filter was

```text
FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Repair.|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Ammunition.|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.RemoteRepairTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.RemoteAssistanceTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.FittingSnapshotTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.ResidentFitTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.ModuleFittingTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.FittingConstraintTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.FittingCapabilityRulesTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.ShipContentCoverageTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Fixtures.TestWorldSeedTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting.DataModelOwnershipTests|FullyQualifiedName=Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting.ServerServicesTests.SharesWorldAndGateButIsolatesPilotSessions|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Propulsion.TackleTests.RestartAndVersionThirtyFourMigration|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Propulsion.MicrowarpdriveTests.VersionThirtyMigration|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.CloneGradeTests.VersionThirtySixMigration|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.ShipStorageTests.VersionThirtySeven|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.EnergyTierTests.Version31|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.MissileCombatTests.VersionThirtyThree|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.ShipSafetyTests.VersionThirtyFiveMigration
```

Final assertion-only filter:

```text
FullyQualifiedName=Eden.Protocol.Tests.Repair.AncillaryRepairTests.RejectedArmorActivationsLeaveFuelResourcesHealthAndCooldownUnchanged|FullyQualifiedName=Eden.Protocol.Tests.Repair.AncillaryRepairTests.CheckpointReopenRetainsSpentPasteAndExactlyOnePaidArmorCompletion
```

An initial build lacked the Gameplay repair namespace import in the audit; it was corrected. The first broader affected run failed the historical version-28 repair migration and was interrupted. Its downgrade fixture retained the newer remote-assistance triggers while removing their active-module table. Shared historical schema teardown now removes remote assistance before older schema changes; the exact failing method passed after correction. No production migration was changed. The interrupted run is not passing validation. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

**Live acceptance:** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks:** On a copied save, observe fueled quantity/reload HUD behavior with auto-reload enabled; compare partial-paste tooltip and actual repair; confirm observer repair graphics finish with a manual stop. Automated protocol checks establish emitted content, not native rendering or gameplay feel.

Ancillary remote repairs, mutadaptive remote repairs, reactive hardeners, assault damage controls and signature suppressors remain in milestone 5. Overheating and paste repair remain in milestone 6. The separate NPC neutralizer repair-suppression evidence blocker remains open in milestone 4.

## Ancillary remote armor and shield repairs, November 7, 2026

Milestone 5's targeted ancillary checkpoint enables all **eight published static profiles** in build **3662168**: four ancillary remote shield boosters (group **1697**, types **41480–41483**) and four ancillary remote armor repairers (**1698**, **41476–41479**). These use high slots and retain the catalog's one-fitted-module limit for each group. They share the existing player remote-repair lifecycle and the preceding local ancillary fuel rules. Mutadaptive repairs, reactive hardeners, assault damage controls and signature suppression remain unfinished, as does overheating.

The production inventory retains **6,079 entries**, **235 admitted hulls**, **801 charges** and **76 combat drones**. Module/rig admissions increase from 3,384 to **3,392**. Exactly **18 entries** change: these eight modules and ten admitted hulls' ancillary consumer checks. Bantam, Inquisitor, Navitas, Burst, Osprey and Augoror now pass their recorded hull checks; Scythe, Exequror, Orca and Porpoise retain other unfinished roles. The same hull IDs remain admitted. These inventory states do not establish live acceptance or production/acquisition coverage.

Effect **6652**, `shipModuleAncillaryRemoteShieldBooster`, heals at cycle start. A loaded activation consumes one matching-size capacitor charge instead of capacitor; empty activation pays its effective capacitor need for the same shield amount. Effect **6651**, `shipModuleAncillaryRemoteArmorRepairer`, always pays capacitor and heals at cycle end. Paste triples a fully fueled nominal armor repair; a partial magazine consumes all remaining paste and proportionally scales only the added bonus. For example, the medium profile's base 145 armor becomes 217.5, 290, 362.5 or 435 with one through four paste. These remote modules declare fuel through **chargeGroup1 (604)**; local ancillary modules use **chargeGroup2 (605)**. Shared typed compatibility accepts the deliberate field at the adapter boundary and preserves meaningful size zero for small shield charges.

The authoritative transaction validates selected ownership, online high-slot membership, prerequisites, a locked live player target, system, targeting/effect range, assistance policy, real fuel ownership/stack/capacity and the paid deadline. Fuel, capacitor, assistance consequences, saved cycle and cooldown commit together; rejection preserves health, fuel and resources. Shield repairs publish only after commit. Armor saves the paid fueled amount, then samples current target distance and remote repair impedance at completion. The extra paste bonus precedes those shared application multipliers. The existing graph applies earned emission skills and source hull modifiers once.

Ancillary group selectors are now operational remote repair consumers in the shared hull graph. Actual-budget **Bantam (582)** and **Inquisitor (590)** fits verify their trained repair amounts and group-based range/falloff bonuses. All eight module profiles, including capital sizes, are also exercised using explicit artificial Ibis budgets; those cases do not establish capital hull admission or real-budget capital fits. Mutadaptive group selectors remain deferred.

The native dispatcher, ship-state prime and HUD use the actual **6651/6652** identities, with existing `effects.RemoteArmourRepair` and `effects.ShieldTransfer` observer graphics. Fuel notifications include the depleted quantity. A fueled chain stops after its final paid interval; the client's existing auto-reload preference may then request the ordinary cargo load, subject to its **60,000 ms** deadline. Loading during a paid cycle/reload rejects. Explicit empty activation supports unfueled repeats and manual stop finishes the current paid interval. Lock loss, offlining, source/target disappearance, docking, warp and disconnect retain the existing remote cancellation rules. Schema remains **104**: spent fuel and cooldown persist, while restart clears connection-owned remote cycles without refund or offline armor repair. This differs from the paid local armor lifecycle recorded above.

Evidence is the pinned catalog fingerprint **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**, including effect phase/range/impedance metadata, module descriptions, fuel fields and hull selectors. The previously inspected build-3662168 archive **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368** supplies the shared client-owned auto-reload and magazine behavior; no new client execution or live gameplay occurred. CCP's [Citadel patch notes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/patch-notes-for-eve-online-citadel) record corrected remote ancillary paste consumption; actual rates/amounts come from this pinned catalog. EOS's [ancillary remote armor](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/blob/c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494/eos/eve_obj/effect/repairs/ship_module_ancillary_remote_armor_repairer.py) and [ancillary remote shield](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/blob/c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494/eos/eve_obj/effect/repairs/ship_module_ancillary_remote_shield_booster.py) calculators at **c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494** confirm their remote repair families and generic reload-cycle fitting interpretation. They do not provide an authoritative server lifecycle; no source was copied. The [Carbon Destiny thunks](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp) at **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f** were reviewed for the engine boundary; this checkpoint changes no codec, transport or movement rules. That engine reference does not supply EVE-specific remote fuel rules.

`tools/Export-AncillaryRemoteRepairCatalog.py` reads the source catalog without opening a saved world and exports all eight modules, normal/Navy fuel, paste, prerequisites and relevant hull/effect records into the **185-type** `AncillaryRemoteRepairCatalog.json` fixture. It rejects input overwrite. Regenerate with:

```powershell
$catalog = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Eden\catalog.sqlite'
python tools/Export-AncillaryRemoteRepairCatalog.py --catalog $catalog
```

**Automated validation:** Tool/solution restore and solution build passed with zero warnings/errors (2.99 seconds combined). The **650-case affected selection passed**, including all **38 new ancillary remote cases**, in **461.46 seconds** for the test command (feature build 1.11 seconds). The expanded combat/utility hull matrices account for this broader run's longer duration. Both Python suites passed (**8 catalog**, **9 formatting** cases, 0.92 seconds combined); formatting verification passed **1,070 files**, including the 140-column check (15.40 seconds). Documentation checks verified **531 local links and anchors**. Export reproduced byte-for-byte, source catalog bytes remained unchanged and input overwrite was rejected (4.63 seconds). Fixture SHA-256: **77852E5F9CEFE39E22C102A0B5A8C5BB88976DDE40F4756FBD2C5B15B74A6BA5**.

The new cases cover all profiles, zero/partial/full paste, capacitor skills, primary versus secondary fuel fields, magazine capacity and exact sizes, group limits, actual-budget hull bonuses, completion distance/impedance, unchanged-state rejection, restart cancellation and retained payment/deadlines. Native cases assert targeted effect identity, ship-state prime, fuel quantity, observer and recipient updates, paid depletion, cargo reload, empty repeats, manual stop and lock-loss/disconnect cancellation. Twelve warmed ancillary remote-handler frames for each family assert zero SQL commands, opened connections and fitting recalculations; this does not measure complete session frames or checkpoint cost. The final affected filter was

```text
FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.AncillaryRemoteRepairTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.RemoteRepairTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.RemoteHullRepairTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Repair.AncillaryRepair|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Ammunition.|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.ModuleFittingTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.FittingConstraintTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.FittingCapabilityRulesTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.ShipContentCoverageTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.FittingSnapshotTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Combat.ResidentFitTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.SmallHullBonusTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.TechOneHullTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.UtilityHullTests|FullyQualifiedName=Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.SmallHullTests.CohortIncludesEverySmallTechOneEntryAndEveryEffectHasAnExplicitOwner|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.CombatHullTests
```

Development started with the exact `AncillaryRemoteRepairTests.EveryStaticProfilePaysRealFuelAndRepairsAtItsOwnPhase` filter (eight cases), followed by exact native depletion/reload filters (two cases). A protocol test initially lacked its ShipEnergy namespace import. The first new-class run passed 30 cases and failed two cancellation assertions that compared capacitor against its pre-lock value after recharge; assertions now compare payment at the same instant. The completion-impedance fixture initially omitted the compact attribute definition required by its foreign key; it now seeds that definition and preserves the real source metadata. A setup string syntax error was also corrected. Exact corrected methods passed. The first broader affected run was interrupted after the shared hull-bonus verifier rejected the newly operational ancillary groups because it lacked probes. It now conditionally loads the ancillary fixture and verifies both groups through real module attributes; the three exact logistics cases passed after correction. These earlier failed or interrupted runs do not establish validation. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

**Live acceptance:** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks:** On a copied save, observe targeted ancillary fuel/reload HUD state and the recipient's repair update; confirm observer graphics stop after lock loss or manual stop. Native rendering, input and gameplay feel remain unverified.

## Signature suppression calculation foundation, November 7, 2026

Milestone 5's signature suppression foundation covers all **five published static modules** in group **1154**: Signature Radius Suppressor I (**4409**), Domination (**83643**), Dread Guristas (**83644**), Zohar's Modified (**83660**) and Hakuzosu's Modified (**83661**). `SignatureSuppressionRules` defines immutable profiles, shared dogma modifiers and exact duration/reactivation deadlines. `SignatureSuppressionCatalog` validates their complete four-effect shape, declared default, policy and hull restrictions. This validator is not yet connected to production fitting; all five remain unadmitted. No protocol handler, payment operation, saved schema or runtime behavior changes in this foundation.

Effect **8108**, `signatureRadiusBonusOnline`, applies attribute **3113** to ship signature radius **552** while online. Default active effect **8109**, `targetSpectrumBreakerBonus`, applies **3114** to the same ship attribute, pays capacitor attribute **6** and takes duration from **3115**, rather than the ordinary duration attribute. Both native modifiers are **post-multipliers (operation 4)**. Signature radius declares `stackable=0`; the foundation preserves the shared dogma stacking bucket and does not invent an exemption for either effect. The active modifier joins the online modifier, rather than replacing it. An initial five-profile test incorrectly expected their unpenalized product; it failed and was corrected against the pinned stacking metadata. For example, the basic module on a 420-signature input gives 378 while online and approximately **115.04908824** in the foundation's active graph with the second stacking penalty applied to its weaker online reduction. Native rendering/acceptance is unverified.

The basic profile has online/active factors **0.9/0.3**, **12 seconds** duration and **660 GJ** cost. Faction factors are **0.875/0.25** or **0.875/0.275**, with **14 seconds** duration; officer factors are **0.85/0.2** or **0.85/0.225**, with **16 seconds** duration. Dread Guristas and Hakuzosu use **520 GJ**; the others use 660. All declare **150,000 ms** reactivation delay, one fitted/active module per group and disallowed repeating activation. The prepared schedule adds the duration and then the reactivation delay, rounds fractional ticks upward and rejects negative clocks or overflow. Runtime cancellation and restart handling remain operational work.

Signature Masking (**4411**) selects group 1154 and increases attribute 3115 by **5% per level**. Calculation tests use its actual selector and source modifier with levels 0, 1, 3 and 5; the basic duration reaches 15 seconds at level 5 without changing cost, cooldown or multipliers. This does not yet add the skill's consumer to production fitting. The three exact restriction alternatives are Battleship (**27**), Black Ops (**898**) and Marauder (**900**). The common typed restriction reader accepts those alternatives and rejects cruiser/corvette inputs. Additional or missing restriction slots, altered fitted/active/repeat policies, missing defaults, malformed profiles and changed effect operators fail validation.

Evidence is build **3662168**, catalog fingerprint **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**, including all module, attribute, effect and prerequisite records. CCP's [March 2021 rebalance notes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/patch-notes-version-19-02) establish the module's passive/active roles, duration skill, costs, delay and allowed hull classes. Its [Equinox expansion notes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/equinox-expansion-notes) identify the four faction/officer additions. Current values and stacking flags come from the pinned catalog. No source was copied. Shared dogma behavior and preceding engine references remain unchanged; this checkpoint changes no Carbon-owned Blue, transport or Destiny rules and conducts no client execution.

`tools/Export-SignatureSuppressionCatalog.py` exports all five modules, Signature Masking, representative hull/MWD inputs and prerequisite closure into the **25-type** `SignatureSuppressionCatalog.json` fixture. It reads only the catalog and rejects an output path matching its input. Regenerate with:

```powershell
$catalog = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Eden\catalog.sqlite'
python tools/Export-SignatureSuppressionCatalog.py --catalog $catalog
```

## Signature suppression operation and timed activation expiry, November 7, 2026

Milestone 5's signature suppression operation enables all **five published static profiles** from the preceding calculation foundation. The existing **235-hull** admission set is unchanged. Eligible admitted battleships can fit one suppressor in a medium slot; the catalog's Battleship, Marauder and Black Ops alternatives remain enforced without enabling the later T2 cohorts. All five profiles are tested on assembled/boarded **Raven (638)** hulls with their actual budgets and trained prerequisites. No artificial fitting or resource capacities are added. Signature Masking **4411** now reaches the production module graph once, increasing duration through attribute **3115** while leaving cost and reactivation delay unchanged.

Online suppression uses effect **8108** and the native **3113** multiplier. Paid activation uses default effect **8109**, `targetSpectrumBreakerBonus`, adding the **3114** multiplier to the shared dogma stacking bucket. The preceding foundation's pinned stacking interpretation is retained. On the current catalog Raven, base signature 410 becomes 369 online with the basic module and approximately **112.30982424** during its paid burst. Targeting signature reads, lock-time calculations and turret application inputs consume the same committed graph; expiry restores the online reduction without leaving an active penalty behind. Ordinary MWD modifiers continue to use their existing operator bucket.

The World operation validates live selected ownership, singleton/online medium-slot membership, current catalog support, allowed hull, prerequisites, capacitor, health and the paid deadline. It also rejects an already active suppressor in an inconsistent saved fit. Capacitor, activation marker and cooldown commit together, and repeated activation cannot pay twice. The native route accepts only self activation outside warp, normalizes repeat requests to a single cycle and finishes the paid interval after manual deactivation. Offlining, docking, ship change, warp, disconnect and service disposal release the active bonus; payment and its saved cooldown are retained. Restart clears connection-owned markers without replay, preserving remaining reactivation time.

Schema **105** adds `ExpiresAt` to `WorldActiveModule`, with an index over positive deadlines. Its default **zero** preserves ordinary repeating-module markers. The paid cooldown remains in the existing module-cooldown row. The migration checks actual column presence, so historical fixture replays and repeated initialization remain safe; version-104 coverage preserves inventory, marker default and paid cooldown without a grant or refund. Checkpoint/reopen tests retain spent capacitor and the exact saved ready time while clearing the active bonus at restart.

`LiveTimedModules` checks the earliest positive deadline under the existing world owner before coherent reads, including repeated reads within one owner lease. It removes only expired timed markers, causing ordinary fitting revision invalidation before observer or combat snapshots are published. The unchanged/absent deadline cache depends on activation rows and schema, so warmed frames do not scan SQL. Row hooks remain enabled when optional combat/read views are disabled; those switches do not disable lifecycle correctness. Rollback never publishes an uncommitted marker/deadline, and rewinding the clock after expiry does not revive a removed bonus. The SQL fallback's signature-read transaction also commits expiry.

The activation-membership view depends on `WorldActiveModule` and actual item membership, excluding quantity-only updates. The existing tackle sweep now checks only warp-core activation markers, so an active suppressor does not create unrelated tackle reads. A regression updates cargo quantity and asserts zero subsequent SQL, connections or fit recalculations for suppression/tackle/fit views. These changes preserve ordinary indefinite markers and their existing lifecycle.

Native ship-state primes and `OnGodmaShipEffect` use actual effect **8109**, its paid start, effective duration and zero repeats. Module attributes report capacitor, signature radius and `nextActivationTime`; the saved deadline gates the next activation exactly. Observer graphics use existing `effects.Invulnerability` with the module and source ship identities. Shared contacts expire that graphic independently of a late source connection, and a later full snapshot cannot replay the expired graphic. Protocol tests verify manual stop, expiry, cooldown rejection just before the deadline, exact readiness, owner/observer content and cancellation. Native rendering/input remain unverified.

The production inventory retains **6,079 entries**, **235 admitted hulls**, **801 charges** and **76 drones**. Module/rig admissions increase from 3,392 to **3,397**, changing exactly these five profiles from planned to complete under their recorded checks. They declare no overload effect in the pinned catalog; this does not implement the separate overheating milestone or enable other advanced-defense families.

Source evidence remains the build-3662168 catalog fingerprint **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**, the complete five-profile fixture and the first-party records linked in the calculation foundation. Existing native effect/attribute shapes and preceding Carbon engine references are preserved; no Blue codec, transport or movement arithmetic changes occur. No source was copied and no game or client Python was executed. The unchanged **25-type** fixture remains reproducible through `tools/Export-SignatureSuppressionCatalog.py`.

## T1 and T2 ship rigs, November 6, 2026

Ship fitting now admits all **642 published Tech I/II ship rigs** in the pinned build **3662168**, across 14 groups and small, medium, large and capital sizes. Admission requires the rig-slot effect, complete fitted-effect metadata, a valid size/calibration cost and an ordinary T1/T2 profile. Structure rigs, unpublished types and other technology tiers are excluded. Some stock T1 records omit `metaGroupID`; their explicit `techLevel` still identifies them. The reproducible [rig exporter](../../tools/Export-RigCatalog.py) reads only the catalog and supplies the embedded test fixture, including prerequisite skills, attribute definitions and effect records.

Fitting uses the existing bound ship inventory `Add`/`MultiAdd` routes with automatic allocation or rig flags **92–94**. It requires the selected pilot's docked active ship, owned equipment from its cargo/current hangar, an available hull rig slot, matching `rigSize`, sufficient `upgradeCapacity` calibration, and any catalog hull/group/type allowances. Corvettes have no rig allowance. Fitting one unit from a stack creates an assembled singleton; a failed batch rolls back every unit, slot and calibration change. Rig slots consume neither ordinary rack slots nor turret/launcher hardpoints. `upgradeLoad` reports the sum of installed calibration costs; `upgradeSlotsLeft` reports remaining slots. Rigs cannot be switched online/offline or moved between fitted slots to recover their item.

All rig effects apply while fitted, independently of online state. The existing typed attribute graph evaluates bonuses, drawbacks, native stacking flags and stacking penalties alongside other fittings. It handles hull attributes, group/required-skill equipment modifiers, missile character/warhead modifiers, and drone owner modifiers. Armor rigs in this build increase **inertia**, through attribute 70, rather than directly reducing maximum speed. Shield rigs can increase signature; weapon rigs can increase turret power or launcher CPU; drone rigs can reduce CPU output. The exact catalog determines each individual profile. Jury Rigging has no drawback reduction; the nine specialist rigging skills apply their catalog group reductions through `rigDrawbackBonus` and `drawback`, normally reducing a 10% drawback to 5% at level V. They also appear in native brain replies. The rig types have no fitting skill prerequisites in this catalog; installing a rig does not require inventing a legacy skill gate.

Drone rigs feed the existing light/medium combat-drone profiles and actual command/control ranges. Their speed, optimal range and durability bonuses reach simulation as well as item readouts; pilot drone skills remain applied once by the existing drone rules. Cached owner profiles use the same fitting revision invalidation as other equipment. Bonuses targeting unavailable equipment or systems remain attribute modifiers: fitting a salvage, scanning, remote-repair, command or utility-drone rig does not introduce those separate gameplay systems. The currently supported hull and drone families retain their existing limits. This milestone adds no rig manufacturing recipes or economic supply changes.

Inert inspection of stock `eve/client/script/ui/shared/fitting/fittingSlotController.pyj`, `Unfit`, confirms the client displays **RigUnFittingInfo** and then calls its bound ship inventory's **`DestroyFitting(itemID)`**. The selected-pilot handler validates ownership, active hull, docked state and the installed rig flag. It atomically deletes the rig, updates calibration/resources and sends the existing junk-location `OnItemChange` plus ship/module attribute notifications. Ordinary cargo/hangar move requests also destroy installed rigs, closing a recovery bypass. Resource reconciliation preserves health/capacitor semantics and offlines equipment when needed; removal of cargo expansion is rejected atomically if the remaining cargo cannot fit. Fitted rigs are always destroyed during ship-loss settlement, whereas unfitted cargo rigs retain ordinary loot rolls. Persistence uses existing item/skill rows; **world version 95** adds rig-slot fitting-revision triggers so committed changes invalidate resident ship and owner-profile caches. Repackaging continues to require fittings to be removed first; destructive repackage retries and special rig-preserving hull exceptions are outside the supported hull scope.

Sources: exact-build type, dogma, effect and skill catalog records; inert code-object inspection of the unchanged client archive, SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. Consulted [Carbon Blue Marshal.cpp, revision af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp); existing dictionaries, primitive values and inventory/attribute notifications require no codec or Destiny format changes. Gameplay rig behavior comes from the pinned catalog/client rather than Carbon's lower-level transport code.

That build preceded the concurrent main-branch biomass migration (version 94). The final filter covers the 32 rig cases, version-94 upgrade/reopen, general migrations/initialization, character deletion, inventory ownership and resident fitting caches. ## Multifit item-creation capability, November 9, 2026

The maintainer reported **Invalid Packet Destination: Unsupported local call publicQaToolsServer.CanGiveItemForMultifit**. Build **3662168** `PublicQaToolsClient.CanGiveItemForMultifit` forwards a zero-argument call and returns its Boolean reply directly. `MultiFitWnd.AddButtons` and `SetInventoryStatus` use that capability only to construct and update the public-QA **Create Missing** button. Its `GiveAllPlayer` callback builds `/giveitem` commands through `publicQaToolsServer.SlashCmd`. Ordinary fitting controls do not depend on a positive capability reply.

Eden now owns this exact service/method in a selected-pilot handler and returns native Python **False**. The public-QA item-spawning path is unavailable for ordinary and developer accounts alike. Additional positional or keyword arguments fail explicitly; unrelated QA methods remain unsupported. The query changes no inventory, wallet or saved fitting and emits no notifications. This fixes capability discovery; it does not add batch fitting or public-QA commands.

Gameplay reference: [EVE University fitting management](https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/NeoCom#Fitting_Management), which describes multifit as fitting multiple ships. Protocol evidence comes from inert inspection of the hash-verified original script archive, SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. Both relevant modules match the currently staged skill-overlay client byte-for-byte. Consulted [Carbon Blue Marshal.cpp at fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae/src/Marshal.cpp), specifically native Boolean read/write forms. The existing Blue codec is unchanged.

The combined filter `FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Fittings|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Information|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Exploration.CombatAnomalyTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Flight.StationUndockTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions.PilotServicesTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting` passed **234 cases**, zero failed/skipped, in **69 seconds** (**72.85 seconds** test-command wall time). ## Personal saved fitting plans (November 6, 2026)

The user reported **Invalid Packet Destination: Unsupported local call charFittingMgr.SaveFitting** while saving a Brutix Navy Issue fitting. Eden previously returned an empty dictionary for personal fitting reads and had no save implementation. This milestone adds personal plan storage without fitting equipment, buying items or changing the active ship.

Inert inspection of pinned build **3662168** `eve/client/script/environment/fittingSvc.pyj` confirms `SaveFitting(ownerID, fitting)` returns a positive fitting ID; `UpdateFitting(ownerID, fitting, fittingID=...)` overwrites an existing ID; `GetFittings(ownerID)` returns a dictionary keyed by fitting ID. `DeleteFitting(ownerID, fittingID)` and `UpdateNameAndDescription(fittingID, ownerID, name, description)` return None. Each fitting is `utillib.KeyVal` with `name`, `description`, `shipTypeID`, `fitData` and `ownerID`; saved replies additionally include `fittingID` and FILETIME `savedDate`. `fitData` remains an ordered Python list of **(typeID, flag, quantity)** tuples. Names/descriptions retain Unicode and quantities retain integer wire forms.

Native `PersistFitting` updates the local cache itself only for an overwrite. A newly saved fit needs **OnFittingAdded(ownerID, fittingID)** after the successful reply: the native handler invalidates the primed fitting dictionary when that ID is absent, allowing the next draw to fetch the new entry. Eden queues this notification only after a committed create. Rename, overwrite and delete retain the client's existing reply-driven cache updates.

The handler derives the owner from the selected pilot and checks the explicit owner argument and any payload owner field against it. Fitting IDs are owner-scoped for updates, renames and deletion. Saving is allowed both docked and in space; a template does not require owning its listed equipment or meeting fitting/skill/capacity requirements. The catalog must contain a ship or structure hull and every listed item type. Gameplay checks the pinned **50-character name**, **500-character description**, **500 personal fitting** limit, nonempty data, positive quantities, reserved `@@` marker and allowed fitting/cargo/drone/fighter flags. The flag set follows `inventorycommon.const.fittingFlags` and `IsShipFittingFlag`, including three rig, four subsystem and eight service positions, plus hidden modifiers. Eden additionally bounds a template to **1024 entries**. Corporation/alliance storage and bulk import are deferred; their support is not implied by saving a personal plan.

World schema **84** adds `WorldSavedFitting`, with an autoincrement ID, pilot foreign key, owner index, hull, name, description, typed-item JSON and save timestamp. Creates enforce the per-pilot quota inside the same transaction as the insert; overwrites preserve IDs and remain available at the quota. Related fields commit together. Reopening retains plans and deletions. Schema version **83** is reserved for concurrent NPC mining work; that work will create its tables idempotently even for saves already upgraded to version 84 by fittings. No runtime database or client files were edited.

Sources: unchanged staged `code.ccp`, SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**, `fittingSvc` save/prime/verify/cache methods and `appConst` limits. Consulted [Carbon Blue Marshal.cpp at fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae/src/Marshal.cpp) for native dictionaries, lists, tuples and object state. Existing explicit Blue adapters suffice; the codec and transport remain unchanged. Client bytecode was inspected as data, never executed.

**Automated validation:** Tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. Final filter `FullyQualifiedName~SavedFittingsTests|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotFittingTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotServicesTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Market.MarketTests`, run with `dotnet test --no-build` after rebasing onto purchasing fix **1fe56c0**, passed **110 tests** with zero failed/skipped in **21 seconds**. Both Python policy suites (**3 catalog**, **9 formatting**), formatting verification (**763 files**), documentation link/anchor/whitespace checks and `git diff --check` passed. Required restore/build/test/policy/format checks took **43.31 seconds** combined. Coverage includes native request/reply/notification order, preselection rejection, Brutix Navy Issue and in-space saves, Unicode, list/tuple distinctions, overwrite/rename/delete, unknown/foreign owners and IDs, malformed entries, native fitting-location boundaries, quota/reclaimed capacity, forced update rollback, unchanged ships/items/funded wallets, version-82/83 upgrades and restart persistence. The earlier affected filter without market tests passed **77 tests in 16 seconds**; the pre-rebase final check passed the same 77 cases in **15 seconds**, with all required checks passing in **37.02 seconds**. A later funded-wallet refinement passed those 77 cases in **20 seconds** before the purchasing rebase; the run named `integrated.trx` predates that rebase and is not evidence for the combined tree. The first development run exposed an assertion assuming empty dictionaries decode with numeric keys; the fixture now accepts the existing empty-dictionary representation. No full .NET suite was requested or run.

**Live acceptance:** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled. The screenshot is evidence of the original unsupported call; post-change native UI behavior remains unverified.

**Optional maintainer checks:** After updating/rebuilding/restarting the server, save the Brutix Navy Issue as a personal fitting, reopen the fitting browser and reconnect to confirm it remains available. Overwrite the same plan, rename it and delete the test entry.

## Persistent weapon grouping

Build 3662168's dogma location calls now support `LinkWeapons(ship, master, source)`, `MergeModuleGroups`, `PeelAndLink`, `UnlinkModule(ship, master)`, `DestroyWeaponBank`, `LinkAllWeapons` and `UnlinkAllModules`. Link/merge/peel/group-all return the complete master-to-slave-list map. Unlink returns the detached slave ID. Destroy and ungroup-all return None. The client updates its bank cache from these replies. The third member of the four-member ship-state tuple carries the same map for login and boarding. Fitting changes notify `OnWeaponBanksChanged(ship, banks)`.

Gameplay owns typed grouping rules. Supported online high-slot weapons group by exact module type. Merge combines banks, peel moves one slave, unlink detaches one slave and destroy dissolves a bank. Group-all builds one bank per module type in slot order. Ownership, active ship, supported/online modules, active effects and paid cycle/reload deadlines are checked before changes. World schema **70** adds `WorldWeaponLink`, with cascading ship/module references. Existing worlds start with empty banks. Unfitting, offlining and automatic resource shutdown prune invalid membership within the fitting transaction. Destruction cascades through existing inventory cleanup.

A turret bank has one repeating controller and one damage settlement per volley. Each member pays ammunition, independent crystal wear, capacitor and cooldown, with independent accuracy draws. All members prepare in one transaction before combined damage is applied, including fatal volleys. An invalid member rolls back the entire payment and damage. The owner receives each magazine and module effect, all turret space effects and one banked combat message per volley. The bank waits for its slowest cycle and stops as a unit. Missile banks keep one native projectile per launcher, with one atomic launch transaction and one repeating bank controller. Existing projectile impact handling settles each warhead. Reload/unload expands scalar or list bank requests to distinct members. A partial ammunition stack is shared across the bank's remaining magazines. A grouped load that leaves a member without the requested ammunition rolls back.

References are inert inspection of pinned `code.ccp` SHA-256 `9EC838341FCDAAC99080BC849FAB47DBFABBA9EBF97A1AA0F2CBAE474265C6D3`, particularly `clientDogmaLocation.LinkWeapons`, `UngroupModule`, `LinkAllWeapons`, `UnlinkAllWeapons`, `DestroyWeaponBank`, `_MakeShipActive` and `baseDogmaLocation.SetWeaponBanks`. Carbon's public repository inventory was checked for the relevant ownership boundary. Container representations reuse the existing Blue adapters, checked against locally saved [Carbon Marshal.cpp revision fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/fcbf0c2e7f092568adb82edb7b8fb97df75ad4ae/src/Marshal.cpp), including distinct tuple/list readers. Native presentation remains unverified.

**Automated validation.** Integrated resident-fit milestone `c7cbd1a` while retaining revision checks, staged cache publication and the bounded catalog connection around each bank transaction. The final tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. Filter `FullyQualifiedName~WeaponBankTests|FullyQualifiedName~AmmunitionTests|FullyQualifiedName~PilotCombatTests|FullyQualifiedName~MissileCombatTests|FullyQualifiedName~HybridTierTests|FullyQualifiedName~EnergyTierTests|FullyQualifiedName~ProjectileTierTests|FullyQualifiedName~FittingTests|FullyQualifiedName~InventoryTests|FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Hosting|FullyQualifiedName~WorldMigrationTests|FullyQualifiedName~ShipLossTests|FullyQualifiedName~FleetTests|FullyQualifiedName~EquipmentBonusTests|FullyQualifiedName~ResidentFitTests|FullyQualifiedName~ResidentFitFleetTests|FullyQualifiedName~NpcCombatTests` passed **328 cases in 98 seconds**, with two workers. Coverage includes version-68/version-69 upgrades, short ammunition stacks, rejected bank payments, fatal volleys, native group replies, firing/repeat/stop notifications, ship primes, energy/projectile charge rules and missile banks. A dedicated integration case requires zero fitting rebuilds on warm grouped volleys and refreshes after a grouped ammunition-type change. Both Python policy suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting cases), and formatting/diff checks passed. The full .NET suite was not requested or run.

An expanded development run was stopped after mining fixture failures at `MiningTests.Fixture.Client` line 182, before weapon code executed. The representative `MiningTests.NativeActivationWaitsForCyclePaysCapacitorAndRetriesDoNotDuplicateYield` case failed identically on isolated unchanged parent `9b40a03`. This baseline mining limitation remains outside the passing filters. A later focused run exposed redundant bank notifications on ungrouped fitting changes; notifications now compare membership before/after, with coverage for both existing notification order and clearing a pruned bank. An initial grouped-cache test counted an incidental fixture pilot lookup; the corrected measurement scopes the actual volley.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled.

**Optional maintainer checks.** Restart onto the updated server and check the grouped icon/count on the restored Brutix Navy Issue. Fire, stop, reload and split the bank. Reconnect and confirm the saved group returns.

## Shield boosters and physical skillbooks, November 2, 2026

Checked the same build **3662168** and pinned `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37fb2af4fa0ec5c8fa320f34cd28c8b7dbf4917418336b38cba20a7860db0368**. The catalog's default effect for shield-booster group **40** is **4 / shieldBoosting**, with **effects.ShieldBoosting**, capacitor attribute **6**, duration attribute **73**, and `isWarpSafe=1`. Small Shield Booster I **399** uses a medium slot (effect 13), 25 CPU, 2 power grid, 20 capacitor and 26 shield HP per 2,000 ms, requiring Shield Operation **3416** level I. Civilian Shield Booster **10039** belongs to the same supported family but is not a starter market offer. Shield Compensation **21059**, effects **1499/1500**, multiplies capacitor need by its trained level and attribute **851** (-2 percent per level). Overheating and ancillary charge behavior are not implemented.

The native module button reaches the existing `dogmaIM.Activate(itemID, 'shieldBoosting', targetID, repeat)` call, with no target for this self effect. One activation owner dispatches mining, weapons and shield boosters to their scoped controllers. Fitting allocates medium flags **19–26** separately from high flags **27–34**, preserves turret hardpoints, and accounts for CPU/grid across both racks. Godma item and ship primes include medium-slot attributes, online state and active effect 4.

Each boost is one world transaction: validate the owned online assembled medium-slot module on the active ship in space, check skills and durable cooldown, recharge/read shared capacitor and shields, spend capacitor, clamp the shield increase to capacity and save the next deadline. Armor, structure, cargo and wallet stay unchanged. Eden applies the boost immediately at cycle start; a manual stop finishes that paid cycle. A full shield still consumes capacitor. The session permits this warp-safe effect during warp, stops it on docking, transitions or hull loss, and advances at most one overdue cycle rather than simulating missed boosts. NPC damage and shield boosts serialize through the same SQLite world. The C# cooldown entity is now `ModuleCooldown`, retaining the existing `WorldWeaponCooldown` table and all saved rows; schema remains **24**.

`OnGodmaShipEffect` carries effect 4 and the paid cycle's start/duration/repeats. `OnModuleAttributeChanges` updates capacitor and shield charge, and `OnDamageStateChange` updates the health ring after healing. `OnSpecialFX` uses the native shield effect with the ship as source/target and the fitted module identity. Shared contacts publish the active effect and positive shield changes to observers. These use existing Blue/Destiny shapes; the previously recorded Carbon revisions remain applicable and no codec change is required.

The legacy Eden Pilot lacks Shield Operation, so the starter policy now offers its physical book for **5,000 ISK** and Small Shield Booster I for **1,000 ISK**, each with finite initial stock of 100. These are explicit Eden starter prices, not the catalog's base price or a live market feed. Existing station orders retain their stock and history; only missing offers are added. No pilot receives a skill, book, module or ISK on migration.

Pinned `SkillsSvc.InjectSkillIntoBrain` collects physical item IDs and calls `godma.GetDogmaLM().InjectSkillIntoBrain(skillIDList)`. It checks published types, then raises its own skill-injected notification after a successful response. Eden's selected-pilot skill handler owns that dogma call and returns None. Book consumption and adding `WorldPilotSkill` at **zero SP** are atomic. A stack loses exactly one book; the last book uses the existing junk-location `OnItemChange` removal. `OnServerSkillsChanged` sends `CharacterSkillEntry(typeID, 0, 0, rank, None)`, allowing the existing queue to train level I. Owned ordinary cargo on the current hull or the current docked station hangar is accessible; foreign, remote, specialized-hold, unpublished, non-skill, repeated-ID, repeated-type and already-known requests are rejected as a batch. Injection does not start training, spend free points or bypass the queue's prerequisite/Alpha restrictions. Injection without prerequisites follows [CCP's February 2016 skillbook change](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/patch-notes-for-february-2016-release). Direct `SkillsSvc.PurchaseSkills` remote purchases are separate work.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **265 tests**, and formatting verification passed. New checks cover mid-slot purchase/fitting/unfitting and resource limits, required skills, immediate/clamped healing, retained armor/hull and wallet, trained capacitor cost, insufficient-capacitor rollback, restart cooldowns, competing boosts and NPC damage, native activation/prime shapes, observer healing/effects, warp safety and fatal-loss cleanup. Physical-book checks cover market stock/payment, single-stack consumption, zero-SP injection before prerequisites, ordinary-cargo injection, inaccessible/invalid batches, duplicate and competing requests, native notifications, selection gating, restart and subsequent earned training.

The station Market button now owns the native `corpStationMgr.DoStandingCheckForStationService(7)` check, validating the current docked station and catalog service without seeding orders or moving ISK. A failed first method inside `MachoBindObject` now propagates its RPC error instead of embedding an internal error object in a successful binding tuple. ## Fitting defense-rate selector, November 2, 2026

Rechecked the prior **0 HP/s** observation against the same pinned client/build/hash. `DefensePanel.UpdateBestRepair` defaults its `activeBestRepair` preference to passive shield recharge. `UpdatePassiveShieldRecharge` explicitly converts the calculated rate to `int` before displaying it. The Ibis's peak passive recharge is below one HP/s, so zero is the native rounded display. Selecting **Shield boost rate** instead uses `GetTotalHpPerSec`, summing the fitted boosters' shield amount divided by their duration in seconds. No server behavior or client assets needed changing.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on the normal-damage recovered-Ibis save. Inventory showed the active Ibis with its **100 Tritanium and Civilian Miner / 6 of 125 m³**. Opened Fitting using the circled ship button, expanded Defense, opened the repair-rate selector, and selected **Shield boost rate**. The display changed from passive **0 HP/s** to **13.0 HP/s**, with one fitted booster. The panel also showed the expected trained **220 HP shield / 817 s recharge**, **68/162 CPU** and **17.6/27.6 power grid**. This resolves the earlier suspected active-defense readout issue. This milestone changes documentation only; the preceding **270-test** code validation remains the current automated result.

## Small hybrid turrets and ammunition, November 2, 2026

Checked the same pinned build **3662168**, `code.ccp` SHA-256 **37fb2af4fa0ec5c8fa320f34cd28c8b7dbf4917418336b38cba20a7860db0368**, and catalog definitions. Small Tech I hybrid turrets have group **74**, charge size **128 = 1**, and tech level **422 = 1**. 75mm Gatling Rail I **561** has **0.5 m³** magazine capacity, **5,000 ms** reload attribute **1795**, charge group **604 = 85**, and charge rate **56 = 1**. Iron Charge S **215** and Antimatter Charge S **222** each occupy **0.0025 m³**, making 200 rounds per full magazine. The default firing effect is **34 / projectileFired / effects.ProjectileFired**, even for this railgun. These shapes reuse the existing Blue and Destiny codecs and their previously recorded Carbon revisions.

The starter policy adds the 75mm gun at **1,000 ISK**, plus Iron and Antimatter at **1 ISK per round**, with finite stocks of 100 guns and 100,000 of each charge at visited market stations. Existing orders, stock and history stay intact. Other small Tech I hybrid types can use the family implementation when available as owned equipment; the starter market initially offers only the 75mm gun. Required skills, high-slot/turret limits and combined CPU/grid remain enforced.

Pinned `DogmaLocation.LoadChargesToModule` and `LoadAmmoToModules` call `dogmaIM.LoadAmmo(shipID, moduleIDList, chargeItemIDList, ammoLocationID)`. `UnloadAmmoFromModules` passes a module list; `UnloadAmmoToContainer` passes a single module, a `(locationID, ownerID, flagID)` destination and optional quantity. The HUD's native unload menu uses current-ship cargo. One ammunition handler owns both methods; source IDs must be distinct owned stacks of one compatible charge type in ordinary cargo or the current docked station hangar. Unload supports partial quantities for a single module. Invalid batches and capacity failures roll back the entire transfer.

Loaded rounds remain actual owned stacks whose durable `LocationId` is the fitted module's ID. They are absent from ordinary cargo until unloaded. The wire identity is the native **(shipID, flagID, chargeTypeID)** sublocation. Godma primes carry a null `invItem`, that tuple identity, charge attributes including **805 / quantity**, and no active effects. Mixed-key `shipInfo` uses the existing `BlueDictionary` type. `shipState.instanceFlagQuantityCache` contains a four-column native DBRow `(locationID, flagID, typeID, quantity)` per slot, as consumed by `BaseDogmaLocation.LoadSublocations` and `GetQuantityFromCache`. Quantity changes use the tuple as the item key; zero quantity removes the exhausted sublocation. The existing reduction opcode now carries the inventory descriptor's reduce state; no new opcode is introduced.

Loading, topping up or swapping consumes only real selected stacks, returns the old charge type to the source, fills available magazine capacity and commits a reload deadline. `OnGodmaPrimeItem`, `OnModuleAttributeChanges` and `OnChargeBeingLoadedToModule(moduleIDs, chargeTypeID, milliseconds)` drive the native charge/count/reload presentation. Attribute **2729 / nextActivationTime** permits the existing `StateManager.GetReloadTimes` calculation after reconnect. World version **25** adds a named `ModuleCooldownKind` to the retained `WorldWeaponCooldown` table; old deadlines become ordinary cycles without being reset. Reload and firing serialize through that same module deadline. Migration grants no equipment, ammunition, skills or ISK.

Each hybrid shot checks owned fitted online state, skills, same-system target/range, compatible loaded rounds and the saved deadline before spending capacitor or ammunition. It spends charge-rate rounds even on a miss. The magazine, capacitor, next cycle, NPC health, aggression, fatal wreck and bounty share one transaction. The existing hit-quality/resistance calculations and gunnery modifiers apply. Small Hybrid Turret adds damage; Ibis's catalog rookie hybrid bonus adds optimal range. Charge effect **596** multiplies range by attribute **120** (Iron **1.6**, Antimatter **0.5**); effect **804** applies capacitor percent **317** (Iron **-30%**, Antimatter **0%**). Ammo supplies the damage mixture: Iron **3 kinetic + 2 thermal**, Antimatter **7 kinetic + 5 thermal**. The civilian gun retains its built-in charge and effect 10.

Unfitting atomically returns remaining rounds into the module's destination, with combined cargo-capacity validation. Fatal hull loss includes the loaded stacks in its original manifest and rolls their normal independent survival chance; surviving rounds become loose wreck cargo even if the gun itself is destroyed. No nested rounds are orphaned or rerolled. Grouped weapon banks, other turret/launcher families, Tech II hybrid charge groups and heat remain separate work.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **286 tests**, and formatting verification passed. New cases cover market-funded equipment and ammo, physical quantity conservation, partial/unload/swap paths, reload persistence, catalog range/capacitor/damage modifiers, paid hits and misses, empty-magazine rejection, concurrent reloads and shot-versus-reload competition, inaccessible/invalid batches, capacity rollback, unfit returns, native sublocation/quantity-cache/reload notifications, loaded-round loss/drop accounting, and version-24 migration retaining existing deadlines without grants.

The native loading check exposed the inventory header's two physical extra columns: Godma derives nine-field charge rows and seven-field internal rows from this header, so physical `stacksize`/`singleton` produced `sequence is too short` and a null stack size. [Carbon Blue `PyRowSet.cpp` at af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/PyRowSet.cpp) confirms that descriptor reduction state carries virtual `(name, getter)` pairs. The header now has nine stored fields and the pinned `eve.common.script.sys.eveCfg.StackSize`/`Singleton` getters as its two virtual fields, preserving indices 9/10. The decoder retains this exact allowlisted state as inert data, rejects other getters, and never executes Python globals. Packed-row validation still counts only stored fields. Regression coverage checks the derived header lengths and state round-trip. Native reconnect restored five Antimatter rounds and displayed the fitted gun's charge icon and offense calculation. `clientDogmaLocation.OnModuleAttributeChanges` also requires an explicit positive quantity update after a new charge prime to fit its tuple sublocation; a prime alone updates Godma but leaves the live fitting calculation empty.

The catalog retained the normal **0.625 NPC damage multiplier** throughout; no equipment, ammunition, skills or ISK were added outside gameplay. Selected **Eden Pilot** and verified active recovery Ibis **2000000000023**, its recovered Civilian Miner and **100 Tritanium / 6 of 125 m³** cargo. Bought one **75mm Gatling Rail I**, **five Antimatter Charge S** and **20 Iron Charge S** through Regional Market for **1,025 ISK** total. Unfit the civilian gun into Item Hangar and used **Fit to Active Ship** on the purchased gun. The new gun **2000000000028** occupied online high slot **28**, retaining miner **24** at slot 27 and booster **27** at slot 19. Dragging charges onto the gun in the circled-ship Fitting window loaded the selected type; swapping to Antimatter returned the 20 Iron rounds to hangar. After the two notification/header corrections above, the live charge icon and offense calculation updated without reconnecting. Moved the Iron reserve into ordinary cargo.

**Live Windows client, firing and reload.** Undocked and warped to Perimeter II - Asteroid Belt 1. Locked the Rookie and fired Antimatter: the magazine declined **5 → 2**, the NPC exploded and became a wreck, the target lock disappeared and the weapon ring stopped. At Perimeter VIII - Asteroid Belt 1, fired the last **two Antimatter** rounds at a second Rookie. The magazine emptied, the gun stopped and the damaged NPC remained alive.
