## Trained weapon tooltip calculations, November 2, 2026

Investigated the preceding mismatch on the same pinned build and `code.ccp` hash. `ModuleButtonTooltip.LoadTooltip` chooses the current client Dogma location. `clientDogmaLocation.GetAccurateAttributeValue` explicitly uses its local modifier graph when docked, and Godma's server-primed values while in space. Eden sent an empty four-part pilot brain in `GetAllInfo.charInfo`, so the docked graph applied hull and charge effects but lacked earned weapon-skill modifiers. The observed Iron tooltip was **3.2 DPS / 12 km optimal / 136.50 tracking**, while the offense panel and in-space readout used **3.9 DPS / 13 km / 150.15**.

`GetAllInfo.charInfo[1]` now supplies `(version, characterEffects, shipEffects, structureEffects)`. Weapon skill effects occupy `shipEffects` and use the pinned `eve.common.script.dogma.effect.BrainEffect` state: resolved percentage value, source skill IDs, **LRS** location-required-skill modifier, affected attribute, **6 / post-percent** operation and `(requiredSkillID,)` application arguments. Shared gunnery bonuses target modules requiring Gunnery; Small Hybrid Turret's damage bonus targets its own requirement. Surgical Strike uses the catalog's **LG** location-group modifiers for the supported Energy Weapon **53** and Hybrid Weapon **74** families. The catalog confirms effects **414, 582, 290, 2847, 287, 298 and 173** use `shipID`/LocationRequiredSkillModifier, while **587/589** use LocationGroupModifier. Native owner modifiers exclude ordinary fitted modules and are not used for these effects. Zero-level and unrelated skills contribute no weapon effects. The authoritative weapon calculation and native brain use one named definition of each supported bonus. Firing, charge and hull rules remain authoritative in persistence, and this read does not grant skills or change saved SP.

The first live check rejected an old-style `INSTANCE` encoding of this new-style Python class. [Carbon Blue `Marshal.cpp` at af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp) distinguishes `ReadObjectInstance` from `ReadObjectNewobj`: the latter reads `((class,), state)`, calls the class's allocator and then restores its dictionary. Eden now sends BrainEffect with the existing **35 / NEWOBJ** opcode and two empty iterator markers. The existing paper-doll named-tuple form retains its validation. Incoming brain objects are retained as inert name/state data for this exact class only; no Python callable is executed by the server. Skill progress notifications include `OnServerBrainUpdated((version, marshaled(characterEffects, shipEffects, structureEffects)))`, allowing the native client to remove its old modifiers before applying the new earned levels. This requires no world migration.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **291 tests**, and formatting verification passed. Coverage adds initial pilot-brain integration in GetAllInfo, source skills and earned levels, required-skill versus group targets, unrelated/zero-level exclusions, versioned gray-matter refresh, training-completion notifications without duplicate completion, and NEWOBJ state round-trip with rejection of unknown classes, non-dictionary state and invalid iterator markers. Existing shot, fitting, ammunition and paper-doll tests continue to pass with the shared modifier definitions.

**Live Windows client.** Used the Computer Use skill on the same normal-damage `hybrid-ammo-20261102` save after the final rebuild/restart. Selected **Eden Pilot** and verified Inventory's active recovered Ibis, Civilian Miner and **100 Tritanium / 6 of 125 m³**. Opened the circled-ship Fitting button and hovered the loaded 75mm gun. Its tooltip now showed **18 Iron Charge S**, **3.9 DPS**, approximately **13 km optimal / 16 km optimal plus falloff**, and **150.15 tracking**; the offense panel also showed **3.9 DPS**. This visibly corrected the preceding 3.2 DPS / 12 km / 136.50 result. The fitted miner and booster remained present, with **13.0 HP/s** still selected under Defense.

Closed Fitting, undocked safely outside the same station, and hovered the gun's HUD button. It displayed the identical **18 rounds / 3.9 DPS / 13 km / 150.15** values. Selected the nearby Caldari Navy Assembly Plant, docked, reopened Fitting and hovered the gun again: the same values remained, without stacking an extra copy of the bonuses. No weapon was activated. Independent read-only comparison retained every item, wallet, skill, queue and cooldown row across the code restarts and this round trip; **20,944.01 ISK** and 18 loaded Iron remained. SQLite integrity and foreign-key checks passed. The final client log had no brain, new-style-object, unmarshal or modifier-application exception; unrelated optional services remain unsupported. The normal-damage lab is left running docked with Fitting open. Native acceptance covers initial learned modifiers and dock/undock persistence; notification-driven training refresh is automated coverage. Other skill domains and simulated unsupported weapon families remain separate work.

## Afterburner propulsion and Navigation skills

Build **3662168**'s `eve/common/script/dogma/pythonEffects/propulsionModules.pyj`, inspected without executing its code, adds the afterburner's mass before reading ship mass for the speed multiplier. It then applies `1 + speedFactor / 100 * speedBoostFactor / modifiedShipMass` to the unboosted maximum velocity. Stop removes both modifiers; refresh replaces the speed modifier rather than stacking it. The packaged `code.ccp` SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**.

The pinned **1MN Afterburner I / 439**, group **46**, has duration **73 = 10,000 ms**, capacitor need **6 = 20 GJ**, speed factor **20 = 115%**, thrust **567 = 1,500,000**, mass addition **796 = 500,000 kg**, maximum active group count **763 = 1**, CPU **50 = 15**, power **30 = 10**, and requires Afterburner I. Its default activation effect is **6731 / moduleBonusAfterburner**, graphic **effects.Afterburner**. Navigation **3449** supplies **315 = +5% speed per level**; Afterburner **3450** supplies **66 = -5% duration** and **317 = -10% capacitor cost** per level. Fuel Conservation **3451** separately multiplies capacitor cost through attribute 317. Acceleration Control **3452** supplies **318 = +5% speed factor** per level. Brain effects use the catalog's ship item, required-skill and group modifiers so native previews agree with paid server activations.

The Ibis's **295 m/s** base becomes **339.25 m/s** at Navigation III. Adding the module changes its mass **1,163,000 → 1,663,000 kg**, and its limit becomes **691.15 m/s**. Afterburner III produces **8,500 ms / 14 GJ** cycles. Changing earned Navigation updates the unboosted baseline once and reapplies an active module to that baseline. Module costs and bonuses are reread for each new paid cycle.

Destiny updates use **SetBallMass** and **SetMaxSpeed**, retaining actual velocity so acceleration and deceleration follow the changed inertia. Nearby pilots receive the same properties and propulsion effect; joining snapshots retain the active contact. This reuses Carbon Destiny **27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f**, whose thunks accept `(long ID, double mass)` and `(long ID, float speed)` and whose ballpark updates mass/time factor and maximum velocity. [Carbon thunks](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp) · [Carbon ballpark](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp)

Activation validates ownership, active hull, in-space state, medium slot, online state, requirements, group limit, health and capacitor. Paying saves capacitor and the UTC cycle deadline atomically in the existing records. World version stays **27**; no equipment, SP or money is granted. A late tick starts at most one new paid cycle. Consecutive paid cycles retain the same flight properties, avoiding a temporary base-speed command at each boundary. Manual deactivation finishes the paid cycle. A warp order prevents another cycle; docking, loss, gate transition and disconnect remove the effect. Reconnecting restores the ordinary Navigation limit and preserves an unexpired cooldown, without simulating offline cycles. The finite starter market adds 100 modules at **1,000 ISK** each as explicit Eden pricing. Microwarpdrives, overheating, implants and other navigation bonuses remain separate work.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with **zero warnings/errors**, all **324 tests**, and formatting verification passed. New coverage checks catalog/brain agreement, mass-dependent speed, trained cost and speed bonuses, atomic competing activations, persisted cooldown and charge, missing skills/ownership/online/rack/resource rejection, combined fitting limits, one active module, manual stop, velocity continuity, observer updates, non-stacking repeats without transient base-speed commands, insufficient capacitor, invalid targets, loss cleanup, warp/docking cleanup and Navigation training during an active cycle. Native observations follow separately.

**Live Windows client, November 2 local / November 3 UTC.** Used the Computer Use skill with a separate copy of the completed manufacturing save, retaining normal catalog data and existing skills. Selected Eden Pilot; Inventory showed the active Ibis, Civilian Miner and **100 Tritanium / 6 of 125 m³**. Searched Regional Market for `1m`, bought one **1MN Afterburner I for 1,000 ISK**, and chose **Fit to Active Ship** in Item Hangar. The module appeared beside the shield booster in the second medium slot. The circled-ship Fitting window showed **339.3 m/s** Navigation, and the module tooltip showed **339.25 without / 691.15 m/s with**.

Undocked, set a direction and full speed, then activated the HUD module. The cycle ring ran and speed rose above the ordinary limit. Clicking it again finished the paid cycle, cleared the ring and restored the **339 m/s** full-speed tooltip while velocity coasted down. This first run exposed a brief repeated-cycle slowdown; the corrected build retains flight properties across paid repeats. After rebuilding and restarting both host and client on the same save, the HUD returned with **339 m/s** normal limit, an inactive afterburner, **115 rounds** and the original cargo. Reactivated on the final build: speed rose through **687, 690 and 691 m/s** while repeated cycles continued, with no accumulating speed bonus.

Chose **Dock** on **Perimeter II - Moon 1 - Ducia Foundry Mineral Reserve** while the module ran. The client warped, the afterburner ring cleared, and the Ibis docked with its cargo intact. Undocked without reactivating and used Dock to return to the **Caldari Navy Assembly Plant**. Fitting again showed **339.3 m/s** and the retained module. The acceptance save is left running there, docked with Fitting and Inventory open.

Read-only SQLite comparisons retained every pre-existing item and skill row, the completed job and its exact recipe/deadline, and all **115 Iron rounds**. Exactly one purchased module, **2000000000043**, was added, fitted online in flag **20**. Wallet became **18,944.01 ISK**, exactly 1,000 less than before. The restart preserved capacitor and cooldown records; only movement advanced between the pre-stop and restarted snapshots. Final changes were the purchased item, finite market stock/trade/wallet, and paid capacitor/cooldown records. Schema **27**, integrity and foreign-key checks passed; server error output was empty. Peer visuals, loss cleanup, immediate unexpired-cooldown rejection, insufficient capacitor and other propulsion skill levels were tested automatically rather than repeated in the native client.

## Rearranging fitted modules

The existing ship-inventory **Add(itemID, sourceID, qty=1, flag=destinationSlot)** call now accepts an already fitted module on the docked active hull. Dropping it onto an empty compatible slot moves that same module; dropping onto another compatible fitted module exchanges their slots in one transaction. AutoFit or the current slot is an idempotent operation for an already fitted module. Ownership, active-hull/location, assembled quantity, required skills, rack limits and turret hardpoints remain authoritative. Cargo/hangar fitting retains its existing free-slot checks rather than replacing another module.

Both modules retain their item identities, quantities and online state. Loaded rounds remain real child stacks inside their module, and paid cycle/reload records remain keyed to the same module ID; rearranging does not grant ammunition, change a deadline or spend capacitor. Medium-slot swaps retain their existing CPU/power costs and cannot trigger a boost. World version stays **27**, with no schema or economic change.

Inspected build **3662168**'s `fittingScreen/fittingSlotController.ShipFittingSlotController.AddRealItem`, `fitting/fittingSlotController.FitModule` and `clientDogmaLocation.DogmaLocation.TryFit` without executing their code. They forward a fitted item's identity/current ship and requested flag through that existing Add call. `FittingSlotController.GetDragData` returns charge nodes instead of the module when a charge is loaded; dragging the other unloaded module onto a loaded gun exercises a slot swap without unloading its ammunition. The staged `code.ccp` SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. No Carbon codec or Destiny behavior changes are involved.

A native check exposed why inventory flag updates alone are insufficient: `DogmaLocation.OnItemChange` updates a continuously fitted item's `flagID` while retaining the ship's prior slot/effect association. The moved gun still showed its rounds, but Iron's range modifier applied to the miner in its former slot. Notifications therefore clear all old charge tuple quantities, detach every relocated module from the client rack using a temporary cargo flag, and publish the final module rows. Charge primes and quantities wait until fresh `ItemGetInfo` requests have read every relocated module; the connection sends the final module reply before those charge notifications, so charge passive effects can bind to the new rack. The temporary cargo rows are cache staging only; persistence commits the final fitting atomically without moving modules or rounds into real cargo or changing its capacity. Clearing the old tuples first also prevents one gun's removal from clearing another gun's new tuple during a loaded-gun swap.

**Automated checks.** Restore, build with zero warnings/errors, all **360 tests**, and formatting verification passed. New cases cover moving a loaded gun to an empty slot, exchanging two loaded guns, preserved identities/online costs/physical rounds/reload deadlines, atomic rejection of invalid moves, fresh-module-before-charge notification ordering, persistence, and medium-slot swaps without activation or repair.

**Live Windows client, final swap and flight.** Used the Computer Use skill on isolated `fitting-layout-verified-20261103`, copied from the completed module-manufacturing save with the ordinary catalog. Selected **Eden Pilot** and checked active Ibis **2000000000023** in Inventory, retaining **100 Tritanium**, the spare Civilian Miner and **6/125 m³** cargo. Automated short drags were interpreted as online toggles/current-slot drops; the maintainer performed the final occupied-slot drag in Fitting. The resulting normal Add call exchanged Civilian Miner **2000000000024** from high slot **27 to 28** and manufactured railgun **2000000000045** from **28 to 27**. Comparing all 23 world tables against the initial snapshot showed exactly those two flag changes: identities, online state, **113 Iron rounds**, wallet **16,686.41 ISK**, completed jobs, skills and all other rows were unchanged.

Without reconnecting, the gun tooltip showed **F1**, **113 Iron Charge S**, **13 km** optimal, **16 km** falloff, **3.9 DPS** and **150.15** tracking; the miner showed **F2**, its original **10 km** optimal and **6 m³ per 10 seconds**. This verifies the fresh-module-before-charge correction on the native client. Undocked, warped to Perimeter II Asteroid Belt 1, locked the Guristas Rookie and pressed **F1** with Auto-Repeat Off. The first gun's count decreased **113 to 112**, and the saved child stack remained inside the same manufactured module. Incoming NPC damage and movement continued normally. Further maintainer input fired another round and exercised the afterburner during this flight; those ordinary gameplay changes are accounted for separately from the earlier swap-only comparison.

**Live Windows client, restart.** The maintainer docked the Ibis at Perimeter II – Moon 1 – Caldari Navy Assembly Plant. Verified the docked station and active Ibis inventory, stopped both isolated processes, and restarted the host/client against the same save. All **23 world tables** matched exactly between the stopped and reconnected snapshots. Reopened Fitting using the station's circled-ship button: the railgun retained **F1**, **111 Iron rounds** after the two shots, **13 km** optimal, **16 km** falloff and **3.9 DPS**; the miner remained in the second high slot. Inventory still showed the original **6/125 m³** cargo. Both modules were online, the wallet and completed jobs were unchanged, no NPC aggression remained, world version **27** and SQLite integrity/foreign keys passed, and both final host error logs were empty. Empty-slot moves, loaded-gun-to-loaded-gun swaps and medium-slot swaps have automated coverage; this native run exercised the miner/loaded-gun exchange, new F1 weapon activation and full restart.

## Fitting expansion milestone 1: catalog fitting definitions

Fitting now reads the pinned type's rack, turret/launcher fitting markers and group/type limits rather than inferring every high-slot item's hardpoint from its position. Rack capacity and hardpoint capacity are independent: a hull with three high slots and one turret hardpoint can put its single turret in the third slot. Named low-slot flags and hull attributes are recognized throughout fitting, inventory transfer and dogma priming, preparing the passive-module milestones without enabling unsupported effects.

Existing active families require their catalog activation effect. At this milestone, propulsion group 46 microwarpdrives with effect 6730 moduleBonusMicrowarpdrive were rejected by fitting, afterburner activation and manufacturing eligibility; effect 6731 moduleBonusAfterburner remained supported. The later MWD milestone below supersedes fitting/activation rejection for unrestricted static drives. Existing unsupported stored items were not deleted. No world schema change or market adjustment was involved in this original milestone.

References: build 3662168 client/typedogma and client/dogmaeffects; inventorycommon.const low flags 11–18, medium 19–26, high 27–34; dogma attributes lowSlots 12, launcherSlotsLeft 101, maxGroupFitted 1544 and maxTypeFitted 2431. The packaged code.ccp SHA-256 is unchanged. Blue and Destiny retain the Carbon revisions already recorded above; this milestone changes neither codec nor simulation format.

Automated acceptance: restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all 430 .NET tests passed; both Python suites passed (3 and 9 tests); formatting verification passed. New regressions cover propulsion effect eligibility, independent slot/hardpoint limits, remaining-hardpoint totals and atomic rejection without consuming the remaining stack.

Live acceptance used the Computer Use skill on the separate fitting-passives-20261103 save copied from the current world. Eden Pilot entered Jita IV Moon 4 in the Ibis; Inventory showed Ibis (Ibis) and 0/125 m³ cargo. Opened native Fitting with the station service and used Item Hangar → Civilian Miner → Fit to Active Ship. The miner appeared online in the first high slot, and available resources changed from 162/162 CPU and 27.6/27.6 power grid to 97/162 and 25.6/27.6. Read-only inspection confirmed the original module identity in flag 27, online. Slot-position rejection and microwarpdrive eligibility were automated checks, not additional native actions.

## Fitting expansion milestone 2: hull passives and shared modifiers

Gameplay now evaluates a typed dogma attribute graph with item, group and required-skill selectors, pre-multiplication, addition, post-multiplication and percentages. The server normalizes the pinned catalog into those inputs. The first profile boundary enables 107 published meta-group-1 Tech I variants in 22 hull-passive families. Every effect must be recognized; mixed equipment-bonus profiles remain disabled until their final consumer milestone. Active variants sharing Damage Control group 60 are rejected rather than receiving partial passive behavior. Low slots use the same fitting, swap, unfit, online and saved inventory paths as other racks.

Fitted category-0 penalties and category-4 online bonuses have distinct lifetimes. Damage Control's pre-multiplied resonances remain outside stacking penalties. Penalized multipliers use strongest-first, separate positive/negative sequences and the pinned eight-entry exp((index / 2.67)^2) denominator table. Skills are exempt. Relevant compensation, fitting-cost, armor-layering and mining/weapon-upgrade skills feed module source attributes and the native brain. Existing hull, weapon and Navigation skill rules keep their single owners. Catalog attribute defaults, limits and integer/numeric forms are interpreted at the boundary; missing redundant attribute IDs are resolved from the source-record key.

Cargo transfers, reprocessing output, ordinary-hold mining, magazine unloading and wreck pickup read the evaluated hold capacity. Targeting uses fitted ship signature for player targets and catalog signature for other contacts. Flight snapshots and local fitting refreshes use evaluated mass, agility, speed and capacities; reconnect does not restore raw hull values. Existing weapon/mining/shield/propulsion calculations receive evaluated module inputs. Skill completion publishes the brain update before current ship attributes.

Fitting mutations checkpoint capacitor and shield recharge under the previous fit, preserve charged percentages across capacity changes, and retain armor/hull damage. They reject cargo overflow or a resulting zero-structure hull. Lowered CPU/power output and fitted penalties recalculate until legal, offlining the highest consumer of the most overloaded resource (item-ID tie break), in the same world transaction. SetOnline returns all resulting item changes. An explicit online request that cannot afford its final fit still fails atomically. Paid cycles, magazines, identity and economics are unaffected. World version remains 28; no schema or market/production expansion is introduced.

References: build 3662168 dogma/attributes/{attribute,chargedAttribute,stackingNurfedAttribute}, baseDogmaLocation fitting/resource checks, clientDogmaLocation online/fitting paths, and the imported client/typedogma, client/dogmaeffects and client/dogmaattributes. The code.ccp hash and Carbon Blue af0d65b / Destiny 27ee40b references remain unchanged. Tests embed a minimal pinned catalog fixture, not a world save or resource cache.

Automated acceptance: restore/build passed with zero warnings/errors, all 437 .NET tests passed, both Python suites passed (3 and 9 tests), and formatting verification passed. New coverage checks the complete hull profile count, active/mixed exclusions, stacked resistances, modifier ordering, cycles, compensation-to-brain mapping, fitted penalties, overfilled rollback, automatic CPU shutdown, charged percentages, preserved armor/hull damage and restart. The signature regression verifies a shield-extender Ibis at 54 m against its 52 m hull basis. Existing magazine, mining, targeting, propulsion, flight, combat, economic and migration tests remain green.

Live acceptance used Computer Use on fitting-passives-20261103. Eight two-item passive stacks were supplied only to that copied save; pilot skills, wallet and ordinary catalog were retained. Selected Eden Pilot and verified the active Ibis in Inventory at Jita IV Moon 4. Fit Expanded Cargohold I through the native item menu: Fitting showed 146.9 m³ cargo, 288.4 m/s and 161 structure HP. Put Offline returned cargo to 125 m³ while retaining those penalties; Unfit restored 339.3 m/s and 201 structure HP. Fit Co-Processor I: output became 175.0 CPU (174.96 before UI rounding). Small Shield Extender I raised shields from 220 to 660 HP. Added 200mm Steel Plates I: armor showed 632 HP, mass 1,303.00 t, agility 4.5000 and align time 8.13 s. The final fitting retained the original Civilian Miner, the plate in flag 11, processor in flag 12 and extender in flag 19, all online, with 75.0/175.0 CPU and 12.8/27.6 power available.

Undocked that fitting, double-clicked a heading and observed acceleration through 74.5 and 307 m/s to about 339 m/s. Chose Dock on the Navy Assembly Plant and observed the automatic approach and return to its hangar. Stopped both processes, saved a consistent snapshot and launched the final compiled host/client against the same save. After login, all 24 world tables matched the stopped snapshot exactly. Reopened Fitting: all four modules, 660 shield HP, 632 armor HP, 201 structure HP, 1,303.00 t mass, 339.3 m/s and the same resource totals returned. SQLite integrity and foreign-key checks passed and both host error files were empty. Cargo overflow, cascade ordering, compensation and fitted target-signature effects were automated coverage; this native run did not include a belt fight or a two-pilot lock.

## Fitting expansion milestone 3: passive equipment bonuses

The complete boundary now enables **130 published Tech I/meta-group-1 variants across 31 families**. This extends the 107 hull profiles with weapon damage/cycle upgrades, tracking enhancers, mining upgrades, shield-boost amplifiers and mixed capacitor-relay profiles. Character-domain ballistic-control and required-skill missile-guidance modifiers use the same graph; missile launcher activation and other new weapon families remain unavailable. Missing effect records, unknown modifier functions/operators and activated special variants still reject the whole profile. Market, manufacturing eligibility and world schema remain unchanged.

Weapon, mining and shield operations consume the evaluated module attributes, then apply their existing skill rules once. Mining-upgrade CPU penalties use the fitted phase while yield requires online state. Shield boost amplifiers and capacitor relay penalties affect actual restored shields. Public fitting reads use a consistent world transaction. Equipment changes now send evaluated attribute updates for existing fitted modules as well as the ship, using the same module projection as initial dogma priming. This fixes stale gun readouts after fitting or offlining upgrades. The native information panel retains its displayed rows while open; close and reopen Attributes when comparing a changed fit.

Native testing also exposed an empty-ammunition call in the passive unfit path. UnloadAmmo now accepts owned fitted supported equipment in every rack when it has no loaded charge, without producing any item or magazine change. Real charge loading remains restricted to supported high-slot hybrid weapons. Ownership, destination, distinct-item and paid-deadline validation remain in place.

References: the same pinned build 3662168 typedogma/effect/attribute records and stackingNurfedAttribute implementation recorded in milestone 2. Stacking eligibility follows each target attribute's stackable flag, so not every module family is penalized. Equal penalized modifiers contribute at 100%, 86.91199808%, 57.05831435% and subsequent table weights. Skills remain exempt. Milestone 18 corrects the former blanket pre-multiplier exemption: native pre-multipliers have their own stacking sequence, which matters for multiple ballistic controls. A lone Damage Control remains separate from post-percent resistance modules. Carbon Blue/Destiny references and the code.ccp hash are unchanged.

Live acceptance used the Computer Use skill on the same copied fitting-passives-20261103 world. Supplied two Heat Sink I and two Tracking Enhancer I only to that save. Eden Pilot remained docked in the Ibis at Jita IV Moon 4; Inventory showed its active ship. The civilian gun is catalog group 53 / Energy Weapon despite its name, so its damage-upgrade comparison used Heat Sink I.

After restarting into the corrected host with both enhancers online, the gun's Attributes showed **6,019 m optimal, 2,707 m falloff and 624.303397609 tracking**. Offlined one enhancer and reopened Attributes without restarting: **5,662 m optimal, 2,404 m falloff and 588.5 tracking** appeared. The trained basis is 5,280 m / 2,100 m / 550; exact server multipliers for two enhancers are 1.1400795105071109 / 1.289295644812386 / 1.1350970865619499. Two unpenalized optimal modifiers would instead show 6,073 m. This directly verifies the diminished second modifier and updated native values.

Unfit the offline enhancer through its native menu: the slot cleared and the same module returned to Item Hangar without the previous ammunition error. Fit Heat Sink I into that slot. Offense changed from **3.7 DPS to 4.3 DPS**; Put Offline restored **3.7 DPS**. The remaining tracking enhancer stayed fitted. SQLite integrity and foreign-key checks passed at world version 28. Read-only comparisons with the pre-equipment snapshot confirmed wallet, skills, training queue, weapon cooldowns, industry jobs and testing grants unchanged; the host error file remained empty. The final client is on the copied acceptance save. Actual mining and shield-boost bonus cycles were automated checks, not additional native belt actions in this milestone.


The maintainer correctly distinguished Heat Sinks from hybrid upgrades. Confirmed the civilian classification against client/types, not only Eden's normalized ItemType: Civilian Gatling Railgun / 3638 has group 53, while 75mm Gatling Rail I / 561 has hybrid group 74. Supplied one standard railgun and one Magnetic Field Stabilizer I to the copied save, preserving skills. Replaced the civilian gun through native Unfit/Fit controls. With Heat Sink I online, the hybrid Attributes panel showed **1.732× damage and a 2.2 s cycle**; removing the heat sink left both unchanged. Fitting Magnetic Field Stabilizer I instead changed them to **1.854× and 2.0 s**, with the existing tracking enhancer retained. The gun was unloaded, so this comparison used damage/cycle attributes rather than DPS. Added explicit cross-family regression coverage and kept the pinned catalog classification intact.


Automated final acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; **447 .NET tests**, **3 catalog setup Python tests**, **9 formatting policy Python tests**, and formatting verification passed. New regressions cover all 130 variants/31 families, hybrid upgrade damage/cycle stacking with unpenalized skills, Heat Sink exclusion from hybrid weapons, group/required-skill isolation, exact diminished enhancer values in native notifications and offline removal, fitted mining CPU penalties and online yield, actual shield restoration with mixed amplifier/relay modifiers, character-domain ballistic multipliers, empty passive ammunition unload, and whole-profile rejection for unknown/missing effects. Existing combat, ammunition, mining, targeting, flight, training, economic and migration tests remain green.

## Repeating module HUD timer repair

The maintainer reported periodic resets of the circle around active HUD modules. The four module controllers sent a stopped OnGodmaShipEffect followed immediately by a new start at every automatic repeat. In build 3662168 shipmodulebutton.pyj, ModuleButton.Update routes that stop through SetIdle and IdleRamps. IdleRamps clears ramp_active and deletes the module from shipui.sr.rampTimers. The next start therefore creates a new DoActivateRampsThread with first-activation timing correction, even though the module should remain continuously active. This is distinct from the normal full-circle wrap at a completed cycle.

Automatic repeats now retain the existing dogma activation. Each repeat still calls its authoritative operation to validate, spend capacitor/ammunition and apply damage, yield, repair or propulsion. Only the initial activation and actual shutdown send HUD start/stop notifications. Per-cycle Destiny graphics and contact snapshots retain their existing updates. Failed payment, target loss, exhaustion, manual-stop policy and transition cancellation still stop the effect. Start timestamps for monotonic scheduling are captured before persistence work so that database time is included in the paid cycle, matching its wall-clock deadline.

References: directly inspected the pinned modulebutton Update, SetActive, IdleRamps, GetRampStartTime and DoActivateRampsThread, together with Godma StateManager.OnGodmaShipEffect/ActualStopEffect. The pinned client hash and the previously recorded Carbon Blue/Destiny references remain unchanged; there is no wire-format, simulation-packet or schema change.


Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **451 .NET tests**, **3 catalog setup Python tests**, **9 formatting policy Python tests** and formatting verification passed. Four new regressions exercise two automatic repeats with deliberately late scheduler advances, assert no intermediate HUD stop/start notifications, and verify that a real manual stop emits the final stop. Existing payment, ore, damage, reload, target-loss, shutdown and persistence coverage remains green.

Native acceptance (2026-11-03): used Computer Use with the same copied fitting-passives-20261103 save. Supplied one 1MN Afterburner I without changing skills or wallet. The maintainer fitted it, undocked the active Ibis, activated it and flew a heading. Inventory showed Ibis with 0/125 m³ cargo. Observed the green active module and progressing HUD circle while the ship accelerated through 365, 640, 657 and 668 m/s. Four paid-cycle attribute updates occurred with only one HUD activation message after Activate; the saved cooldown advanced with those payments. A real shutdown produced the second HUD message. The maintainer explicitly confirmed, "its fixed". Host stderr remained empty. Other module families received automated repeat/stop coverage; this live run used the afterburner.

## Fitting expansion milestone 4: local Tech I armor repairers

Ordinary Tech I armor repairers in group 62 now fit in low slots and activate through `dogmaIM.Activate` / `Deactivate` with effect `armorRepair` (27). The pinned basic Small Armor Repairer I (523) uses attribute 84 for its 69 HP repair, attribute 6 for 40 capacitor and attribute 73 for a 6,000 ms base duration. Repair Systems (3393) applies its -5% per level duration modifier once, through the shared evaluated module graph and native brain. Twelve ordinary published Tech I variants are represented in the catalog fixture; Tech II, remote and ancillary repairers and overload activation remain outside this milestone.

Capacitor is paid at activation; armor is restored at the paid cycle's end, capped at maximum armor without healing shield or structure. A pending repair captures its paid amount, original start and completion deadline in the same transaction as capacitor and cooldown. World version 29 adds `WorldArmorRepair`, retaining prior inventory, skills, health and cooldowns and inventing no pending cycles. Health/resource reads settle due repairs transactionally once, before combat or fitting uses the result. A destroyed hull never receives armor or revives. Manual stop completes the current paid repair. Docking and disconnects retain that one completion without buying offline cycles. Reconnecting into space before the deadline restores its original HUD interval with zero repeats and no second payment. Live repeats retain the Godma active state fixed in the preceding timer milestone.

References: unchanged build 3662168 `client/typedogma`, `client/dogmaeffects` (effect 27, `effects.ArmorRepair`, warp-safe) and `client/dogmaattributes`; Repair Systems effects 216/272 and source durationBonus 312. The code.ccp hash remains unchanged. Existing native notification and Destiny adapters reuse the consulted [Carbon Blue af0d65b](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp) and [Carbon Destiny 27ee40b](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Thunkers.cpp); neither codec nor simulation stream format changes. No market or manufacturing offers are added.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **466 .NET tests** passed; both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy tests); formatting verification passed. Armor regressions cover the ordinary Tech I cohort and Tech II exclusion, skill-to-brain duration, payment before delayed repair, exact deadline, clamp, two simultaneous repairs, destroyed hulls, invalid starts without payment, reconnect without double payment or offline repeats, docking/resource-read settlement, stable repeat HUD state, paid manual stop, and version-28 migration retaining items and cooldowns. Restart/reconnect behavior was covered automatically rather than repeated as a live acceptance action. Warp safety follows the pinned effect flag and existing self-module flight gate; it was not separately exercised in this client check.

Live acceptance used the Computer Use skill on the separate `fitting-passives-20261103` save, backed up as `before-armor-check.sqlite` before version-29 migration. The normal save and read-only catalog were retained. This explicit fixture supplied one Small Armor Repairer I (2000000000025), set the pilot's existing Repair Systems skill to level I (250 SP), and set the active Ibis to 100 armor damage with no structure damage. This was artificial test damage, not combat-earned damage. The agent observed the repairer fitted and online, the damaged HUD armor ring, and subsequent active cycle ring. ## Fitting expansion milestone 5: active Tech I hardeners

Fitting now enables the 15 published ordinary Tech I shield-hardener variants (group 77) and 12 armor-hardener variants (group 328). Catalog eligibility checks publication, meta group, tech level, complete known effects and supported modifiers. Shield modules use medium slots and effect 5230 `modifyActiveShieldResonancePostPercent`; armor modules use low slots and effect 5231 `modifyActiveArmorResonancePostPercent`. The pinned EM Shield Hardener I (2293) costs 20 capacitor per 10,000 ms, while EM Armor Hardener I (11301) costs 30 per 20,000 ms. Both use -50% EM resonance bonus attribute 984. Multispectrum Shield Hardener I (578) supplies -25% to each shield resonance and costs 40 per 10 seconds. Required skills and evaluated CPU/power costs use normal fitting paths. Tech II, reactive, flex, scripted and overload activation remain excluded.

Each activation transaction validates owner, active hull, position, rack, online status, catalog effect, skills, health and cooldown before spending capacitor and recording its activation marker. The graph reads live markers only for online modules still fitted to that hull. Active and passive post-percent modifiers share the same strongest-first stacking sequence, while Damage Control retains its exempt pre-multiplication. Combat and health readers use the resulting real defenses. Hardeners do not repair HP, compound a bonus on repeats or gain passive-amplifier compensation bonuses. Repeats retain the Godma HUD active state; manual stop retains resistance through the paid cycle. An unpaid repeat removes it. The native graphics are `effects.ModifyShieldResonance` and `effects.ArmorHardening`, replayed to nearby pilots through existing timed-effect adapters.

World version 30 adds `WorldActiveHardener` connection-owned markers without adding equipment, SP, offers or money. Docking, gate/hull transitions and connection-scope disposal remove markers; explicit world initialization clears orphaned markers after validation/migration so a host restart cannot leave permanent resistance. Saved capacitor and cooldowns remain authoritative across reconnects and prevent a free early reactivation. These live markers represent the server's current module-cycle state, advanced on the existing session tick; they do not buy offline cycles. The clock captures activation before persistence, as in the HUD timer repair. Blue and Destiny formats retain the already consulted Carbon Blue af0d65b / Destiny 27ee40b implementations; activation names, effects, warp-safe flags and numeric attributes come from unchanged build 3662168 catalog records.

Automated acceptance: restore/build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **485 .NET tests**, both Python suites (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. New regressions cover the full 27-module cohort, Tech II rejection, real shield and armor damage mitigation, separate capacitor/deadline values, duplicate activation, stacked hardeners, passive/active stacking and Damage Control exemption, compensation exclusions, repeat HUD continuity, paid manual stop, failed payment, disconnect disposal, restart cleanup, warp-safe activation, hull-loss cancellation, invalid starts without payment, native active-effect priming, peer graphics and version-29 migration retaining equipment/cooldowns.

Live acceptance used Computer Use on `fitting-passives-20261103`, backed up before migration as `before-hardener-check.sqlite`. The normal save and read-only catalog stayed separate. This explicit test fixture supplied two EM Shield Hardener I modules (2000000000026/27) and one EM Armor Hardener I (2000000000028), docked the test Ibis, and raised only its existing Hull Upgrades from 2,829 SP to the required level III / 16,000 SP. Tactical Shield Manipulation already had 5,657 SP and remained unchanged. The intermediate 8,000-SP fixture setup was corrected before fitting the armor hardener; `before-hardener-skill-correction.sqlite` retains that intermediate snapshot.

Selected Eden Pilot and verified Ibis (Ibis) in Inventory, with 0/125 m³ cargo. Through native fitting controls, returned the afterburner, extender and tracking enhancer to Item Hangar, took the miner offline for CPU room, and fitted the two shield hardeners in flags 19/20 and armor hardener in flag 12. The repairer and unloaded railgun remained fitted. While online but inactive, Defense showed the original 8% shield and 50% armor EM resistance. Undocked at Jita IV Moon 4. The maintainer activated both shield hardeners; the agent observed 74% shield EM resistance across repeated payments, with the two activation markers and falling/recharging capacitor confirmed read-only. The agent activated the armor hardener on the HUD and observed armor EM rise to 75%. No HP was repaired. Stopping one shield hardener kept its bonus through the paid interval, then the panel showed 54% with one remaining; stopping the second returned shield EM to 8%. Stopping the armor hardener retained 75% until its paid cycle ended, then returned armor EM to 50%; the saved activation table was empty. The 74%/54%/8% progression matches shared stacking penalties rather than an accumulating repeat bonus. Native tooltips showed -50% EM damage resistance bonus for each module. Restart cleanup, insufficient payment, actual incoming damage and peer graphics were automated checks rather than additional live scenarios. The host error log remained empty.

## Fitting expansion milestone 6: Tech I capacitor boosters

Eight ordinary published Tech I capacitor-booster variants in group 76 now fit in medium slots and activate through effect 48 `powerBooster`. Eligibility checks the pinned publication/meta-group/tech-level profile and normal fitting effect. Loaded cap charges use group 87 and capacitorBonus attribute 67; the Small Capacitor Booster I (3566) has a 12 m³ magazine, chargeRate 56 = 1, a 15,000 ms cycle and a 10,000 ms reload. Cap Booster 100 (3554) occupies 4 m³ and injects 100 GJ. Compatibility is determined by charge group and available magazine volume, not the weapon-only charge-size restriction. All 18 published cap-charge variants are represented in the fixture, including Navy and capital charges; oversized charges still cannot fit a small magazine. Tech II booster fitting and overload activation remain excluded. Manufacturing eligibility and market offers are unchanged.

Magazine reading, priming, reload/unload and native charge quantities now support fitted medium slots as well as weapons. An activation validates the owned online fitted booster, required skills, compatible loaded charge, quantity, hull health and cooldown before atomically consuming the real charge, adding capacitor up to capacity and saving the new cycle deadline. Activating with insufficient missing capacitor still consumes the charge; excess injection is wasted. No public resource-grant RPC is added. Loaded charges participate in existing unfit and loss accounting. Repeats keep the native Godma HUD active, consume another charge only at each successful next cycle and stop on an empty magazine. Manual shutdown finishes the current interval. Docking, loss and disconnect remove the live controller; already injected capacitor, remaining charges and reload/cycle deadlines persist without offline injections. World version stays 30.

References: unchanged build 3662168 `client/typedogma`, `client/dogmaeffects` 48/3002 and `client/dogmaattributes` 56/67/73/604/1795. Effect 48 is warp-safe and has no spatial effect guid, so it uses HUD notifications without inventing a Destiny particle effect. Existing Blue row/sublocation adapters retain the consulted Carbon Blue af0d65b implementation; transport and Destiny stream formats are unchanged. The shared fitting fixture now passes its TestClock through the host's optional clock registration so handler calls and reload deadlines use the same clock. Production still defaults to TimeProvider.System.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **503 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. New tests cover all eight fitting profiles and Tech II exclusion, volume-based charge limits including larger/Navy charges, medium-slot reload deadline priming, mixed-load atomic rollback, oversized rejection, immediate injection and clipping, real charge consumption, duplicate activation, empty-magazine shutdown, stable repeat HUD state, paid manual stop, restart without offline injections, saved reloads, returned charges on unfit, warp-safe activation, hull-loss cancellation, failed starts without mutations, and native active-effect/magazine priming. Existing ammunition, fitting, repair, hardener and persistence regressions remain green.

Maintainer acceptance used the separate `fitting-passives-20261103` save, backed up as `before-capacitor-booster-check.sqlite`, with one supplied Small Capacitor Booster I (2000000000029), three Cap Booster 100 charges in the station hangar and nine in Ibis cargo. No additional skill or wallet grants were made for this check. The agent's Computer Use attempt was stopped with Escape; the maintainer asked that game windows be left alone, then manually tested the module and explicitly reported “the booster works.” The agent did not repeat input or independently observe the capacitor-gain animation after that instruction. Read-only checks corroborated the online booster fitted in the active Ibis's medium slot 19, native LoadAmmo/Activate/Deactivate calls with reload and quantity notifications, and five consumed charges: six remained in ordinary cargo and one in the reloaded magazine. The host error log remained empty. Exact injection amounts, reload duration, empty-magazine handling, restart behavior and other failure cases have automated coverage; the native result is maintainer-confirmed rather than an agent-observed screenshot sequence.

## Fitting expansion milestone 7: unrestricted microwarpdrives across tiers

Group 46 now admits all **62 unrestricted static MWD profiles** in build 3662168: Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction, deadspace and officer. All use the complete effect set 13/16/58/3175/6730. Eligibility validates publication, static tech attributes, supported effects and the absence of any canFitShipType/canFitShipGroup restriction. The seven ship-restricted capital profiles are deferred under the maintainer's ordering instruction. Four Abyssal types lack a static tech profile and require per-item mutation persistence before use; they remain rejected. Manufacturing and market offers are unchanged.

Pinned `eve/common/script/dogma/pythonEffects/propulsionModules.pyj` defines Microwarpdrive as Afterburner.MODIFIER_CHANGES plus a ship signatureRadius post-percent modifier from signatureRadiusBonus. The same mass-first thrust calculation therefore owns flight speed/mass. Effect 58 applies the module's capacitorCapacityMultiplier (147) to ship capacitorCapacity (482) while online, including when inactive. The fitting graph applies this penalty and preserves the charged percentage through online/offline changes. Only active MWD markers add signatureRadiusBonus (554) to signatureRadius (552); this includes signature changes from other fitted equipment. Skills are selected by actual required skill or group: Afterburner/Fuel Conservation do not affect MWDs, High Speed Maneuvering affects MWD capacitor need once, and Acceleration Control applies to both propulsion families.

The 5MN Microwarpdrive I has a 25% online capacity penalty, 500% speed factor, 500% active signature bonus, 1,500,000 thrust, 500,000 kg added mass and a base 45 GJ / 10-second cycle. High Speed Maneuvering III reduces cost to 38.25 GJ. Higher tiers retain their distinct catalog values: the 5MN II uses 80% capacity, a 510% speed factor and 475% signature bonus; Domination uses 90% / 512% / 450%; Coreli A-Type 97% / 518% / 410%; Asine's Modified 99% / 520% / 390%. No universal tier multiplier is invented.

Activation pays capacitor and saves its cooldown and live marker in one transaction. Afterburners and MWDs share the single active propulsion limit. Repeats retain one flight/signature bonus and the native HUD effect; manual stop finishes the paid interval. Warp blocks new activation/repeats. Docking, hull loss, connection disposal and explicit host initialization clear the live bonus. World version **31** renames `WorldActiveHardener` to shared `WorldActiveModule`, retaining foreign-key cleanup while preserving paid resources and deadlines. Hardener behavior remains unchanged. Stopping a module never deletes its cooldown, so reconnecting cannot buy an early free cycle. No offline propulsion is simulated.

Native activation uses `moduleBonusMicrowarpdrive` and `effects.MicroWarpDrive`; active-effect priming and peer replay preserve the MWD identity. Blue/Destiny wire formats retain the consulted Carbon Blue revision af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997 and Destiny revision 27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f. Effect, attribute, skill and graphics details come from the unchanged build 3662168 catalog and inert inspection of the staged code archive (code.ccp SHA-256 37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368).

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **522 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. Coverage includes full cohort eligibility; deferred capital/Abyssal exclusion; online capacitor penalty and charge-fraction preservation; inactive/active signature; skill separation and single application; four higher-tier payment/attribute profiles; simultaneous propulsion rejection; continuous repeats; paid manual stop; failed payment; scope disposal; warp gating; invalid starts without resource changes; version-30 migration/restart retaining cooldowns; native priming and observer MWD graphics. Historical save fixtures now remove the newer live table before replaying old migrations and capture modern pilot snapshots before downgrading their schema.

Native MWD acceptance remains **pending**. The maintainer instructed that game windows be left alone; no MWD was staged into or activated in a running client, and neither the lab host/save nor the separate launcher/client was restarted for this change. The Windows client guide contains the pending fitting, online capacity, active speed/signature, HUD, shutdown and restart checks. Automated results do not establish visible native acceptance.

The continuing scope now includes every tier of existing module families before additional families, with ship-type/group-restricted modules deferred until unrestricted modules are implemented. This supersedes the original Tech-I-only expansion scope; it does not claim that the remaining tier backfill or module families are complete.

Mining, Astrogeology, critical skills, supported Venture bonuses and fitted mining upgrades retain their existing ownership. New tiers do not invent manufacturing recipes or grants. World version remains 31.

Mining waste probability (3154) is a percentage: Miner II is 34%, Gallente is 20%, and the other ordinary profiles are 0%. Their waste-volume multiplier (3153) is catalog-driven. Typed cycle inputs supply independent probability and rounding rolls to pure gameplay rules. Cargo bounds the planned base yield; a residue success removes its bounded amount from the deposit before extraction, and a completed-cycle critical success adds bonus cargo without consuming the deposit or increasing residue. Partial manual stops prorate yield and residue but cannot crit. Base yield, bonus and residue use stochastic rounding independently, with floating-point noise suppressed at integer boundaries. Cargo additions and both deposit reductions commit together, including residue-only depletion with no cargo row.

Source references: unchanged build 3662168 catalog, plus inert code-object inspection of the staged `notifications/client/oreMinedNotificationAdapter.pyj` and `mining/client/asteroid_scanning_service.pyj` in code.ccp (SHA-256 `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`). `quantity_removed` means extracted base ore; the native scanner separately adds `amountWasted` when subtracting from its estimate. Including residue in both would double its visible depletion. `quantity_added` includes critical ore, while `amountCritBonus` stays separate. Existing effects.Laser and consulted Carbon Blue af0d65b / Destiny 27ee40b transport/FX formats are unchanged.

[CCP's Catalyst notes](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/catalyst-expansion-notes) specify stochastic rounding and critical bonus ore independent of asteroid contents. The residue-first exhaustion rule follows [CCP Swift's design-team clarification](https://forums.eveonline.com/t/from-extraction-to-production/336331/1015). That clarification predates this client; exact residue ordering at exhaustion and cargo clipping still need native comparison. Current probabilities come from the pinned catalog, not the earlier pre-release balance table.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **642 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. The focused mining run passed all **59 tests**. The expanded active fixture contains 658 published module profiles and 13 skill definitions, 47 effects and 84 attribute metadata records; existing active-family definitions and metadata are unchanged. Older minimal mining fixtures now include the targeted effect contract while preserving their artificial values. The 36 new cases audit all 18 profiles, every admitted tier's payment/range/cycle/yield, residue thresholds, stochastic expectations, independent bonus/residue rounding, cargo bounds, zero-yield depletion, persistence/replay rejection, invalid inputs/resources, unrestricted hull activation, destroyed ships and unsupported stored profiles.

Higher-tier native acceptance remains **pending** under the explicit instruction to leave game windows alone. No game input, running-save mutation, deployment or host/client restart occurred. The broader fitting/module goal remains open, including both deep-core lasers, hybrid tier backfills, remaining unrestricted families, instance mutations and subsequent ship-restricted work.

## Fitting expansion milestone 14: hybrid turret and charge tiers

The published group-74 cohort contains **223 hybrid turrets**. All **216 unrestricted profiles** now pass the complete fitting/firing boundary: small, medium, large and capital weapons across Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer tiers, including all three polarized blasters. The seven profiles with explicit positive ship restrictions remain deferred under the maintainer's ordering. This cohort has no published deadspace or Abyssal variants; defensive mutation/unknown-effect checks also reject synthetic or changed stored profiles. Capital weapons without ship restrictions still need their real fitting resources and skills. No hull assembly, manufacturing, grants or market policy is expanded; world version remains 31.

Each turret must have 12/16/34/42 and exactly one known overload effect (3001 or 3025); polarized variants additionally have the complete paired 5994/5995 overrides. Effect 34 is targeted, not warp-safe and uses duration 51, capacitor 6, range 54, falloff 158 and tracking 160. Overload remains inactive. Fitting, loading and firing all revalidate the supported profile, including an item already saved in a fitted slot. Destroyed hull checks, transactional ammunition/capacitor/cooldown/NPC updates, the existing hit-quality model and continuous HUD effect ownership apply to every admitted size.

All **224 published hybrid charges** are supported: 208 ordinary/faction group-85 profiles, eight advanced railgun group-373 charges and eight advanced blaster group-377 charges. Loading and firing require matching size and either chargeGroup1 (604) or chargeGroup2 (605). This follows the actual catalog rather than assuming advanced ammunition requires a Tech II gun: several faction, officer and storyline guns also advertise a secondary advanced group. Ammunition skill requirements are checked on load and again on fire, independently of the gun's requirements. A wrong group/size or unsupported charge effect cannot pay, move a stack, consume a round or damage a target.

Charge effects 596/599/600/804 multiply optimal range, falloff, tracking and capacitor respectively, using source attributes 120/517/244/317 and their pinned defaults. Effect 1173 uses hidden source 779 to modify sentry/NPC orbit range (416), whose default is 500 m; it does not replace player turret optimal range. Void S retains its 0.75 optimal / 0.5 falloff / 0.75 tracking / +25% capacitor changes. Spike S retains 1.8 optimal and 0.25 tracking; Javelin S uses 0.25 optimal and 1.25 tracking. Missing identity multipliers do not zero other attributes. Real magazine volume, partial unloads, paid reload/cycle deadlines and per-shot charge-rate consumption remain shared across all sizes.

Small/Medium/Large/Capital Hybrid Turret each grant 5% damage per level only to modules requiring that skill. Matching railgun/blaster specializations grant 2% per level only to guns requiring that specialization; loading advanced ammo into an eligible faction gun does not invent a specialization bonus on the gun. All eight specialization types are represented, including capital. Generic gunnery selectors remain required-skill/group based, and the native brain uses the same table. The existing rookie hybrid optimal bonus is confined to Small Hybrid Turret selectors. Other hull-specific weapon traits remain separate work. Heat sinks continue to affect energy weapons only.

The polarized profiles require post-assignment operation **7**. Re-inspection of build 3662168's inert `dogma/const.pyj` confirms its position after ordinary multipliers and percent modifiers. `dogma/attributes/attribute.pyj`, `Attribute.Update` lines 362–376, clamps both the base and final modified value. The graph previously performed only the first clamp; it now implements final clamping and post-assignment. Polarized shield/armor source 1978 assigns 100 and hull source 1979 assigns 1; the catalog resonance ceilings then clamp all twelve to 1, meaning zero resistance. Damage Control, active hardeners and corvette resistance roles cannot survive that final override. Fitted phase-0 penalties remain while offline. Corvette role resistance bonuses now live in the shared graph before the override instead of being applied again afterward. Unfitting removes the override without healing saved damage.

Representative pinned values: 125mm Railgun II is 2.64 damage multiplier / 3.25 seconds / 1.505 GJ and requires Small Hybrid V plus Small Railgun Specialization I. Shadow Serpentis 200mm is 3.16 / 4.455 seconds / 3.612 GJ; Asine's Light Neutron is 5.145 / 3.5 seconds / 1.4161 GJ. 425mm Railgun II is 3.63 / 8.129 seconds / 21 GJ. Dual 1000mm Railgun II is 8.16756534 / 20 seconds / 300 GJ; Ion Siege Blaster II is 6.125625 / 10 seconds / 100 GJ. At Gunnery V/Rapid Firing V, cycles use 0.9 * 0.8; Controlled Bursts V applies 0.75 before the charge's cost modifier. The existing industry product boundary explicitly retains small Tech I hybrids while fitting expands.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **674 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. The focused hybrid/graph run passed **34 tests**. The 32 new cases cover the tier expansion and attribute corrections; existing ammunition, combat, fitting, industry, mining and other feature tests passed in the final full suite. The dedicated fixture includes 223 turret profiles, 224 charge profiles, 19 skills, 43 effects and 82 attribute definitions including transitive bounds. Legacy fixtures retain artificial attributes while loading complete effect metadata. Exhaustive eligibility, twelve representative paid shots across all sizes/tiers, magazine capacities/reload persistence, group/size rollback, module/ammo skill gates, native brain selectors, polarized offline/online restoration and stored-profile rejection have automated coverage. Larger-weapon shot fixtures explicitly use artificial fitting/capacitor budgets and establish no real Ibis fitting claim.

References: pinned build 3662168 type/effect/attribute records and inert dogma code-object inspection in code.ccp, SHA-256 `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. Existing consulted Carbon Blue af0d65b / Destiny 27ee40b formats and projectile FX are unchanged. The user-directed game-window hold remains in force: no client input, host/client restart, deployment or running-save change occurred. Tooltip/defense, advanced charge effects, actual resource expenditure and native HUD acceptance remain **pending**.

Remaining unrestricted work includes deep-core hazards, new weapon/module families, outstanding mixed/drone effects, and per-item mutations before ship-restricted modules. Deep-core investigation confirmed the pinned Mining Pollution Cloud (11546): radius 5 km, 30-second effect delay, 300-second lifetime and 50 damage of each type, plus Deep Core Mining's current -10% per-level chance modifier. That data is not an implemented hazard lifecycle; both deep-core laser profiles remain unavailable until the shared cloud, damage, lifetime and native visibility behavior is implemented and verified.

## Fitting expansion milestone 15: projectile turrets and ammunition

The pinned group-55 cohort contains **167 published projectile turrets**. All **160 unrestricted profiles** now fit and fire through the shared ammunition-fed turret path: small, medium, large and capital autocannons/artillery across Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer variants, including all three polarized autocannons. Seven explicit ship-restricted profiles remain deferred. No published deadspace or Abyssal projectile profile occurs in this cohort. Synthetic mutations, unknown effects and changed default firing modes are rejected even through already stored fittings. No market policy, manufacturing product scope or world schema changes; world version remains 31.

Hybrid-specific helpers were generalized into typed turret rules and catalog adapters. Both families use default `projectileFired` effect 34 with the existing wire/FX path, real magazines, reload and paid-cycle cooldowns. Projectile weapons retain **zero capacitor need**, including at empty capacitor, and still consume charge-rate ammunition on misses. Magazine capacity comes from catalog volume: 125mm Gatling I with small charges holds 200; Dual 180mm I with medium charges 200; 1200mm Artillery II with large charges 40; Hexa 2500mm II with XL charges 80. All use ten-second reloads. Hybrid reloads retain their own five-second values.

All **144 published projectile charge profiles** are supported: 128 ordinary/faction group-83 types and eight each in advanced autocannon group 372 and artillery group 376. Catalog chargeGroup1/2 and exact size determine compatibility, including eligible faction/storyline/officer guns with advanced groups. The shared adapter validates the complete expected fitted effect set for each charge group, including optional capacitor modifiers on applicable hybrid charges. Missing or extra effects cannot be loaded or fired. Ordinary projectile charges use 596/600/1173; advanced projectile charges also use falloff effect 599. Four damage components, range, tracking and falloff retain each charge's real values. Hail S uses 0.5 optimal, 0.75 tracking and 0.75 falloff; Quake S uses 0.25 optimal and 1.25 tracking.

Small/Medium/Large/Capital Projectile Turret each grant 5% damage per level only to modules requiring that size skill. Eight autocannon/artillery specialization skills grant 2% per level only to guns requiring the corresponding specialization. Surgical Strike selects projectile group 55 as well as the existing supported turret groups; generic gunnery cycle/range/tracking/falloff selectors retain their own ownership. Loading advanced ammunition requires its specialization independently of the gun and firing rechecks it. Reaper's existing catalog role source `rookieSPTDamageBonus` (1836) supplies 10% damage only to Small Projectile Turret selectors; Ibis's small-hybrid optimal bonus cannot leak into projectile guns. This adds that weapon-role calculation without claiming complete Reaper hull behavior.

Gyrostabilizers now affect an operational projectile weapon path. Their damage/cycle modifiers retain strongest-first stacking penalties: the second and third factors are approximately 0.8691199808003975 and 0.5705831435105602. Heat sinks and magnetic field stabilizers remain confined to their respective other weapon groups. Polarized autocannons reuse the final resistance overrides and catalog ceilings implemented for hybrid polarized weapons, including offline fitted penalties and restoration after unfitting. They do not heal saved damage.

The full projectile effect profile also contains legacy fitted derivations 253/254 and non-default targeted effect 263 (`barrage`). The catalog boundary checks those records and requires effect 34 as the unique default. Derived attributes 326/328 are calculated from the final skilled/fitted damage multiplier and falloff, with mutators 324/325; these stay distinct from advanced Barrage ammunition. A separate legacy barrage activation is not routed. Overload and that alternate activation remain explicit future work. The pure graph now supports pre-assignment -1 with an explicit native operator order; enumeration's unsigned sorting would place -1 last. This ordering is covered by a calculation whose correct result depends on pre-assignment preceding addition and percentages.

Client module priming now dispatches projectile group 55 to the authoritative combat attribute calculator, alongside hybrid group 74. It includes loaded charge modifiers, earned skill effects, legacy derived attributes and the paid reload deadline rather than returning only base fitting attributes. A scoped protocol regression checks the precise 125mm Gatling II/Hail readout: 3.6073125 damage multiplier, 2,268 ms cycle, 528 m optimal, 359.6625 tracking and 3,386.25 m falloff at the fixture's specified skills. The native brain advertises the same required-skill selectors. Existing HUD repeat ownership and paid shutdown are retained.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **717 .NET tests** passed, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed. The 43 new cases include the projectile family, native module priming and pre-assignment ordering; existing hybrid, ammunition, fitting, industry, mining, combat and other paths passed in the final full suite. The projectile fixture has 167 turret profiles, 144 charges, 31 combat skills, 57 effects and 86 attribute metadata definitions. The hybrid fixture adds the 12 shared projectile skill definitions/effects while retaining its existing gun/charge definitions; it now has 478 total definitions, 55 effects and 82 attribute definitions. Coverage audits every profile/charge, fourteen representative paid shots across sizes/tiers, empty-capacitor misses, magazine volume/reload/restart conservation, group/size/skill rejection, specialization brain selectors, all three polarized profiles, gyrostabilizer diminishing returns/family isolation, role/legacy derivations, native module priming and unsupported stored profiles. Larger-gun fixtures use explicitly artificial CPU/grid/capacitor budgets and establish no real corvette fitting claim.

References: unchanged build 3662168 catalog type/effect/attribute records, particularly 34/253/254/263/5994/5995 and the projectile skill effects. The staged `eve/client/script/environment/effects/turrets.pyj` was inspected as inert code objects. The code.ccp SHA-256 remains `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. Consulted Carbon Blue af0d65b / Destiny 27ee40b transport/simulation formats are unchanged. No game input, host/client restart, deployment or running-save change occurred.
