## Fitting expansion milestone 16: energy turrets and persistent frequency crystals

Build 3662168 contains **216 published group-53 energy weapons**. The **204 functional unrestricted profiles** now fit and fire: small, medium, large and capital beam/pulse weapons across Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer variants, including three polarized pulse lasers. Eight explicit ship-restricted entries remain deferred. Lux Kontos 34580, Lux Xiphos 47446, Lux Ballistra 88348 and Lux Kopis 88349 have no normal fitting/firing dogma definitions and remain unavailable; publication alone does not establish a usable module. No published deadspace/Abyssal energy profile occurs in this cohort. Changed mutation, ship-restriction and additional firing-effect profiles are rejected at the authoritative fitting/firing boundary. No market policy, inventory grants or manufacturing product rules were changed.

Ordinary energy weapons use default **10 / targetAttack / effects.Laser**, with cycle 51, range 54, tracking 160, falloff 158 and capacitor 6. This shares the native laser activation/FX with the starter Civilian Gatling Railgun, whose built-in charge exception remains intact. The typed activation/effect names now describe laser firing rather than assuming effect 10 only belongs to civilian equipment. Energy weapons require a real loaded crystal. Their catalog reload value **1795 = 0.01 ms** is retained as a saved 100-FILETIME-tick deadline; it is not replaced with a hybrid/projectile reload. Only one crystal is loaded per weapon, with catalog volume and capacity still enforced. Dual Afocal Light Laser I 6633 also retains a non-default legacy barrage effect 263, validated as metadata while only its default laser firing is routed; alternate barrage activation remains pending. Paid cycle/reload cooldowns and stable repeated HUD effects use the existing owners.

All **200 published frequency crystals** are validated against their complete fitted-effect profiles: 184 ordinary/faction group 86 (596/804/1173), eight advanced beam group 374 (596/599/600/1173), and eight advanced pulse group 375 (those four plus 804). Exact charge size and the weapon's chargeGroup1/2 determine compatibility; eligible faction/officer weapons can use advanced crystals when their catalog allows it. Charge specialization requirements are checked during loading and again during firing. Scorch S uses its actual 1.4 optimal and 0.75 tracking multipliers, retaining hidden 1.5 entity fly-range separately. Gleam S uses 0.25 optimal and 1.25 tracking. Swaps notify range, tracking, falloff, capacitor and reload attributes.

Small/Medium/Large/Capital Energy Turret grant 5% damage per trained level only to their required-skill selectors. Eight beam/pulse specializations grant 2% per level only to weapons requiring the specific skill; loading advanced crystals does not grant a specialization damage bonus to an otherwise ineligible faction gun. Generic Gunnery, Rapid Firing, Controlled Bursts, Sharpshooter, Motion Prediction, Trajectory Analysis and group-selected Surgical Strike apply once. The native pilot brain uses the same typed skill modifiers. Impairor role attributes 1822 (-20% capacitor) and 1823 (+10% damage), from catalog effects 4990/4991, apply only to Small Energy Turret selectors. This implements those weapon role inputs without claiming complete Impairor hull behavior. Heat sinks modify operational energy guns with the existing strongest-first stacking penalties; magnetic field stabilizers and gyrostabilizers do not. Polarized lasers retain the fitted resistance overrides, including while offline.

Crystal condition is separate from ammunition quantity. Catalog **783 crystalVolatilityChance** is a probability, **784 crystalVolatilityDamage** is HP per wear event and **786 crystalsGetDamaged** enables wear; HP is attribute 9. Ordinary Tech I crystals have flag 0 and never wear. Faction crystals have flag 1, chance 1 and damage 0.00025, lasting 4,000 paid shots from pristine HP 1. Tech II crystals have chance 0.1 and damage 0.01; a draw below 0.1 causes wear. Independent server randomness supplies wear draws; pure rules accept explicit draws for deterministic boundary tests. Decimal accumulation prevents floating-point drift from granting a nominal extra shot. Misses still use and can break crystals. Failed activation rolls back resources, condition, damage and deadlines. A breaking crystal pays and resolves its final shot, is removed atomically, and prevents another shot until reloaded.

**World version 32** adds zero-default CrystalDamage to owned items without granting inventory or resetting resources. Loaded wearable crystals are individual singleton items. Unloading, same-type crystal swaps, cargo/hangar moves, unfit and restart retain the individual ID and wear instead of merging with fresh stacks. Existing wreck movement retains the owned row. Native magazine primes and damage notifications carry attribute 3 on the existing charge sublocation; quantity becomes zero when a crystal breaks. Saved worn items expose their condition in the typed inventory model and owned loose crystals answer ItemGetInfo in current cargo or the docked station hangar. This is crystal condition only; module overheating and module repair remain future work.

Pinned client evidence: inert marshal/code-object inspection of `shipModuleButton/moduleButtonTooltip.pyj` shows `GetCrystalDamage` calls `GetAccurateAttributeValue(chargeInfoItem.itemID, const.attributeDamage)`, and `GetChargeText` displays crystal damage as a percentage. `shipmodulebutton.pyj` hides the ordinary quantity label for crystal groups. No game Python was imported or executed. Archive SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. The [Carbon Blue source](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/tree/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997) was consulted again; existing Blue/DBRow and Destiny FX formats remain unchanged (Destiny revision 27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f). Wear values come from the exact pinned catalog, rather than current balance documentation.

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **759 .NET tests** passed in 11 minutes 8 seconds, both Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed across 322 files. The 42 new energy cases passed alongside the existing fitting, ammunition, combat, mining, propulsion, repair, inventory, industry and migration regressions. The isolated energy fixture contains 216 weapons, 200 crystals, 43 combat skills, 68 effects and 86 attribute metadata definitions. The hybrid and projectile tier fixtures also now contain all 43 shared combat skills (490 and 354 total type definitions respectively); comparison against their prior JSON preserved every existing weapon, charge and metadata record. This keeps their restart checks valid when training the expanded combat-skill enum. Tests exercise every functional profile, negative controls, representative shots across sizes/tiers, real crystal modifiers/capacitor payments, wear boundaries and 4,000-shot faction lifetime, last-shot destruction, independent miss wear, saved identity, failed transactions, native primes, skills, stacking and schema migration. Large/capital shot fixtures deliberately increase an Ibis's resource budgets; they do not establish a valid real corvette fit.

Native acceptance remains **pending** because the maintainer requested leaving game windows alone. No client window was controlled, running host restarted, normal save changed or deployment made. Laser FX, crystal tooltip wear, swap timing, break feedback and repeated HUD rings still need a copied-save client check when testing resumes.

## Fitting expansion milestone 17: local hull repairers across tiers

All **25 published group-63 Hull Repair Unit profiles** in build 3662168 are unrestricted and now supported: 18 Tech I/named, four Tech II and three storyline profiles across small, medium, large and capital sizes. This cohort contains no published faction, deadspace, officer or Abyssal profiles. Synthetic ship restrictions and mutations are rejected. Hull repairers use medium-power effect 13, online effect 16, default **26 / structureRepair / effects.StructureRepair**, and overload-duration effect 3002. The catalog adapter validates the complete fitting/default/effect profile and rejects malformed amount/duration/cost inputs. No market policy, inventory grants or manufacturing product rules changed.

Hull repair amount is **attribute 83**, separate from armor amount 84. Small Hull Repairer I 524 repairs 25 HP for 30 GJ over 30 seconds; Small Hull Repairer II 2355 repairs 30 HP for 30 GJ over 24 seconds. Small Inefficient 5693 uses 30 HP / 24 seconds at lower CPU; Small Integrative 21504 uses 45 HP / 20 seconds. Medium/large profiles retain their own fitting, amount, cost and duration. Capital Hull Repairer I 41511 uses 1,800 HP / 720 GJ / 60 seconds, Capital Hull Repairer II 41514 uses 2,160 HP / 864 GJ / 60 seconds, and capital Enduring 41512 and Compact 41513 use 1,980 HP with their distinct costs. Tests deliberately increase an Ibis's budgets for large/capital rules; they do not establish a real corvette fit.

Repair Systems 3393 effect 272 applies -5% duration per level through required-skill selector 3393. Capital Repair Systems 21803 effect 1635 applies -5% per level to selector 21803. Capital **hull** repairers require both, so both apply once: V/IV gives 60,000 × 0.75 × 0.8 = **36,000 ms**. Ordinary hull repairers receive only Repair Systems. Existing capital **armor** repairers retain their separate capital-only selector. Native skill brain effects and module primes match the authoritative calculation. Decimal conversion to FILETIME ticks prevents a binary artifact from adding an extra 100 ns tick while preserving legitimate fractional milliseconds.

Armor-specific schedule/controller/contact types were generalized into typed local-repair models carrying **RepairLayer.Armor/Hull**. Gameplay owns validated cycles, deadline conversion and layer-specific clamped health results. Persistence checks ownership, active ship, online singleton, correct rack/group, complete fitting profile, skills, health, resources and paid cooldown before committing the schedule and capacitor payment together. The scoped controller remains the single activation/repeat owner. Duplicate activation cannot buy a second cycle; the other layer's activation/deactivation effect is rejected. Armor call defaults and native armor wire names remain unchanged.

Repairs resolve at the end of the paid cycle. Multiple repairers add independent completions rather than sharing a passive stacking penalty. Excess repair is discarded without a capacitor refund. Settlement writes only the repaired layer, including when another layer's saved damage exceeds its current maximum, and leaves destroyed hull damage untouched. Manual stop finishes the paid cycle; docking/disconnect stop repeats, and restart restores at most that one payment without offline repeats. Warp remains safe for this catalog effect. Moving the module to another ship retains its deadline: a due old-ship repair settles on the original hull before a new cycle can start, preventing duplicate pending module keys or transferring repair to the new ship.

**World version 33** adds a zero-default Layer column to the historical WorldArmorRepair table. Existing rows become armor schedules while retaining module/ship IDs, amount, start/completion times and resources. The table name and indexes remain. A paid armor-cycle migration and repeated initialization verify preservation and idempotence; older migration fixtures reconstruct the pre-33 table before replay.

Pinned native evidence: `client/effects` registers **effects.StructureRepair** as **ImpactEffect**, with animationName **hullrepair**, merge flags [1,2], transform flags [0] and a 10,000 ms base visual duration. Armor's separate registration uses armorrepair. The existing timed FX carrier supplies actual module duration and saved start time for pilot and observer replay. Hull HUD effect 26 and damage attribute 3 remain distinct from armor. The client archive SHA-256 remains **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. Consulted [Carbon Destiny](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny) and [Blue](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue) again; existing carriers require no packet-format changes (previously recorded Destiny 27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f and Blue af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997).

Automated acceptance: tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **796 .NET tests** passed in 12 minutes 9 seconds, the Python suites passed (3 catalog and 9 formatting-policy checks), and formatting verification passed across 326 files. The 37 new hull-repair cases passed alongside all existing regressions. Focused armor/hull checks also passed (52 cases), covering every hull profile, representative sizes/tiers, capital skill ownership, exact deadlines, clipping, failure rollback, restart, shutdown, independent layers, native HUD/FX selection, module transfer and version-32 migration. The isolated fixture contains 25 modules, three skills, ten effects and 27 attribute metadata definitions.

Native acceptance remains **pending** under the maintainer's request to leave game windows alone. No game server restart, deployment or normal-save mutation occurred. A copied-save client check still needs to verify fitting, start payment/end repair, repeat rings, manual stop, hull animation/observer replay and restored paid cycles. Record native observations separately from automated results.

## Fitting expansion milestone 18: missile fitting, ammunition and owner modifiers

Build 3662168's ordinary launcher cohort contains 145 published profiles. The 139 unrestricted profiles now have validated fitting and loading rules across groups 506/507/508/509/510/511/524/771/1245/1674, including civilian, Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction, officer and polarized variants where present. The Defender launcher and five rapid-torpedo profiles have explicit ship restrictions and remain deferred. Bomb, probe, festival and other special launcher mechanics are separate families. This milestone does not implement missile activation or flight; existing turret firing rejects missile launchers without spending their rounds or damaging an NPC.

All 201 published compatible charges have validated missile effect/warhead profiles, including ordinary, faction, advanced and auto-targeting ammunition. Launchers select from all six native charge-group attributes 604–609, rather than turret charge size. Torpedo Launcher I uses selector 606; Republic Fleet Cruise accepts advanced cruise ammunition through selector 609. Volume determines magazine size: Light I 40 rounds, Rocket I 37, Rapid Light I 19, Torpedo I 30 and XL Cruise I 15. Normal reloads take ten seconds and rapid launchers 35 seconds; the real stacks and reload deadlines survive restart and partial/full unloading. Loading checks the ammunition's own skill requirements independently of the launcher. Civilian Light Missile Launcher consumes a high slot without inventing an effect-40 launcher hardpoint.

The fitting graph now includes loaded charge nodes. Character-domain OwnerRequiredSkill modifiers select implemented missile skills, while ordinary ship LocationRequiredSkill modifiers reach loaded ammunition for guidance enhancers. Six mixed drone upgrades remain unavailable because their drone selectors are still unsupported. Legacy skill effects 660/661/662/668 and specialization effect 1851 are normalized at the catalog boundary. Missile Launcher Operation and Rapid Launch affect launcher cycle; matching specializations affect only launchers requiring them. Base missile skills and Warhead Upgrades modify ammunition damage; auto-targeting ammunition also requires and receives its own skill. Bombardment, Projection, Guided Missile Precision and Target Navigation Prediction modify flight time, speed, explosion radius and explosion velocity. Skills remain exempt from equipment stacking penalties. Ibis role effect 5000/source 1827 adds kinetic damage to missile ammunition without changing other components or claiming full Ibis hull behavior.

Ballistic controls retain the character's missileDamageMultiplier (212) separately from the charge's four damage attributes. Inspection of pinned dogma.const and stackingNurfedAttribute corrects an earlier graph assumption: pre-multiply operator 0 belongs to dgmNurfableOperations. Multiple ballistic-control damage bonuses therefore diminish strongest-first, in their own operator bucket, just as their post-multiplied rate bonuses do. A single Damage Control pre-multiplier stays separate from post-percent resistance bonuses. Heat sinks, hybrid upgrades and gyrostabilizers cannot modify missiles. Guidance equipment shares each applicable charge attribute's stacking sequence, independently of exempt skills. Polarized launchers reuse the shared fitted resistance overrides, including while offline.

Native charge primes now use effective loaded missile attributes and include quantity; the pilot brain emits owner-required-skill effects as `ORS` in CharacterEffects, targeting the pilot. Launcher priming includes its saved reload deadline. Pure application rules cap damage by target signature and speed. Catalog 1353 already stores the transformed reduction exponent (light 0.604, rocket 0.644, torpedo 0.944); it is not transformed through log(drf)/log(5.5) again. These rules are preparation for delayed impacts, not an instant-hit missile implementation.

References: pinned catalog types/typedogma/effects/attributes and inert code-object inspection of dogma/effect.pyj, dogma/const.pyj, dogma/attributes/stackingNurfedAttribute.pyj and the native missile space object. The code.ccp SHA-256 remains `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. Flight investigation consulted Carbon Destiny revision `114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072`, Ballpark::MissileFollowBall and the public missile evolution tests. The client reads sourceShipID/launchModules from the missile slim and its target from the following ball; actual missile balls, guidance, expiry, delayed NPC damage, observer replay and auto-target acquisition remain to be implemented. No wire simulation or world schema is added here; world version remains 33.

The older ballistic-control regression now expects the native diminished second bonus. All 796 existing cases remain covered by the final run. Launch/impact acceptance must wait for the separate flight implementation.

## Fitting expansion milestone 19: missile motion, swept contact and native balls

The shared missile flight component now implements launch, guidance, exponential inertia, contact and lifetime independently of hosting/persistence. It is the motion component for the upcoming launcher firing path; no module activation, ammunition payment or NPC damage has been connected to it yet. The existing turret operation continues to reject launchers. This milestone adds no world schema or running-server change; world version remains 33.

Reference: [Carbon Destiny revision 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/tree/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072), particularly Thunkers.cpp's PyLaunchMissile and WriteBallToStream, Ballpark.cpp's EvolveMissile/GotoThrust/Integrate/StopAllFollowers, IDstConstants.h and python/destiny/test/ballpark/evolve/test_missile.py. Build 3662168's inert missile-space-object inspection and code.ccp hash remain as recorded in milestone 18. There are no new live client observations under the game-window hold.

Unaimed launches inherit platform velocity plus max(150 m/s, platform maximum speed) along its movement direction, or the supplied rear orientation when stationary. Aimed launches start at a unit 1 m/s velocity toward the target, matching Carbon's public tests. MissileFollow's initial absolute guidance point is velocity direction times 1e16. Native evolution uses one-second ticks; the 800 ms straight-launch condition occupies the first tick. Later ticks aim at the target's current position plus its velocity times distance/missile maximum speed. The negative follow range makes this a center interception. Mass times agility / 1,000,000 supplies inertia, with the same analytic exponential-drag solution as Carbon. Stable small-ratio forms avoid cancellation for large inertia. Guided missile thrust remains full near the target; ordinary initial/orphaned Goto retains its fourth-power taper. After losing a target, a missile keeps flying along its velocity, without reacquiring or dealing contact damage, until its original lifetime expires.

Contact follows the analytic missile curve and the target's supplied linear motion within each tick. An adaptive sweep bounds exponential curvature before solving relative segment entry into the sum of the two radii; it searches earlier intervals first. The curve tolerance is 0.1 mm, subject to floating-point coordinate precision. This catches fast and crossing targets between endpoints, avoids false hits where a chord cuts inside a curved trajectory, and permits a faster target to escape. Fractional final ticks stop at the exact lifetime budget. Impact and expiry are terminal states. This is Eden's explicit contact policy for nonmassive missiles; it does not claim to implement Carbon's full general-purpose collision response. The core accepts the target's availability from the owning world operation, which still needs integration.

The missile charge catalog now builds a typed drive from catalog mass/radius and effective speed/agility/lifetime, validating finite positive motor inputs before loading. All 201 published charge profiles pass the stronger admission check. A malformed motor with a valid warhead cannot move rounds or create a reload deadline.

Native serialization supports navigation mode 5 / MISSILE. After the normal free-ball header/kinematics and formation byte, it writes target ID (int64), negative combined follow range (float32), launching ship ID (int64), launch stamp (int32), and absolute guidance point (three float64 values). Projectiles use the free flag and have no health entry. The slim uses charge category 8, pilot ownerID, sourceShipID and a list-valued launchModules field. Snapshot and incremental additions share the same writer. A lost-target missile uses Goto mode with only its guidance point, retaining no stale follower payload. Completed or nonfinite states cannot be streamed as live balls. Grouped launcher operation remains pending despite the native field's list shape.

Automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors; all **886 .NET tests** passed in 15 minutes 27 seconds. All 35 focused motion/native/catalog checks passed. The 34 new cases cover Carbon's public launch and twenty-tick guidance expectations, inherited platform speed/orientation, velocity-led guidance, moving/crossing targets, tunneling, false chord hits and small curved-path contacts, escape and fractional lifetime boundaries, lost-target coasting, stable large inertia, invalid geometry/motors, exact native byte fields/slim types, orphan payloads, terminal-state exclusion, and snapshot/addition agreement. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy tests), and formatting verification passed across 337 files. All 201 missile charge profiles retain admission under motor validation.

Remaining integration includes atomic launch/payment/cooldown/projectile storage, shared NPC impact damage and single bounty/wreck ownership, auto-target aggressor acquisition, HUD cycle ownership, world ticking, observer visibility/removal, and transition/restart behavior. No operational missile-fire acceptance is claimed from the component tests. Native validation remains pending under the maintainer's instruction to leave game windows alone.

## Fitting expansion milestone 20: paid missile firing and shared NPC impacts

The launcher firing path now connects the 139 unrestricted ordinary launcher profiles and 201 validated charges from milestone 18 to milestone 19's flight rules. This includes the supported static tiers and sizes; restricted launchers and special bomb/probe/festival mechanics remain deferred. Existing module and ammunition skills, magazine volumes, guidance modifiers, Ibis kinetic role and diminished ballistic-control bonuses feed actual shots. Launcher activation requires a real owned online high-slot module, sufficient compatible rounds, completed reload/cycle deadlines, a live source hull and an available NPC. Ordinary ammunition requires an authoritative target lock and targeting/visibility range. Missiles consume no capacitor. Rejected operations preserve ammunition and prior deadlines.

One SQLite transaction pays the round, saves the next launcher deadline, snapshots the effective motor/warhead and inserts the projectile. Later skill or fitting changes do not rewrite an in-flight shot. Catalog attribute 644 (`aimedLaunch`) selects Carbon's launch mode. Flight ages start at shared FILETIME one-second boundaries, while paid cycle deadlines retain their actual activation time. Connection ticks advance shared world projectiles under a transaction, independently of which observer triggers the tick. Each saved boundary runs once. Target positions use saved NPC motion and linear velocity within the missile tick; contact uses the swept analytic path. Impact damage then applies the target's current signature, speed, defenses and remaining health. Losing a lock stops future ordinary shots but does not erase an already launched missile; losing the target makes the missile coast without reacquiring.

Automatic ammunition effect 104 acquires only a live same-system NPC whose saved aggression targets the launching hull. Attribute 2700 supplies its acquisition range, capped by Eden's visibility range. The nearest eligible aggressor wins, with ID as a deterministic tie break. An idle nearer NPC is not eligible. The native call may omit a target lock/ID for this ammunition. This is the current NPC encounter policy; player combat and arbitrary non-NPC targets are not introduced.

World version **34** adds `WorldMissile` with source, target, launch times, typed flight/warhead snapshot and an optional typed impact outcome. Source-generated JSON serialization uses explicit model metadata, excluding computed vector properties. Projectile IDs start at 6,000,000,000,000 and use SQLite AUTOINCREMENT so cleanup cannot reuse them. Migration retains fitting, charges and existing paid deadlines, without granting items or skills. A projectile keeps its launching module ID even if inventory later removes the module; it deliberately has no inventory foreign key. Completed reported/empty outcomes can be removed after 120 seconds while a system has active missile work; unreported impacts remain available to their owner.

NPC damage, recharge, retaliation, kill rewards and wreck creation now share one persistence helper with turret combat. A launch starts retaliation rather than waiting for contact. Terminal steps are settled by contact time then projectile ID inside the shared transaction. Simultaneous arrivals cannot destroy the same NPC twice, pay two bounties or create duplicate wrecks. Impact outcomes are claimed once by the firing pilot and queued before flight updates; the damage log identifies the ammunition as the weapon. Observers receive health, wreck/NPC changes and projectile state without claiming another pilot's combat log or reward.

The scoped missile controller owns the paid HUD cycle. Duplicate activation does not buy another shot, repeated payments do not send idle/restart effect pairs, and manual stop finishes its paid interval. Every repeat rechecks the saved magazine, fitting/skills and target. Native module primes can read the active effect from the same controller. Flight snapshots and incremental additions include projectiles with the correct source/module/target identities; subsequent updates reconcile position/velocity, switch a lost-target ball to Goto and remove impact balls with the terminal flag. No turret firing effect is substituted for missile flight.

Disconnect, docking, warp initiation, accepted gate jump, hull loss and restart cancel pending projectiles without refunding rounds or cooldowns. The shared store also checks that the source is still the active in-space hull in the same system, so a stale observer cannot settle shots after a saved docking/loss transition. A lifetime that passed without observation does not replay retrospective damage. Boot expires remaining flying rows; completed impacts/rewards cannot be applied again. These are explicit Eden transition/offline policies, not a claim of the full cluster simulation behavior.

References: [Carbon Destiny revision 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/tree/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072), including Thunkers.cpp, Ballpark.cpp, Ball.cpp and the public missile tests. Inert inspection of pinned build 3662168's ModuleButton.ActivateEffect/GetRelevantEffect and godma EffectWrapper/StateManager confirms the launcher Activate call and charge-selected targeting requirement. The missile space object's Prepare reads sourceShipID, launchModules and followId; its native model schedules its warheads from time-to-target. Michelle's RemoveBall terminal flag selects destruction handling. The previously recorded code.ccp hash is unchanged. Automated ball/removal checks do not establish visible launch, trail, collision callback or explosion behavior; those need direct native observation.

Automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. All **916 .NET tests** passed in **17 minutes 13 seconds**, covering the 886 existing cases and 30 new operational missile cases. All 30 focused missile cases also passed independently, including representative ordinary/named/Tech II/faction/officer and XL shots, real payment/cooldown rollback, delayed contact, signature application, automatic aggressor filtering, mixed-tier ballistic diminishing returns at zero capacitor, immutable launch skills, restart/cancellation, v33 migration, concurrent kill settlement and production dispatch/HUD/observer packets. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy tests), and formatting verification passed across 343 files. Tests used isolated temporary worlds/catalogs; larger launchers used artificial fitting budgets rather than claiming their Ibis fixture is a legal in-game fit.

Native/deployment acceptance remains pending because the maintainer instructed us to leave game windows alone. No server/client restart, staging, game input or normal-save mutation was performed. Required visible checks are launcher/ammunition behavior, moving missile/warhead/impact effects, expiry/lost-target behavior, observers and late joins, uninterrupted repeat circles, reward/wreck ownership and paid-state conservation through transitions. The Windows client guide records the reproducible checks for when testing resumes.

## Fitting expansion milestone 21: scripted tracking, guidance and sensor modules

Build 3662168 contains **32 published modules** across these three unrestricted families: group 213 has 11 tracking computers, group 1396 has five missile guidance computers, and group 212 has 16 sensor boosters. All are now admitted through their complete fitted/online/default/overload-metadata profiles, across their actual Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer tiers. This cohort has no published deadspace or Abyssal profiles. Synthetic ship restrictions, Abyssal metadata, missing defaults and unsupported active modifier functions are rejected. Remote tracking/sensor boosters, guidance disruptors and drone tracking modules remain separate families. No inventory/skill/money grants, market policy changes or manufacturing additions are made.

The default effects are **4559 / gunneryMaxRangeFalloffTrackingSpeedBonus**, **6135 / missileGuidanceComputerBonus4**, and **2670 / sensorBoosterActivePercentage**. Each is category 1, warp-safe, pays capacitor attribute 6 and uses duration 73. Tracking Computer I costs 7 GJ per ten seconds and adds 5% optimal, 10% falloff and 10% tracking. Tech II costs 10 GJ and adds 7.5%/15%/15%; Cormack's costs 14 GJ and adds 9.9%/19.8%/21%. Missile Guidance Computer I costs 7 GJ and adds 4% missile speed/lifetime, 5.5% explosion velocity and -5.5% explosion radius. Tech II retains 7 GJ and uses 5.5%/5.5%/8.25%/-8.25%; the Caldari Navy and Republic Fleet variants retain their distinct range/application strengths. Sensor Booster I costs 10 GJ and adds 25% lock range/scan resolution and 40% sensor strength; Tech II costs 12 GJ and gives 30%/30%/48%. All other profiles retain their own catalog values rather than inheriting a tier-wide multiplier.

The existing typed dogma graph applies tracking computers only to equipment requiring Gunnery, and guidance computers to loaded ammunition requiring Missile Launcher Operation. Sensor boosters modify the hull's target range, scan resolution and four sensor strengths. Their equipment modifiers share the existing attribute/operator stacking sequences with passive tracking enhancers, guidance enhancers and signal amplifiers; earned skills remain exempt. Manual firing/target-lock operations read these effective values. Sensor-strength attributes are primed, but this does not implement the separate ECM/jamming family.

All **seven published scripts** are supported as real owned, reusable one-unit sublocations: Optimal Range/Tracking Speed (group 907), Targeting Range/Scan Resolution/ECCM (910), and Missile Range/Missile Precision (1400). Complete script effect sets are validated, including native `otherID` ItemModifier targets and post-percent operator 6. The graph adapts those modifiers explicitly to the containing module, without widening the general passive modifier parser. A script doubles selected source bonuses and suppresses alternatives by 100%; script changes themselves do not acquire an equipment stacking penalty. Tracking scripts retain their additional missile-related metadata rather than silently dropping it. Group, charge size and launcher compatibility prevent using a script in the wrong family. Empty modules work with their unscripted base bonuses.

Script loading/unloading uses the existing owned-magazine transactions, source/destination checks and saved deadlines. Capacity and the explicit one-script limit prevent stacked scripts in a module. Catalog reload attribute 1795 is **0.01 ms**, retained as 100 FILETIME ticks rather than replaced with a gun reload. Repeats never consume or damage scripts. Changing a running module's script is rejected even if its previous deadline has passed but its active marker has not yet been removed; stop and complete the paid interval first. Wrong-family/size/effect requests preserve stacks, the existing magazine and its deadline. Swap/unload/restart conserve all script units.

The scoped support controller pays capacitor, adds a connection-owned `ActiveFittedModule` marker and saves a cycle deadline in one transaction. It retains one HUD active effect across ordinary repeats, rechecks each payment, finishes the paid interval on manual stop, and removes the bonus when another interval cannot be paid. Docking, gate/hull transitions and disconnect stop the live effect; warp remains valid for these effects. Restart clears active markers while preserving scripts, capacitor expenditure and deadlines. Existing schema **34** already owns those tables; no migration is needed. Duplicate activation does not pay twice. Invalid wire shapes are declined before advancing the controller. The current pilot, fitted slot, online state, skill requirements, live hull and requested effect are checked before payment.

Native module primes include effective scripted source bonuses and the controller's active effect. Start/stop also notify effective turret, loaded missile and hull sensor attributes. Repeats update timed space effects without resetting the HUD effect. Shared-space contacts and snapshots carry typed support contacts, so existing observers and fresh snapshots replay the same effect. Tracking/guidance use catalog GUID `effects.TurretWeaponRangeTrackingSpeedMultiplyActivate`; sensors use `effects.ElectronicAttributeModifyActivate`, through the existing timed OnSpecialFX adapter. Source/module/ship identities and paid start time remain explicit. This reuses the established Blue/Destiny wire path and does not change Carbon simulation or ball encoding.

References: read-only pinned catalog types/typedogma/dogmaeffects/dogmaattributes, plus the inert build-3662168 ModuleButton/EffectWrapper activation inspection recorded in milestones 18–20. The same code.ccp SHA-256 and Carbon Blue/Destiny source revisions recorded there remain in use. The static fixtures contain 43 types (32 modules, seven scripts, three prerequisite skills and Ibis), 32 effects and 149 attribute definitions; they contain no normal world/save data. Integration fixtures use artificial medium-slot/CPU/power budgets where multiple large test combinations require them, and place ships outside physical celestial/station collision volumes for targeting acceptance.

Automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. All **957 .NET tests** passed in **18 minutes**, including the 916 existing cases and 41 new support-module cases. All 41 focused support-module tests also passed independently. Coverage includes full catalog admission, representative tier costs/bonuses, all seven scripts, gun/miner isolation, loaded missile flight/application, mixed active/passive stacking in both activation orders, script conservation and active-swap rejection, unsupported-profile/skill/payment rollback, restart cleanup, unpaid repeats, production dispatch/primes, stable HUD effects, extended target lock/removal, and observer/snapshot effects. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy tests), and formatting verification passed across 350 files. All changed behavior was tested in isolated temporary worlds/catalogs.

Deployment and native acceptance remain pending under the maintainer's game-window hold. No game-window input, staged deployment, running-server/client restart or normal-save mutation occurred. Native checks still need to cover actual fit/script drag-and-drop, tooltip/target-lock/application values, tier-specific costs, continuous repeat rings, warp and paid stop, observer/snapshot visuals, and reconnect/restart conservation. The Windows client guide records those checks separately from the automated evidence.

## Fitting expansion milestone 22: tackle and warp navigation

The resumed module goal prioritizes unrestricted tackle/navigation before ship-restricted equipment. Build 3662168 has **82 published unrestricted static profiles** in this cohort: 18 webifiers (group 65), 53 disruptors/scramblers (52), eight warp core stabilizers (315) and three hyperspatial accelerators (1289). Every static Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction, officer and heavy profile present in these groups is admitted through its complete catalog effects. Five Abyssal profiles remain excluded because per-item mutations are not implemented. Cloaks and micro jump systems remain separate work. No normal-save grants, market changes or manufacturing offers are added.

Targeted defaults are **6426 / remoteWebifierFalloff**, **39 / warpDisrupt**, and **5934 / warpScrambleBlockMWDWithNPCEffect**. They require an online medium-slot module, its earned skills, a live source hull, an actual locked player ship or NPC in the same system, target range and module range. Payment, the live contact and the saved cycle deadline commit together. Each repeat revalidates and pays its actual catalog capacitor cost; Propulsion Jamming reduces qualifying costs by 5% per earned level. Civilian web/disruptor profiles do not require that skill and receive no discount. Civilian disruption strength is genuinely zero. Manual stop completes the paid cycle. Unpaid repeats, target-lock loss, transitions, hull loss and disconnect remove live effects; restart clears effects while retaining capacitor expenditure and deadlines.

Web strength uses source speedFactor (20) multiplied by target stasisWebifierResistance (2115, default 1). Max velocity (37) shares the existing strongest-first dogma stacking sequence, with independent positive/negative sequences and skills exempt. Tech I is -50%, Tech II -60%, X5 -55%, and the civilian module -35%; every other profile retains its own values. All 18 static web profiles have zero falloff (2044). Nonzero or unsupported falloff metadata is rejected. NPC maximum and cruise speed are scaled together to preserve the catalog cruise/max ratio; actual NPC integration and native SetMaxSpeed both consume those values. Player motion retains inertia rather than truncating velocity when a web starts or ends.

Disruption strength (105) adds to target warpScrambleStatus (104) without a stacking penalty. Warp is denied when the total is positive: equal core and hostile strength permits it. Scramblers also add activationBlocked (1349) to MWDs requiring High Speed Maneuvering, cancel a running MWD, and restore its online signature/mass/speed policy. A disruptor does not block MWDs. Targeting remains available during warp alignment, so new points cancel an existing alignment order; actual warp entry clears targeting. Subcapital gate travel and ordinary station docking remain available. Micro jump stoppers remain catalog validation metadata until that module family is implemented.

Warp core effect **8121 / warpCoreStabilizerActive** is a self-only, non-warp-safe low-slot activation. It pays 80 GJ, grants -2 disruption status for its own duration (10 seconds Tech I, 15 Tech II, 18 Aura), forces a single cycle, and retains the 150-second post-cycle reactivation delay (669) through stop/restart. Shared sweeps expire core strength even if the owner's controller has not advanced. Online scan resolution, targeting range and drone bandwidth penalties use the catalog's own modifiers. Only one stabilizer can fit; offline removes its online penalties. Drone bandwidth math does not implement drone combat. Core strength does not bypass scrambler MWD blocking.

The three hyperspatial accelerators use online effect 5900 to add source warpSpeedAddition (1950) to warpSpeedMultiplier (600), with a maximum of three fitted. The effective baseWarpSpeed (1281) times multiplier now drives both saved pilot flight and in-session fitting refresh; raw hull values previously hid this fitted bonus from actual warp timing. Basic/Experimental/Prototype add 0.2/0.25/0.3 AU/s on the Ibis's base of 1.

Typed source contacts feed the existing timed native effects: `effects.ModifyTargetSpeed`, `effects.WarpDisrupt`, `effects.WarpScramble`, and the core's catalog `effects.Invulnerability`. Source repeats preserve the single HUD active effect. The victim also receives named `OnEwarStart`/`OnEwarEnd` notifications with exactly `(sourceBallID, moduleID, targetBallID, buffSlot)`. Pinned buffBarConst names are `webify`, `warpScrambler` and `warpScramblerMWD`; these differ from the module effect names. Continuous repeats do not churn victim status indicators. Effective speed, disruption status, MWD activation block and capacitor/cooldown values use explicit module attribute notifications.

Schema **35** adds WorldTackle with owned source/module identities, polymorphic ship/NPC target, paid attributes and start/expiry. Migration from version 34 is transactional. Live rows have no polymorphic target foreign key; sweeps validate target existence, system and hull state. Source item/ship/pilot references use normal ownership foreign keys. Restart removes live rows and active markers while retaining resource/cooldown rows. Native Blue/Destiny encoding is reused.

References: read-only pinned catalog types/typedogma/dogmaeffects/dogmaattributes and inert inspection of tactical.OnEwarStart/End, OnEwarOnConnect, dogma.effects.GetEwarTypeByEffectID and buffBarConst in unchanged code.ccp SHA-256 `37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368`. Carbon Destiny revision `114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072`, Ballpark.cpp SetMaxSpeed and Thunkers.cpp PySetMaxSpeed, confirms the native speed update and inertia boundary. CCP's [warp scrambling/disruption reference](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/115004925705-Warp-Scrambling-and-Warp-Disruption) supports additive strength, equality and the distinct MWD policy; its [stacking reference](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203280381-Bonuses-and-Stacking-Penalties) distinguishes absolute warp strength from penalized bonuses. The isolated fixture contains 91 types, 26 effects and 135 attribute definitions. Automated combinations use artificial CPU/power/capacitor and rack budgets; live acceptance uses legal Ibis fits in a copied save.

During manual acceptance, the maintainer found that the safety selector could not change to yellow. The host trace confirmed native `crimewatch.SetSafetyLevel` calls reached Eden but had no handler. The existing GetClientStates response also hardcoded full safety. The handler now validates the exact 0/1/2 wire values, saves a typed per-pilot safety preference and returns Python None; GetClientStates returns that saved preference. Pinned crimewatchSvc.SetSafetyLevel updates its own state and scatters OnSafetyLevelChanged after a successful reply, so the server does not duplicate that notification. OnSessionChanged reads the fourth GetClientStates member after ship/system transitions. Schema **36** adds a default-full SafetyLevel to WorldPilot; migration from 35 and new saves preserve full safety until a pilot chooses otherwise. Settings survive restart/reconnect and are isolated between pilots. This enables the native selector; full criminal/suspect timers, CONCORD, security penalties and duel mechanics remain unimplemented. The local player-versus-player test requires red safety because yellow still prevents criminal actions.

The next manual check exposed the pinned client's independent **Alpha clone** restriction: Alpha cannot disable safety in high security space even when the selector RPC works. `clonegrade.const` in the same pinned archive defines Alpha/Omega as 0/1 and the Alpha training multiplier as 0.5. Schema **37** adds a saved per-account CloneGrade, defaulting to Alpha for both migrated and new accounts. Native subscriptionMgr.GetCloneGrade and pilot/account reads now use that saved grade. Training uses half rate and the existing skill/SP caps for Alpha; Omega uses full rate and removes those two Alpha limits while preserving prerequisites, level validation and queue limits. The two accounts in the isolated copied acceptance save were explicitly set to Omega, and fresh development clients were launched. No normal account was upgraded and no external subscription was changed.

The maintainer then observed severe input delay and periodic recovery of the webbed ship's speed. The broad regression run was stopped to separate development load from live behavior; it had been using the runner's default processor-based concurrency on a 20-logical-processor machine. The test assembly now copies an explicit xunit.runner.json with four conservative workers, following [xUnit's configuration reference](https://xunit.net/docs/config-xunit-runner-json). This limits later test load; it does not by itself prove that every reported live delay is resolved. Slow native RPC processing and pilot updates (250 ms or more) now log elapsed time, with RPC dispatch and subsequent updates separated, without logging payloads.

The web discontinuity was a connection-order race: shared cleanup deleted a row at its nominal paid end before the source connection renewed the next paid interval. Remote rows now remain owned by their source controller, which atomically renews the row or explicitly stops it. Shared cleanup still removes invalid hull/module/location/target states, and disconnect/restart clears connection-owned effects. NPC web reads use the same ownership rule. This follows the lifetime already used by other connection-owned active module markers. The source captures wall and monotonic cycle starts before payment so its row and native phase share a start time. A regression deliberately advances the victim first at three consecutive cycle boundaries, then advances the source: actual webbed speed and scrambler strength remain unchanged, EWAR does not end/start, and explicit cancellation restores both. All 40 focused tackle cases passed after this correction. The updated host and fresh Omega clients were staged on the copied acceptance save for a further manual check.

With the broad regression runner stopped, timing logs still showed real multi-second pilot updates (including 6,136 ms) and module dispatches over 1,300 ms. A bounded read-only probe against the copied save and full catalog measured two consecutive pilots' fitting calculations at 419-504 ms. The nonpooled catalog connection was being reopened for individual queries throughout a fitting graph. FittingCalculation now explicitly keeps that connection open for its owning catalog context; disposal still closes it, without introducing pooled handles or cached mutable gameplay state. The same probe measured 53-70 ms for two warm reads after the change (110 ms on the first measured pair). This is a measured calculation improvement, distinct from native end-to-end latency. The broad regression run is bounded to four workers; it is not run during the active manual acceptance check.

Native acceptance: selected **Eden Pilot**, opened the existing Inventory view and selected its active **Ibis**; its cargo view showed 0/125 m3. Opened Fitting and observed Webifier II/Scrambler I plus the Prototype Hyperspatial Accelerator, with **3.30 AU/s** warp speed and **368.8 m/s** maximum speed. Undocked Pilot and Wing, selected the other ship, and issued Wing's native **1,000 m Orbit** before manual takeover. The maintainer then performed the module checks, identified the safety/Alpha restrictions and repeat-boundary gap described above, and after the updated build explicitly confirmed: **“The modules work as intended.”** Host traces showed the saved red safety selection, native module activations and victim OnEwarStart/End notifications. That report confirms the exercised test fits; it does not establish individual native acceptance of all 82 tiers, NPC web motion, every observer/snapshot path or all transitions. Automated coverage remains separate. All staging, fixture skills and module loadouts used the copied test save; the normal save was not changed.

Final automated acceptance: pinned tool/solution restore and build passed with zero warnings/errors. All **1,010 .NET tests** passed in **8 minutes 10 seconds**, including the 957 previous cases, 40 tackle cases, eight safety cases and five clone-grade cases. The focused tackle run also passed all 40 cases independently after the cycle-boundary correction; the account/safety/training focus passed all 20 selected cases. Existing warp-order assertions were updated to exercise actual warp entry, with synthetic ships clear of collision volumes, while retaining the paid-cycle and hull-loss checks. Both Python suites passed (3 catalog setup and 9 formatting-policy cases); formatting verification passed across 361 files and the 140-character width policy. The full run used the four-worker configuration. Read-only production-catalog performance measurements and the maintainer's native acceptance are recorded separately above. Native end-to-end latency after the final connection-lifetime improvement remains unmeasured; the measured fitting bottleneck is substantially reduced.

The maintainer requested stopping after this milestone. The module goal is paused after committing and pushing this verified milestone; no subsequent module family is started.

## Player electronic warfare (November 6, 2026)

The unrestricted player EWAR cohort implements **77 published static modules**: 71 active modules and six passive Signal Distortion Amplifiers. `tools/Export-PlayerEwarCatalog.py` reproducibly extracts the read-only build-3662168 definitions, their requirements, representative hulls, skills and six scripts into `PlayerEwarCatalog.json`. This fixture also retains the eleven excluded burst jammers, so the admission audit verifies the restriction boundary rather than dropping those entries from its input.

| Family | Group | Supported profiles | Default activation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Racial and multispectral ECM | 201 | 43 | 6470 / `remoteECMFalloff` |
| Remote sensor dampeners | 208 | 6 | 6422 / `remoteSensorDampFalloff` |
| Tracking disruptors | 291 | 6 | 6424 / `shipModuleTrackingDisruptor` |
| Guidance disruptors | 291 | 7 | 6423 / `shipModuleGuidanceDisruptor` |
| Target painters | 379 | 9 | 6425 / `remoteTargetPaintFalloff` |
| Signal Distortion Amplifiers | 514 | 6 | Online passive effects 4358/4809–4812 |

All published unrestricted Tech I/named, Tech II, storyline, faction and officer profiles present in these families are admitted. There are no published deadspace or Abyssal entries in this cohort. Synthetic mutations, extra/default-effect changes, unsupported effect metadata and ship restrictions are rejected. All eleven published group-80 burst jammers require specific ship groups in the pinned catalog; they remain unavailable under the unrestricted-first scope. No items, skills, ISK, market offers or manufacturing recipes are granted by this work.

Gameplay owns typed module profiles, range/application rules and effective target modifiers. The integration includes origin/main's resident drone changes through 8d08821. World owns complete catalog admission, ownership/skills, fitting/online checks, source/target health and location, saved capacitor/cooldowns, legality and atomic contact updates. Protocol owns effect-name dispatch, connection-owned cycles, module primes, notifications and observer/snapshot effects. ECM/dampening/disruption/painting skills and the four racial Sensor Compensation skills run through the existing catalog graph once. Admitted hull EWAR bonuses now have an operational owner; representative Maulus, Griffin, Vigil and Impairor coverage checks their earned or role bonuses independently of work skills. Signal Distortion Amplifiers affect only ECM modules through their own catalog selectors and ordinary stacking sequences.

Targeted activation requires a locked live player ship or NPC in the same system, within the effective lock range and the 250 km targeting envelope. It pays the actual effective capacitor cost and writes the contact and cycle deadline in one transaction. Player aggression is checked before payment and recorded even when ECM fails. Non-ECM falloff scales strength by `0.5 ** (((distance - optimal) / falloff) ** 2)` outside optimal. ECM instead rolls racial jamming strength / target racial sensor strength, scaled by range and ECM resistance, clamped to [0,1]; zero resistance is immunity and rolls at or above the resulting probability fail. A failed roll still spends the cycle. Successful ECM removes unrelated locked and pending targets, including completing pending RPCs with cancellation, while permitting any currently successful jammer to be targeted. NPC and player jamming share that targeting exception. A jammed NPC may still attack the jammer itself, but cannot redirect that exception to the jammer's drones.

Dampening modifies ship lock range and scan resolution with target dampening resistance. Tracking disruption modifies turret optimal/falloff/tracking without affecting miners or missiles. Guidance disruption modifies loaded missile motor lifetime/speed and explosion radius/velocity without changing ship speed or turret tracking. Painting modifies signature radius, consumed by lock timing and turret/missile/drone application. Effective modifiers share the native strongest-first dogma stacking sequences. NPC combat uses effective attributes in both SQL and resident paths; NPC missile guidance uses the hull's missile multipliers. Where an NPC catalog profile omits maxTargetRange, dampening uses Eden's existing detection envelope (including nominal missile range where available) as its temporary lock ceiling. This is an explicit adaptation of the existing NPC policy, rather than a claim that the client catalog supplied a missing range.

Scripts 29005/29007 (group 909), 29013/29015 (911) and 40334/40335 (1569) are real reusable one-unit magazines. Selected bonuses double and alternatives are suppressed; the catalog's 0.01 ms reload remains 100 FILETIME ticks. The guidance disruption scripts retain pinned launcherGroup 1396 even though their actual disruptors belong to group 291; the adapter accepts that exact combination only with matching chargeGroup 1569. Wrong-family scripts and active swaps preserve all inventory and paid state. Active markers prevent swapping even after a nominal deadline until the source controller settles its repeat or stop.

Source controllers own paid intervals. Victim-first updates at consecutive repeat boundaries cannot remove an effect before renewal; repeats preserve one HUD active effect. Manual shutdown completes the paid interval. Unpaid repeats, lock loss, source transitions, hull loss and disconnect remove outgoing effects. Incoming player effects may remain on a timed logged-off victim; ordinary travel removes incoming contacts. **World schema 96** adds WorldPlayerEwar, owned module/source/pilot foreign keys, target indexes and transactional fit-cache invalidation triggers. The polymorphic target has no invalid ship-only foreign key. Warm source and victim ticks use resident contact snapshots and a module-ownership/online membership view; they perform no SQL, open no connections and rebuild no fits until a relevant change. Controllers own cancellation; observer ticks do not sweep relational state. Module membership invalidates on item/escrow changes and catalog resets. Restart clears live contacts/active markers while retaining resources, scripts and deadlines. Version-95 migration and repeated initialization are covered without touching the user's save.

Native evidence is inert inspection only: build-3662168 `code.ccp`, SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**, `godma.pyj`, `shipmodulebutton.pyj` and `buffBarConst.pyj`, plus catalog types/typedogma/effects/attributes. `OnEwarStart`/`OnEwarEnd` retain `(sourceBallID,moduleID,targetBallID,buffSlot)`. Successful ECM also supplies `OnJamStart` with the same identities, `electronic`, paid start time and duration, and `OnJamEnd` with the four identifying fields. Observer and snapshot contacts use the catalog GUIDs `effects.SensorDampening`, `effects.TrackingDisruption`, `effects.TargetPaint` and `effects.ElectronicAttributeModifyTarget` through the existing timed OnSpecialFX carrier. The [Carbon Destiny reference](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/tree/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072) was inspected at revision **114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072**, including its network client event path; no Carbon ball/transport/Blue format changes are introduced. CCP's [October ECM change](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/patch-notes-for-october-2018-release) independently documents the jammer-targeting exception; all module values come from the pinned local catalog.

## Player remote shield and armor repairs, November 7, 2026

This first player logistics milestone admits **89 published unrestricted static modules** from build 3662168: all **44 remote shield boosters** (group 41) and **45 remote armor repairers** (325), small through capital sizes, across the catalog tiers where present. All four Abyssal remote armor profiles remain rejected. Ancillary/mutadaptive repair families, remote hull repair, capacitor transmitters, NPC/structure targets, overheating and ship-restricted equipment remain separate work. No items, skills, ISK, market supply or manufacturing recipes are granted. `tools/Export-RemoteRepairCatalog.py` reproduces the 93-module fixture, including excluded profiles and representative logistics hulls/prerequisites.

Native activation uses `dogmaIM.Activate(moduleID,effectName,targetID,repeat)` with `shipModuleRemoteShieldBooster` (6186) or `shipModuleRemoteArmorRepairer` (6188). It requires an owned online high-slot repairer, earned prerequisites, a live source and a different locked player ship in the same system within the effective targeting envelope. Unsupported profiles, self/NPC targets, wrong owners/effects, missing skills, offline modules, destroyed ships, unavailable targets and insufficient capacitor reject before payment or healing. Medium fitted effect 6953 applies its catalog CPU/grid multiplier. Ordinary/capital emission and remote-repair skills use their own selectors once; supported hull shield/armor logistics bonuses now have an operational owner. Other remote equipment groups remain deferred.

Shield HP transfers immediately on payment; armor HP transfers once at the saved cycle end after source/module/target and assistance revalidation. Both clip to missing HP and cannot revive a destroyed hull. Edge distance subtracts both radii. Beyond optimal, amount scales by `0.5 ** (((distance - optimal) / falloff) ** 2)` and target `remoteRepairImpedance` (2116, default 1). Attribute 854, `disallowAssistance`, rejects assistance. Unaffected saved damage is preserved. Repeats pay each successful new cycle; manual shutdown finishes the paid interval. Lock/target loss, source travel, offlining, ship loss and disconnect cancel outgoing cycles. Restart clears pending remote work and retains paid capacitor/cooldowns, with no offline healing. This deliberately differs from local-repair paid completion.

Typed assistance rules run before payment. High-security aid to suspects, criminals, outlaws or pilots in a limited engagement is blocked pending CONCORD enforcement, with safety/Alpha restrictions also enforced. Low-security aid inherits legal flags and incurs the additional suspect offense for intervening in an engagement or an outlaw's active combat; a peaceful outlaw alone creates no offense. Weapons, PvP, NPC and legal timers transfer with their remaining deadlines, so mutually repairing pilots cannot repeatedly reset countdowns. Assistance creates no hostile contact or limited engagement against the repaired pilot.

**World schema 98** adds `WorldRemoteRepair` with module/source/pilot/target references and a target index. Payment, timers, cooldown and healing/schedule commit together through the existing world owner and transaction projection bridge. Committed changes invalidate resident resources; rejections preserve them. Per-frame module membership uses dependency-invalidated resident snapshots, without SQL or fit recalculation once warmed. Post-commit repair events update shared health and the recipient's native attributes. Receiving notifications read current committed health to avoid overwriting a later hit with an older repair message. Repeats preserve one HUD activation. Observers and snapshots receive targeted, nonoffensive `effects.ShieldTransfer` and `effects.RemoteArmourRepair` effects.

Sources: read-only pinned catalog types/typedogma/effects/attributes and inert code-object inspection of staged `code.ccp`, SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. `godma.pyj` confirms activation arguments and the existing `OnGodmaShipEffect` environment; `shipmodulebutton.pyj` resolves target/repeat; `crimewatchSvc.pyj` distinguishes assistance for safety. Consulted [Carbon Blue Marshal.cpp](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp), revision **af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997**, for the existing tuple/list/primitive boundary; no codec changes are needed. CCP's [Crimewatch introduction](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/introducing-the-new-and-improved-crimewatch), [neutral-assistance update](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/war-war-sometimes-changes) and [criminal-timer reference](https://support.eveonline.com/hc/en-us/articles/203268591-Criminal-Timer) establish remaining-time inheritance and later high-security restrictions. No client was launched or controlled.

## Player remote hull repair, November 7, 2026

Ship-content milestone **4a** adds all **eight published static remote hull repairers** from build 3662168, group 585: T1/T2 small, medium, large and capital profiles (3986, 4296, 4299, 27904, 27930, 27932, 27934 and 41475). The native activation name is `shipModuleRemoteHullRepairer`, effect **6185**; its catalog graphic is `effects.RemoteHullRepair`. Amount comes from `structureDamageAmount` (**83**, typed `HullRepairAmount`); duration, capacitor, optimal, falloff and impedance use the same explicit targeted-repair fields as existing logistics. Remote Hull Repair Systems (**27902**) and Capital Remote Hull Repair Systems (**27936**) each contribute their catalog 5% capacitor reduction per earned level to modules requiring that skill. Capital profiles require both skills, so both multipliers apply once; neither changes amount or duration. No new hull bonus classification is inferred.

Capacitor, assistance consequences, cooldown and the pending cycle commit atomically at activation. Hull HP is applied at the saved cycle end after source/module/target and assistance revalidation. Healing clips to missing structure, preserves armor and cannot revive a destroyed ship. Edge distance, falloff and target impedance are evaluated at completion. Repeated or early completions cannot duplicate or accelerate healing. Manual deactivation finishes the paid interval; lock loss, disconnect, travel or offlining cancel pending work. Restart clears pending work while retaining spent capacitor, cooldowns and completed repairs. Existing `WorldRemoteRepair` storage is reused with the appended Hull enum value; no schema change or offline healing is introduced.

Committed repair events use current recipient health for shield, armor and structure attribute updates; structure damage is native attribute **3**, not hit points or repair amount. Native activation/repeats retain one HUD cycle and targeted nonoffensive observer effects, including snapshot replay. Hull updates cannot overwrite a later hit with a stale repair event. No items, skills, supply, production recipes or safety privileges are granted.

## Player capacitor warfare and transfers, November 7, 2026

Ship-content milestone **4b's player-target checkpoint** admits **146 published static profiles** from build 3662168: **37 remote capacitor transmitters** (group 67), **55 energy nosferatu** (68) and **54 energy neutralizers** (71), including their capital and static faction/officer/storyline/CONCORD tiers where present. All eight mutated nosferatu/neutralizer templates remain rejected. These are player-to-player operations; NPC capacitor pools and neutralizer effects on NPC repair behavior remain unfinished. The coverage exporter records that missing consumer on all **109 hostile profiles**, alongside their pending overheating checks. Remote tracking/sensor assistance also remains in milestone 4. No new hull cohort, supply or acquisition chain is enabled.

`CapacitorWarfareRules` owns typed profile validation and two-pool calculations. Each successful pulse spends its effective capacitor cost immediately, applies the transfer/drain immediately, and saves a cooldown at its exact duration. Transmitters add their amount up to the target's available capacity; activation payment is independent of the amount received. Neutralizers remove up to the target's remaining charge. Nosferatu compare absolute remaining GJ, then drain and replenish the source up to its capacity. Equal/higher source capacitor prevents ordinary nosferatu draining; Blood Raider overrides remove that comparison. Empty targets, full recipients, zero resistance and insufficient source energy have explicit bounded outcomes. Range uses edge distance and continuous falloff. Capital nosferatu/neutralizers use `min(1,targetSignature/moduleSignatureResolution)`; ordinary profiles have no resolution penalty.

Transmitters use effect **6184**, `shipModuleRemoteCapacitorTransmitter`, amount **90** and resistance **6463** (`remoteCapacitorImpedance`), independently of remote-repair impedance. Nosferatu use **6197**, `energyNosferatuFalloff`, amount 90; neutralizers use **6187**, `energyNeutralizerFalloff`, amount **97**. Both hostile families use energy-warfare resistance **2045**. Duration/cost/range/falloff use catalog attributes 73/6/54/2044; capital signature resolution is **2451**. The catalog's common overload effect **3002** is recognized metadata, not implemented heat behavior.

Capacitor Emission Systems (**3423**) and Capital Capacitor Emission Systems (**24572**) each apply their earned 5% per-level capacitor reduction through their own required-skill selector. Capital transmitters require both skills, so both multipliers apply once; capital nosferatu/neutralizers retain their actual ordinary-emission requirements. Hull energy modifiers now have an operational consumer on the **18 already-admitted energy-bonus hulls**. Pinned Blood Raider effect **5854** assigns attribute **1945** from the hull onto fitted nosferatu modules using operation 7. The parser accepts that specific group/attribute/source assignment; activation reads the effective module flag. Capital nosferatu signature overrides continue through the module's effective resolution field. No generic unknown assignment or unsupported hull effect is admitted.

The World operation validates active ownership, online high-slot membership, earned prerequisites, live source and target, lock, system and range before committing payment. Offensive immunity and assistance restrictions are distinct. Transfer assistance reuses the same safety, remaining-timer inheritance and engagement rules as remote repairs through `AssistancePersistence`. Hostile pulses use existing player aggression rules and ordered drone-module/incoming triggers, including attempts which drain no energy. Unsafe or invalid requests preserve both pools, cooldowns, active state and aggression; no event publishes before commit. `WorldShipEnergy`, `WorldWeaponCooldown` and `WorldActiveModule` are reused without a migration. Resident resource projection/checkpoint boundaries preserve same-tick changes, rollback and competing-command serialization. Restart retains resource changes and paid cooldowns, clears active modules and never replays a pulse.

The new exact-GJ boundary regression exposed rounding drift in the shared recharge curve when elapsed time was zero: two saved 1,000-GJ pools with different capacities could compare as unequal and incorrectly permit an 8-GJ nosferatu drain. `ChargedResource.Recharged` now returns the exactly clamped saved balance at zero/nonpositive elapsed time when a positive recharge duration exists. Positive-elapsed recharge retains the existing exponential curve; the clock is not rounded. Direct curve cases and the real two-ship activation regression cover this correction.

`CapacitorWarfareHandler` owns native repeats, paid shutdown and cancellation; the shared activation router and pilot coordinator advance/clear it alongside existing modules. Manual stop finishes the paid interval without a third pulse. Lock loss, source travel/warp, committed offlining, ship changes and disconnect stop future pulses without refunds. Targeted `effects.EnergyTransfer`, `effects.EnergyVampire` and `effects.EnergyDestabilization` graphics replay through shared contacts and snapshots; only the hostile graphics are offensive. Incoming HUD slots distinguish `ewEnergyVampire` and `ewEnergyNeut`. The bounded contact registry is indexed by recipient and module, shares unchanged arrays and expires at exact deadlines. Dirty recipient events read current committed capacitor rather than replaying an old balance. Unchanged warmed source/recipient frames perform no SQL, connection opens or fitting recalculations.

Sources: pinned catalog fingerprint **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**, including all module effects/attributes, emission skill selectors **5607/2356** and hull modifier selectors. `tools/Export-CapacitorModuleCatalog.py` reproduces the 154-profile fixture, including excluded mutation templates, relevant hulls and prerequisite closure. Inert inspection of staged `dogma/effects/__init__.pyj` and `buffBarConst.pyj` confirms both native energy slots; catalog effect guids establish graphics. The client archive is the same verified build-3662168 SHA-256 recorded in the preceding checkpoint. CCP's [Odyssey 1.1 balance explanation](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/28-ships-later) establishes the absolute-GJ nosferatu comparison. The historical [Revelations 2.2 explanation](https://www.eveonline.com/es/news/view/more-balancing-changes-for-revelations-2.2) separates NPC nosferatu drains from neutralizer effects on NPC repair chance; those consumers remain open here. [EOS's nosferatu effect](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/blob/c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494/eos/eve_obj/effect/neut/energy_nosferatu_falloff.py) independently reads the override from module attributes; its fitting-rate calculation is not a server simulation. Existing Carbon-reviewed Blue/Destiny packet construction is reused, without codec or movement changes. No client was launched or controlled.

**Automated validation:** Tool/solution restore and solution builds passed with zero warnings/errors. The **400-case affected run** passed before the zero-elapsed recharge correction (**260.25 seconds** test runner, **262.01 seconds** restore/build/test sequence). ## NPC capacitor pools and drains, November 7, 2026

Milestone 4's NPC capacitor checkpoint extends all **55 static nosferatu** and **54 static neutralizers** to live NPC ships with verified capacitor profiles. Transfers remain player-to-player. No hull or module admission changes: the production inventory retains **235 hulls**, **3,350 modules/rigs**, **800 charges** and **76 combat drones**. Exactly **109** coverage records change. The remaining **54** energy-consumer failures identify neutralizer suppression of NPC repair chance; that formula remains evidence-blocked. Overheating and remote tracking/sensor assistance retain their separate unfinished milestones.

`NpcCapacitorRules` reads nominal capacity **482** and recharge duration **55** and uses the existing charged-resource curve. Catalog attribute **18** binds charge to maximum attribute 482 and recharge-time attribute 55. A new identity starts at full capacity; subsequent reads recharge the saved balance at exact elapsed time and preserve exact charge at zero elapsed time. Invalid/nonfinite values and positive-capacity profiles without a verified positive recharge duration are rejected. Zero-capacity actors have an inert pool. Every admitted ordinary NPC spawn profile has a valid nominal profile; this does not admit arbitrary structures or wreck-like Entity definitions with incomplete recharge data.

Schema **103** adds `WorldNpcEnergy`, keyed by NPC identity with charge and update time and a cascading `WorldNpc` foreign key. Pools are saved lazily on capacitor commands rather than updated by a per-frame SQL loop. Transactions project resident state before validating live targets and commit source payment, target charge, cooldown, active module, NPC hostility and ordered drone triggers together. Rejections preserve those fields and publish no resource event. NOS uses the same absolute-GJ gate and fitted Blood Raider override as player targets; neutralizers clamp to available charge. Both retain edge distance, falloff, resistance and capital signature scaling. NPC attacks use NPC aggression consequences without player-versus-player timers, HP damage contributions or bounty credit. Only the source receives a personal capacitor notification.

Checkpoints retain the saved pool and paid cooldown. Restart clears live module activity without replaying payment or drain. A replacement NPC identity starts a fresh pool; deleting an NPC cascades its saved energy row. Ordinary NPC weapon and repair AI remain independent of this nominal pool. NOS does not suppress repairs. Neutralizer repair suppression is deliberately unimplemented until its amount, chance and recovery rules can be established; the nominal pool is not substituted for that private formula.

Native cycles carry an explicit NPC target kind, reuse the existing offensive energy graphics, and avoid registering a personal incoming HUD for NPC recipients. Repeats revalidate locks, source state and NPC availability. NPC warp state now includes the shared contact's authoritative `Warping` state as well as transit membership, so a warp without a current course also cancels the next pulse. Paid shutdown and loss/warp cancellation preserve payment already committed. Twelve unchanged warmed session frames assert zero SQL commands, connection opens and fitting calculations; paid command transactions remain outside that claim.

Evidence: the pinned catalog fingerprint remains **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**, and inert client inspection uses the build-3662168 archive SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. CCP's historical [Revelations 2.2 explanation](https://www.eveonline.com/es/news/view/more-balancing-changes-for-revelations-2.2) distinguishes NPC energy leeching from neutralizer suppression of repair chance; its [Odyssey 1.1 explanation](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/28-ships-later) establishes the absolute-GJ NOS gate. NPC capacitor-level attributes **1894–1897** and neutralizer size modifiers exist in the catalog, but no exposed modifier program establishes their repair formula. Client inspection found their constants, not an authoritative server implementation. The independent historical [EVEmu NPC implementation](https://github.com/EvEmu-Project/evemu_Crucible/blob/f55b488a006e3090470d3b481aee47a1d7a0dade/src/eve-server/npc/NPC.cpp) initializes capacitor from capacity; its [AI implementation](https://github.com/EvEmu-Project/evemu_Crucible/blob/f55b488a006e3090470d3b481aee47a1d7a0dade/src/eve-server/npc/NPCAI.cpp) keeps repair probabilities separately. These were read as supporting historical evidence, with no source copied. Carbon's public repositories expose no private NPC gameplay formula; existing Carbon-reviewed Blue/Destiny packet construction is reused without codec or movement formula changes.
