## Remote-assistance calculation foundation, November 7, 2026

Milestone 4's next foundation covers the calculations for all **eight static remote tracking computers** (group **209**) and **eight remote sensor boosters** (**290**) in build 3662168. `RemoteAssistanceRules` defines their typed cycle/range/bonus profiles, continuous falloff and recipient modifiers. These modules remain unadmitted until their authoritative World operation, saved effect lifecycle and native controller are connected and validated. No production coverage or hull/module admission changes; no schema migration occurs in this foundation.

Tracking effect **6428**, `shipModuleRemoteTrackingComputer`, projects optimal, falloff and tracking percentages onto the recipient's location modules requiring Gunnery. It does not boost the recipient hull or missile ammunition. Sensor effect **6427**, `remoteSensorBoostFalloff`, projects targeting range, scan resolution and all four sensor strengths onto the recipient ship. Recipient remote electronic assistance impedance **2135** scales both families. Range uses the existing continuous `0.5 ^ (((edgeDistance - optimal) / falloff) ^ 2)` strength convention outside optimal, with exact optimal and zero-falloff boundaries. These are positive percentage modifiers in the recipient's ordinary dogma graph, sharing stacking order with comparable local bonuses rather than receiving an independent exemption.

Scripts use existing tracking **907** and sensor **910** charge families. The fixture includes Optimal Range **28999**, Tracking Speed **29001**, Targeting Range **29009**, Scan Resolution **29011** and ECCM **41155**. Their real catalog modifiers concentrate bonuses on the source module before projection. They remain nonconsumable script definitions; this foundation does not enable loading them into these still-unadmitted remote modules. Invalid/nonfinite cycle, range, cost, bonus and application inputs fail explicitly.

Sources: inert inspection of the pinned `remoteTrackingComputer.pyj.BaseRemoteTrackingComputer` confirms three post-percent Gunnery location selectors; `sensorFalloff.pyj.BaseSensorFalloffEffect` confirms the six ship attributes. The archive was hash-verified again as **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. Catalog fingerprint remains **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**; target attribute metadata establishes shared stacking, and the tracking effect plus sensor module `remoteResistanceID` field identify impedance 2135. CCP's [March 2016 patch notes](https://www.eveonline.com/de/news/view/patch-notes-for-march-2016-release) establish continuous strength reduction in falloff rather than random electronic-warfare success. Its [Trinity script record](https://www.eveonline.com/news/view/eve-trinity-features-improvements-and-balance-changes-1) establishes shared nonconsumable scripts for local and remote support. No Carbon-owned codec, transport or Destiny simulation changes occur here.

`tools/Export-RemoteAssistanceCatalog.py` reads the installed catalog in read-only mode and rejects an output path that would overwrite its input. It exports the **16 modules**, **five scripts**, prerequisite closure and relevant hull/attribute/effect definitions into the **39-type** `RemoteAssistanceCatalog.json` fixture. Regenerate with:

```powershell
$catalog = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Eden\catalog.sqlite'
python tools/Export-RemoteAssistanceCatalog.py --catalog $catalog
```

Fixture export reproduced byte-for-byte, catalog bytes remained unchanged, and input-overwrite rejection passed in **4.84 seconds**. Fixture SHA-256: **8AB1794D0DDCED35D227DBD1085A853E29D85508A57E97C14E13C52C8F6FA968**. Documentation checks verified **467 local links and anchors**.

## Player remote tracking and sensor assistance, November 7, 2026

Milestone 4's remote electronic assistance operation enables all **eight static remote tracking computers** (group **209**) and **eight remote sensor boosters** (**290**) across their published static tiers. The preceding calculation foundation is now connected to authoritative payment, saved effect membership, recipient fitting and native cycles. Targets are other pilots' live ships; NPC, drone, structure and self assistance are not admitted. Overheating remains unfinished. NPC neutralizer repair suppression remains evidence-blocked, so this checkpoint does not close milestone 4 or the full completion program.

The production export retains **6,079 entries**, **235 admitted hulls**, **800 charges** and **76 combat drones**, while module/rig admissions increase from 3,350 to **3,366**. Exactly **18 entries** change: these 16 modules and the two future logistics hulls' remote selector classifications. Scimitar and Oneiros move from an unknown-selector evidence block to their planned T2 cohort; they remain unadmitted. The 54 neutralizer repair-suppression consumer checks remain unfinished.

Tracking effect **6428**, `shipModuleRemoteTrackingComputer`, modifies the recipient's fitted modules requiring Gunnery, including their effective optimal, falloff and tracking attributes. Sensor effect **6427**, `remoteSensorBoostFalloff`, modifies targeting range, scan resolution and all four ship sensor strengths. The existing dogma graph owns positive/negative stacking and target attribute bounds. Range strength is sampled at each paid activation/repeat using current edge distance; recipient remote electronic assistance impedance **2135** is read through the current recipient graph. It remains distinct from remote repair and capacitor impedance. Both generic assistance restrictions and the pinned modules' disallow-against-EW-immune policy **1798** are enforced. Unknown effects, altered policy shapes, mutation templates and unverified restrictions fail admission.

All five existing tracking/sensor scripts retain their actual source-module modifiers. They concentrate range/tracking, targeting range/scan resolution or sensor strength before projection and are never consumed by paid cycles. Loading/unloading is rejected while the module remains active or its paid cycle/reload is unfinished. Sensor Linking (**3433**) applies its earned 5% capacitor reduction once through the existing required-skill selector. No new hull cohort is enabled. The **four remote tracking selector effects on each of Scimitar and Oneiros** now classify as operational remote electronic assistance and parse through the generic hull graph; their T2 assembly and real-budget fits remain in milestone 15.

Schema **104** adds `WorldRemoteAssistance`, keyed by module with source pilot/ship, recipient ship, system and a named immutable contact snapshot. Activation validates selected ownership, online medium-slot membership, prerequisites, compatible scripts, live source/recipient, lock, system, targeting range and effect range before saving. Capacitor payment, assistance safety/timer consequences, exact cooldown, active marker and recipient bonus membership commit together. It reuses `AssistancePersistence` rather than hostile player aggression; unsafe assistance leaves resources, timers, cooldown and recipient attributes unchanged. Repeats replace one module's contact atomically and do not accumulate stale bonuses.

SQL membership triggers clear effects when the source module leaves its fit, goes offline, changes ownership/type, loses its active marker, or when either ship docks, changes selection/ownership or leaves the saved system. Recipient fit revision and shared incoming state invalidate in the same transaction; repeated reads before another session frame already see the removal. Coordinate-only movement preserves membership. Trigger-generated changes roll back with the owning command. Checkpoints preserve payment and deadlines; initialization clears live effects and activation markers without refund, offline accumulation or replay.

`RemoteAssistanceHandler` is the selected-pilot native controller, routed by the common activation owner and advanced/cancelled by the pilot coordinator. Paid repeats use one HUD activation, refresh effective source/recipient attributes, and preserve paid manual shutdown. Missing locks, warp, disappearance, committed offlining, ship loss and disconnect cancel further pulses. Late frames start at most one new paid cycle at the current time. The standard dogma prime discovers the controller through `IModuleEffectSource`. Targeted `effects.TurretWeaponRangeTrackingSpeedMultiplyTarget` and `effects.ElectronicAttributeModifyTarget` graphics are nonoffensive and replay through shared contacts/snapshots. Incoming `OnEwarStart`/`OnEwarEnd` use the pinned assistance slots `remoteTracking` and `sensorBooster`.

No client interpreter or game window was launched.

## Ship content milestone 2: hull and fitting foundation, November 7, 2026

Generic `HullSupportRules` now consume explicit tech-level cohorts. Assembly and bonus loading no longer hard-code T1 eligibility. The enabled cohorts still admit precisely the same **235 IDs**, and future T2/T3/capital cohorts need explicit declarations plus verified capabilities. World caches admission by catalog generation and rejects missing effects, unclassified hull modifiers and unsupported operational modifier shapes. Known deferred roles remain visible on already admitted hulls. Invalid stored operational profiles cannot silently receive the legacy corvette bonus path.

Typed ship restrictions validate all 20 group and 12 type alternatives before numeric conversion. Pilot prerequisites remain independently checked by fitting operations. Effective group/type fitted limits now apply to the entire committed fitting, including existing members. Hull assignment modifiers can raise or lower those limits. Attribute **978** (`maxGroupOnline`) is separate: its lowest declared effective value across all fitted members governs the number online, even if the restrictive member is offline. An explicitly assigned zero permits no online modules; absence means no online limit. Explicit online requests reject excess without notifications or state changes. Fitting/attribute changes that lower the limit offline excess members using Eden's existing highest-item-ID tie break and emit ordinary online-state changes. Slot/hardpoint capacity, allocation and final validation use a coherent graph snapshot. Losing an occupied slot rejects the change without dropping equipment.

## Mutadaptive remote armor repairs, November 7, 2026

Milestone 5 now implements all **five published static mutadaptive remote armor repairers** in group **2018**: 49770, 49771, 49772, 49773 and 49774. They retain their exact catalog tier, requirements, fitting costs and one-per-ship limit. Rodiva (49712) and Zarmazd (49713) remain the only permitted types. All five operate on the already admitted Rodiva within its real CPU/power/slot budgets. This adds no hull cohort: Zarmazd assembly and complete T2 role verification remain in milestone 15; overheating remains in milestone 6. Reactive hardeners and assault damage controls remain open in milestone 5. No items, skills, ISK or acquisition sources are granted.

The first paid six-second cycle uses base repair. Each additional completed-cycle continuation on the same target earns one additive step from **2796**, bounded by **2797**: the four ordinary profiles add 10% of base per step up to 150% added; Perun adds 12% up to 180% added. These are additive base-amount bonuses, not compounding multipliers. Earned Rodiva Precursor Cruiser bonuses affect amount and capacitor once, alongside Remote Armor Repair Systems' capacitor reduction. At cruiser V, the basic repairer starts at **637 HP**, caps at **1,592.5 HP**, and costs **261.5625 GJ** per cycle with both relevant skills V. Range receives the actual 525% role bonus. There is no falloff. Recipient impedance is applied to the saved cycle amount at completion, and healing clips to missing armor while preserving other health layers.

Payment, assistance consequences, deadline and cycle state commit together. A repeat requests continuation, not an arbitrary ramp step: World verifies its saved completed predecessor, source/target/module identity and system. Settlement retains a completed marker only for mutadaptive chains, so duplicate/early callbacks cannot heal or advance the ramp. A successful next payment replaces that marker with one pending cycle; failed payments roll back and the session cancels the chain. Manual deactivation finishes the paid interval, then resets; fresh activations start at base. Lock loss, leaving optimal range, offlining, travel, ship loss and disconnect cancel pending work without refund or late healing. Delayed frames settle one paid interval and start at most one new interval at the current time; elapsed time does not fabricate extra steps.

Existing `WorldRemoteRepair.CycleJson` holds named ramp/settled fields with backward-compatible defaults. Ordinary remote cycles still delete their row on settlement. Checkpoints retain committed health, capacitor and cooldowns; initialization clears all connection-owned remote work, including completed ramp markers. Restart never restores an active ramp or performs offline remote healing. Saved-world schema remains **105**. The native activation name preserves the catalog's capital S, `ShipModuleRemoteArmorMutadaptiveRepairer`, with effect **7166**. Existing module activation/priming, capacitor notifications, recipient armor updates and observer replay are reused. The catalog's two graphics, `effects.TriglavianBeam` and `effects.AttackMode`, are emitted separately through the unchanged timed special-effect format. Warm range/cancellation polling uses current shared contacts and the existing cached online lookup.

Sources: pinned build-3662168 catalog fingerprint **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**; `tools/Export-MutadaptiveRemoteRepairCatalog.py` exports the five profiles, both restricted hulls, recipient/baseline definitions and prerequisite/wreck closure into a 30-type inert fixture. CCP's [December 2018 release notes](https://www.eveonline.com/de/news/view/parch-notes-for-december-2018-release) establish the additional-cycle additive rule, one-per-ship limit and restricted hulls; current numeric variants come from the pinned catalog. [EOS revision c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/blob/c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494/eos/eve_obj/effect/repairs/ship_module_remote_armor_mutadaptive_repairer.py) independently treats the maximum as an additional multiplier. No reference source was copied. Inert archive inspection confirmed the native remote-mutadaptive slot and TriglavianBeam implementation using code.ccp SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. Carbon Destiny revision **114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072**, including its ordered special-effect/history handling, was reviewed; no movement arithmetic, Blue codec or Destiny wire shape changes are introduced.

## Assault damage controls and reactive-hardener evidence, November 7, 2026

Milestone 5 now implements the **six ordinary static assault damage control profiles** in build 3662168, including T1/compact/enduring, T2, faction and officer tiers. Their group **60** remains shared with ordinary damage controls, so the catalog one-per-group fitted limit prevents combining an ordinary and assault damage control. The Abyssal template **52230** remains rejected. Group 60's six active profiles now map individually to Advanced Defenses in the coverage inventory; ordinary passive damage controls retain their previous classification.

| 47255 | EFFA Compact Assault Damage Control | 32 GJ | 8.775 s |

The exact restrictions are heavy assault cruisers (**358**), assault frigates (**324**) and the individual Herald (**97793**, electronic-attack group 893). This checkpoint enables **no hull cohort**: all eligible hulls remain outside the current 235-hull assembly set. Production coverage records these six modules as **Partial** for milestone-15 hull consumers. Tests seed existing owned Wolf, Vagabond and Herald hulls with their real catalog attributes, budgets and earned requirements; they do not claim player-reachable T2 assembly or complete hull-role acceptance. The complete T2 cohorts remain in milestone 15.

Online effect **2302** projects twelve module resonance attributes onto the ship by native pre-multiplication, covering all four damage types in shield, armor and structure. Active effect **7012**, `moduleBonusAssaultDamageControl`, post-assigns those twelve module attributes from **2746**, `resistanceMultiplier` (**0.25** on all six profiles). Activation therefore replaces the module's passive values with 0.25; it does not multiply an additional 0.25 into those passive values. Structure module attributes **974–977** map to ship structure attributes **113/111/109/110**. The shared Gameplay graph evaluates these assignments and their dependent ship multipliers. Catalog validation admits exactly those two modifier programs, fitting/online effects, restrictions and one-shot metadata; the generic passive parser is not relaxed to accept arbitrary assignments. Other armor/shield resistance modules retain their existing operator and stacking behavior. Health and damage consumers read the same committed fitting graph.

Activation validates ownership, fitted low slot, online membership, hull restriction, earned skills, hull health and capacitor. Payment, positive `WorldActiveModule.ExpiresAt` and `WorldWeaponCooldown.ReadyAt` commit atomically. The cooldown is the end of the paid interval plus the catalog **150 seconds**. Repeated Activate requests are idempotent while active; requested repeats are normalized to zero. Manual deactivation finishes the paid burst. Offlining, docking, travel, loss and disconnect clear active work while retaining spent capacitor and the original cooldown. Online passive protection remains whenever the module remains online. Checkpoints/restart retain payment and cooldown but clear active bursts; a restart cannot replay or refund activation. Cleanup matches the original paid expiry, so a delayed old controller cannot remove a newer paid burst.

This reuses world schema **105** and the existing world-owner timed expiry and SQL fallback. Resistance bonuses disappear before health/fitting reads at the exact deadline even when the source session is late. Native priming/HUD uses effect 7012, capacitor/resistance/next-activation notifications carry typed values, and observer contacts use `effects.Invulnerability`. SharedSpace prunes expired signature-suppression and assault-control contacts independently, once per expired membership, so observer refresh/re-entry cannot replay a stale burst. Warm active-membership reads use their actual active-row/item-membership dependencies.

The two **reactive armor hardeners**, 4403 and 88709, remain unadmitted and now have an explicit **EvidenceBlocked** classification. Their pinned profiles expose initial armor resonances (0.85 and 0.84 respectively), duration/capacitor, shift amount 6 and active effect **4928**, `adaptiveArmorHardener`, but no modifier program defining adaptation. Inert client inspection found constants and the current-resistance tooltip, not an authoritative server algorithm. [EOS's simulator at c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/blob/c2cc80fd3b1951ac44be3702d59e5047cc7fa494/eos/sim/reactive_armor_hardener.py) models fitting outcomes and averages repeated resistance states; its tick/cycle model does not establish the pinned live server's damage-sampling window, adaptation deadline and edge behavior. The [original investigator discussion](https://github.com/pyfa-org/Pyfa/issues/680) also distinguishes combat-source cases. These are useful evidence, not authority to substitute a steady fitting profile for live adaptation. No simulator source was copied, and no approximate hardener was enabled. Milestone 5 remains open on that evidence; independent overheating work can proceed.

## Overheating calculation foundation, November 7, 2026

Milestone 6 begins with pure typed heat rules and immutable catalog overload programs. This checkpoint does **not** enable overheating: native module/rack toggles, paid-cycle integration, authoritative rack heat/damage, saved module condition, burnout and nanite paste repair remain open. Existing fitting admission, active bonuses, health, inventory and saved-world schema **105** are unchanged. The production coverage export keeps all existing overheating checks false while recording whether their calculation profile validates.

`OverloadCatalog` reads the shared typed catalog boundary and requires a published, admitted non-rig module, excluding Abyssal templates. All **1,826 currently admitted heatable profiles** have one overload effect, spanning **19 distinct native programs**. Each program contains only its own module's percentage modifiers (category 5, `itemID`/`ItemModifier`, operation 6). Unknown domains, selectors, operators, oversized/unnamed attributes, duplicate targets and missing/nonfinite bonus sources remain rejected. Ordinary damage controls, assault damage controls and signature suppressors have no overload program and remain outside this cohort. Valid profiles retain the actual module HP, heat damage, heat absorption, Thermodynamics requirement and immutable target/source bindings.

The programs cover weapon rate/damage, cycle duration, armor/shield repair amount, resistance bonuses, range, propulsion, ECM, tracking/sensor/painter strength, missile guidance and omnidirectional drone tracking links. Modifiers continue to reference effective source attributes inside the Gameplay graph; a future hull/skill modifier can change an overload bonus without replacing it with a hard-coded constant. They target only the intended module and do not change adjacent modules' bonus attributes. No such modifiers are added to live fittings yet. Thermodynamics (**28164**) has its verified 3195 level-scaling and 3196 location-damage selector shape: its -5% per earned level reduces module heat damage, not rack heat generation. The typed damage rule supports levels 0–5 and rejects negative multipliers and invalid values; runtime skill application remains part of activation integration.

Rack profiles keep high, medium and low capacity/dissipation/attenuation separate, with the shared ship generation multiplier. Rig slots do not form a thermal rack. All **657** declared populated racks in the **235 admitted hulls** validate. `HeatRules` follows build 3662168's `dogma/attributes/heatAttribute.pyj.CalculateHeat`, using raw capacity units (normally 100), elapsed milliseconds and unrounded double values. With incoming heat at or above **5e-8**, the rack approaches capacity exponentially using incoming rate times generation multiplier. Below that threshold it cools exponentially by dissipation rate. Native positive Python-2 rounding clears cooled values strictly below **0.5**, retains exactly 0.5, and does not round the remaining temperature. Changing capacity does not forcibly clamp an existing higher temperature; the native heating curve converges toward the new capacity. Independent rack states retain their timestamp and do not rewind it when the supplied clock regresses; sub-millisecond elapsed time is preserved. Invalid inputs and calculation overflow are rejected.

`tools/Export-NativeHeatVectors.py` verifies the archive SHA-256 before extracting only the inert scalar function's code object. It evaluates 13 bounded numeric cases with the staged Python runtime, providing math/round explicitly; it executes no client module-level code, imports no game modules and launches no game process. The committed reference vectors include cold/hot starts, cooling, exact cutoff/rate boundaries, fractional elapsed time, non-default capacity/generation and an initial temperature above capacity. Ordinary automated tests consume the inert JSON and do not require a client installation. The pinned archive remains SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**.

`tools/Export-OverloadCatalog.py` exports a **60-type** representative fixture covering all 19 overload programs, real hull profiles, non-overload controls, Thermodynamics and prerequisite/wreck closure. Its compact matrix separately covers every one of the 1,826 profiles, all 235 admitted hulls, source defaults and attribute bounds. It uses the production coverage inventory's admitted set, guards the expected cohort/build and rejects overwriting source inputs. The matrix records source fingerprint **45BBDCA5C79ECA0353BF05854CDE25042DA02CA3D4607C34BAFE10693C33B2EA**. The [historical EOS heat research](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/issues/12) remains supporting evidence for later damage/spread work, separately from the pinned native temperature calculation. Carbon's [published components](https://github.com/carbonengine) were consulted; this checkpoint changes no Blue/Destiny packet shape or movement arithmetic.

Inert client inspection also identifies the module/rack overload paths and the paste-cost helper in `baseDogmaLocation.pyj.FindNumUnitsToRepairModule`. Those calls are evidence for the remaining operational integration; no synthetic acknowledgement or cost-free overload is added here. Module/rack commands, loaded-script/hull-skill composition, damage probability and ordering, burnout cancellation, condition persistence, paid paste quantities/rate, station repair integration and native heat/condition notifications must be verified before milestone 6 can close.

**Automated validation:** Tool/solution restore and solution build passed with zero warnings/errors in **4.45 seconds**. All **89 affected cases passed**, including **67 new cases**, in **5.81 seconds** for the test command (feature build **1.11 seconds**). The new cases include thirteen native scalar reference vectors, every admitted heatable profile inside the complete 1,826-profile matrix, all 657 populated hull racks, every distinct production program, Thermodynamics selectors/levels, exact cooling/rate thresholds, fractional ticks, clock regression, immutable bindings, effective graph sources, cross-item isolation, missing/duplicate/unknown metadata, malformed domains/operators and overflow. ```

## Persistent module condition and station repair, November 7, 2026

Milestone 6 now has a persistent condition boundary for the forthcoming heat-cycle owner. This checkpoint enables no overload bonus or player-callable damage command. The native calculation and catalog evidence remain in the [preceding record](#overheating-calculation-foundation-november-7-2026). Paid-cycle heat generation, rack damage probability/spread, module/rack commands, scoped activation cancellation and observer notifications, resource resizing on burnout, and nanite paste repair remain open. Overheating coverage stays false for every existing profile.

Saved-world schema **106** adds `WorldModuleCondition`, keyed by the existing module item identity with cascading deletion. No row means healthy. Damage persists through unfitting, movement, ownership changes, checkpoints and restart; repackaging rejects damaged modules. Opening an older save creates an empty table without inventing damage. Finite nonnegative damage is enforced by SQLite and typed Gameplay rules. Current catalog structure HP is used for condition; damage above a changed capacity is retained rather than silently healed.

The internal World transaction helper accepts assembled quantity-one modules fitted to an owned high, medium or low rack, including offline and passive targets. It saturates new damage at HP. Burnout sets the item offline and cancels saved active bonuses, pending local repairs, remote repair/assistance, EWAR and tackle rows in the same transaction. It preserves paid cooldowns, capacitor, consumed fuel and launched ordnance. Rollback restores damage, online state and activity together. Restart reconciles a burned-out saved item with an incorrectly online flag before normal use. Live cycle producers and their native stop notifications must still be integrated before this can become an operational overheating feature.

Station `repairSvc` quotes now report a damaged assembled module's actual damage and HP. Repairs use Eden's existing **1 ISK per damage point** pricing policy, including each quote's native upward damage rounding. One budget fraction applies to every selected damaged ship layer and module. Wallet debit, module condition, ship repair, receipt and journal settle atomically; rejected ownership, location, service or payment leaves them unchanged. Healthy/zero-budget selections cannot consume a payment. Full repair deletes the condition row but never automatically brings a burned-out module online. Ship-only receipt JSON retains its historical array shape; module/mixed receipts store named ship and module lists.

Dogma module priming reads current damage attribute **3** outside the cached fitting graph, including specialized weapon/mining/booster readers. Station repair publishes typed `OnModuleAttributeChanges` with the remaining damage. Charge crystal wear and drone condition retain their separate owners. A condition-only change does not rebuild fitting; committed burnout invalidates online/active bonuses through the existing inventory and activation hooks. This adds a point lookup during module attribute reads, not a new simulation monitor.

### Automated validation

The first exact regression filter was `FullyQualifiedName=Eden.Protocol.Tests.Thermal.ModuleConditionTests.PaidModuleRepairQuotesActualDamageAndKeepsConditionAfterRestart` (**1 passed**, test command **4.83 seconds**). The final affected filter was:

## Overload fitting composition and Thermodynamics, November 7, 2026

Milestone 6 now evaluates explicit overload selections within the same typed fitting graph as earned skills, hull bonuses, loaded scripts and active module-to-ship modifiers. The internal cycle-calculation input admits only owned fitted working online modules with validated overload profiles and the required Thermodynamics level. An unselected module retains its ordinary values. This is a calculation boundary for the future paid-cycle owner: native overload commands and automatic heat generation/damage are still disabled. Saved-world schema remains **106** and every production overheating check remains false.

Thermodynamics (28164) is now a relevant fitting skill. Pinned fitted effects **3195/3196** scale attribute **1229** by the trained level and apply it to fitted module heat damage (**1211**) through the ship's location selector. Base bonus **-5% per level** changes damage, independently of rack heat absorption/generation. Cargo and loose hangar equipment do not receive that ship-location modifier. Training invalidates the existing fitting cache and updates the effective damage and skill brain together. The exact native skill program, phases and selectors are checked; the general passive parser still rejects arbitrary `LocationModifier` programs.

Native brain serialization adds selector **`L`**, without extras, for that ship-wide modifier. Inert inspection of build 3662168's `eve/common/script/dogma/effect.pyj` confirms that `ApplyBrainEffect['L']` calls `AddLocationModifierWithSource`. The existing new-style `BrainEffect` wire class and Blue codec are unchanged. The archive SHA-256 is **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. No game module-level code or client UI was executed.

Explicit cycle calculations bypass ordinary module and ship caches and cannot publish their temporary values into those caches, including a cold cache and a successful transaction commit. Overload source attributes are evaluated inside the graph, so scripted tracking/range bonuses propagate through the active computer to its weapon, resistance bonuses propagate through an active hardener to the ship, and eligible Raven torpedo-launcher bonuses compose with the earned hull rate-of-fire reduction at actual catalog fitting budgets. Optional neutral missile-guidance attributes use their native defaults when overload introduces the modifier. All 19 native overload programs have real fitted composition cases, in addition to the existing 1,826-profile matrix.

The next integration must retain paid cycle boundaries and stable burnout ordering rather than simply toggling a permanent fitting overlay. Further inert inspection distinguishes the network calls from client-local simulation: Godma's `StateManager.Overload(itemID,effectID)` first calls `GetDogmaLM().Overload`, then updates its pending flag and local dogma effect. `OverloadRack(itemID)` receives the selected module IDs from the server before applying local state. Paste repair uses `InitiateModuleRepair(itemID)` and `StopModuleRepair(itemID)`; its native UI estimates completion from damage divided by character `moduleRepairRate` (**1267**, default **10 HP/minute**). These findings are evidence for unfinished lifecycle/payment integration, not enabled commands. The [historical heat research](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/issues/12) remains supporting evidence for later stochastic damage work, not a substitute for establishing pinned cycle and cancellation behavior.

## Resident rack heat and paid cycles, November 7, 2026

Milestone 6 now has a world-owned temperature lifecycle for paid overload contributions. High, medium and low racks use the pinned scalar heat curve with their own capacities, dissipation and the sum of current effective absorption rates. Rigs do not contribute a rack. Contributions stop at their saved completion deadlines even when advancement is late, then the rack cools for the rest of the elapsed interval. Overlapping contributions use their actual intervals. Exact cycle keys prevent duplicate registration and stale cancellation; fractional filetime deadlines and clock regressions preserve ordering. Cooling below the native cutoff resets in the same tick when the final source stops. Profile/capacity changes preserve existing heat and apply the new rate at the mutation boundary.

This checkpoint does **not** enable native overload commands. Temperature completions carry their exact rack heat and can be advanced one deadline at a time, allowing the future damage owner to settle a burnout and remove its source before continuing a late frame. Stochastic damage/spread, the remaining paid module producers, native module/rack pending states and notifications, scoped burnout cancellation, resource resizing and paste repair remain open. No temperature-only path is exposed to players, and every production overheating check remains false.

Saved-world schema **107** adds `WorldShipHeat`, with a cascading ship identity and a validated named snapshot containing rack profiles, heat, timestamps and the remaining paid contributions. Older saves gain an empty table, and malformed timelines fail initialization. Pure immutable Gameplay snapshots separate temperature calculations from SQLite and wire models. The typed `IPilotShipHeat` reader authorizes current ownership; cold foreign reads reject before fitting work. Idle healthy reads do not create saved heat, and unchanged saved idle reads do not queue projection. Ownership, fitting, skill, membership, module condition and catalog changes invalidate their actual resident inputs; ordinary coordinate writes do not force heat hydration. Catalog changes preserve unprojected heat rather than reloading an older SQL value.

The resident owner advances active/cooling ships before subsequent world work, independently of a particular client frame. A newly paid contribution is published to that owner only after its transaction commits. Startup restores saved active/cooling ships before frames begin; neither path requires a heat read to start advancement. Warm reads perform no SQL or fitting calculations. Unheated idle entries are bounded and excluded from background advancement; their invalidated profiles reload only when requested. Dirty temperature snapshots project before SQL transaction boundaries, survive rollback of a later command and remain pending after failed saves. Checkpoints capture immutable heat and the relational image from one generation, then project heat and combat together in one transaction outside the owner. A heat-projection failure rolls back earlier combat projection, including spent ammunition in the detached image, and can retry the same captured generation.

An internal shield-booster producer exercises the real operation: the selected temporary fitting graph supplies overloaded amount/duration, and capacitor or ancillary charges, shield restoration, cooldown and heat contribution commit together. A rejected heat write rolls all of them back. Both ordinary and ancillary shield boosters have automated operation cases at actual fitting budgets; thermal cancellation does not refund their payment. The public shield-booster operation retains its ordinary behavior and cannot register heat. The internal overload producer still prepares a temporary fitting graph per call; player activation integration must reuse effective cycle profiles by fitting/overload generation before enablement. This is one producer, not coverage of every active family.

Pinned temperature evidence remains the [native calculation record](#overheating-calculation-foundation-november-7-2026), and fitting/skill composition remains in the [preceding checkpoint](#overload-fitting-composition-and-thermodynamics-november-7-2026). Carbon Destiny's [server ticker at 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072/python/destiny/net/server/_baseticker.py) was consulted for its ordered world-update boundary. No Destiny timestamps, bubble updates or packet formats change; dogma temperature follows the pinned client instead of importing movement tick arithmetic.

## Nanite repair calculations and character skills, November 7, 2026

Milestone 6 now prepares typed nanite repair quotes from actual saved module damage and the earned fitting graph. This checkpoint enables the two Nanite skill modifiers and their native character brain effects. It does **not** enable `InitiateModuleRepair`, `InitiateModuleRepairMany` or `StopModuleRepair`, consume paste, schedule repairs, heal module damage or declare overheating complete. Payment, progress/cancellation, restart behavior, activation exclusion and the native repair lifecycle remain open alongside damage/spread and the remaining overload producers.

Pinned build **3662168**, archive SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**, supplies the pricing evidence. In `eve/common/script/dogma/baseDogmaLocation.pyj`, `FindNumUnitsToRepairModule` uses `inventoryUtil.GetComputedBasePrice(typeID, 1)` rather than the module's listed base price. That helper uses the static type-material recipe, truncates material unit prices and divides by material portion size in both the unit and final material calculation. It falls back to the item's base price/portion only when no recipe exists. Material rows with zero portion are skipped; an item's zero portion uses one in the fallback path. Paste cost is the ceiling of damage times computed value/HP times character `shipBrokenModuleRepairCostMultiplier` (**1277**), divided by paste type **28668**'s base price (**6,500 ISK**), without dividing by the paste's own production portion. These are static valuation inputs, not market order prices.

The [native vector exporter](../../tools/Export-NativeNaniteVectors.py) constructs only the two isolated function objects, supplies inert typed-catalog adapters and math, and executes no client module or dogma class body. Its [reference matrix](../../tests/Eden.Protocol.Tests/Fixtures/NaniteRepairReference.json) covers all **2,766 admitted non-rig rack modules**, three earned cost levels per profile, and six extra truncation/fallback/zero-value cases. Pure Gameplay calculations match those native results. A zero computed value has a special native free-repair path; the typed quote reports zero units/duration while leaving actual healing to the future command. Reads never clear damage. Burned-out modules cannot receive paste quotes; station repair remains their implemented recovery path.

Nanite Operation (**28879**) applies its level-scaled **1294** bonus to character cost attribute **1277**; Nanite Interfacing (**28880**) applies its level-scaled **1295** bonus to character rate **1267**, whose native default is **10 HP/minute**. The four exact fitted programs (**3521**, **3522**, **3524**, **3723**) and the standard skill program are validated before their selectors enter the graph. Defaults and bonus values come from catalog metadata; trained levels come from saved skill points. Quotes use a filetime ceiling of damage divided by the effective HP/minute rate. The client UI's separate integer-millisecond estimate plus one-second stop-call grace is not a paste cost or a second repair-rate multiplier.

The native brain now sends character-domain fitting bonuses in `CharacterEffects`, addressed to the pilot with the native `M` form. Ship-domain and owner-required-skill forms retain their respective routing. This corrects the previous blanket routing of non-owner fitting bonuses to the ship and makes the earned Nanite modifiers reach the same character node used by server calculations. Training invalidates the existing fitting snapshot; the next evaluation refreshes once, and subsequent bonus reads reuse it. No module or hull admission changes, and no saved-world schema change is needed.

The [overload fixture exporter](../../tools/Export-OverloadCatalog.py) now includes Nanite skill prerequisites, exact material recipes and their price inputs for the existing native-program representatives and a passive-module case. Carbon Destiny revision **114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072** was checked for relevant repair ownership; this work follows the pinned dogma functions and does not copy movement/ticker behavior.

## Paid nanite repair runtime, November 7, 2026

Milestone 6 now has an internal typed paid repair operation and world-owned deadline processing. `IPilotNaniteRepair` starts explicit distinct module selections, stops an exact repair identity, and cancels an owning pilot/ship's work. This checkpoint does **not** bind `InitiateModuleRepair`, `InitiateModuleRepairMany` or `StopModuleRepair`. Native weapon-bank expansion, client-local repair flags, notification/controller cleanup and system-transfer integration still need their adapter. Overload toggles, damage/spread, remaining producers and live burnout orchestration also remain unfinished; production overheating flags stay false.

Saved-world schema **108** adds `WorldNaniteRepair`: one pending repair per module, with pilot/ship identity, a fresh generation, start/completion times, original damage/HP and paid paste quantity. Ship/item/pilot deletion cascades the schedule; the active generation is unique. A same-tick cancel/restart gets a new generation even when both timestamps match, so an old typed stop cannot cancel the replacement. Validated immutable Gameplay schedules clamp elapsed work at both boundaries, preserve fractional ticks, reject invalid profiles/keys and avoid clock-rewind credit. Quotes for positive repair work have at least one filetime tick; a verified zero-value repair remains immediate and free.

The internal payment contract spends the complete quoted amount from owned, unassembled paste stacks in the active ship's ordinary cargo, in stable item order. Hangar, fitted charges and foreign stacks do not fund repairs. Batch results separately name started modules, modules missing paste and rejected modules; a failed selection consumes no partial item quantity. Damage, timer creation/removal and inventory changes commit together. Duplicate starts cannot spend again. Early cancellation applies only the elapsed fraction, without refund; late completion applies at most the original paid work, once. The paid rate and cost are fixed by the earned graph at start. Burned-out modules are neither paste-healed nor brought online. These are the implemented internal lifecycle rules; native payment/refund fidelity remains part of the unbound client lifecycle rather than a claim of observed CCP server behavior.

The world owner caches the active identities and next deadline. Empty worlds, unchanged active repairs and completed/idle frames need no polling SQL or fitting recalculation. Cache dependencies distinguish relevant module/ship/pilot changes from coordinates and unrelated item membership. Invalidation records the mutation's commit time, so a delayed frame after docking settles at the docking boundary rather than crediting time spent in station. The wreck transaction settles earned work before modules move or disappear; surviving item identities retain the resulting damage. Checkpoints copy the paid row, initial condition and consumed cargo together. Reopening settles bounded paid elapsed work once at the reopen clock, then removes the outstanding timer; it does not recreate the client's lost local timer or replay payment. This includes bounded paid work during an offline interval, not a second repair.

Current SQL activation owners and the resident turret/weapon-bank path reject repairing modules before publishing a paid activation. A bank rejects if any selected member is repairing. Other modules remain usable. Paid overload contributions and nanite repairs exclude each other for the ship. Mining now records its funded beam deadline and clears it on collection or cancellation, so repair checks can see active mining without depending on a socket controller. Stale controller stops cannot remove a replacement cycle from the handler's active map.

The preceding [pricing and skill record](#nanite-repair-calculations-and-character-skills-november-7-2026) remains the calculation evidence. Further inert inspection of pinned Godma shows that repair-many deconstructs **three** lists: success, missing charges and failed modules. The single-module path also has weapon-bank-specific failure handling; these must be preserved when the native calls are connected. Carbon Destiny's [ordered ticker at 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072/python/destiny/net/server/_baseticker.py) was consulted for update ordering. No movement arithmetic or packets change, and no repair math is taken from the ticker.

## Native nanite repair controls, November 7, 2026

Milestone 6 now binds `dogmaIM.InitiateModuleRepair`, `InitiateModuleRepairMany` and `StopModuleRepair` to the paid nanite runtime. The single call returns a boolean, or the named `NotEnoughRepairMaterialToFinishAllRepairs` error when paste is insufficient. The batch returns one tuple containing three Python lists: started module IDs, missing-paste IDs and failed IDs, in selection order. Invalid, repeated, empty and oversized selections cannot spend cargo. Successful payments use the shared inventory-change adapter, including exhausted-stack removal. Free immediate work publishes its resulting damage directly.

The scoped handler tracks exact paid generations, monitors world-owned completion, and sends native damage attributes once when work stops or completes. It checks live module controllers as well as saved activation guards: a repeating module remains busy at its paid deadline until its controller stops, even when dispatch happens before the next update. Unchanged active frames share an immutable schedule view and perform no SQL, connection opens or fitting calculations. Docking, gate initiation, developer travel and disconnect clear the session's work; a fresh session settles any orphaned paid schedule rather than replaying payment or restoring a lost client-local timestamp. If the world has already settled docking or expiry, cleanup reads the resulting condition without applying a second repair.

### Pinned native evidence

Inert code-object inspection used build **3662168** `tq/code.ccp`, SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**. No client module was executed and no game was launched. `eve/client/script/environment/godma.pyj`, `StateManager.RepairModule`, starts its local timestamp/thread only when the remote result is not false. `RepairManyModules` unpacks the three result lists and starts local timers for successes. `RepairModule_thread` waits for the damage/rate duration plus one second, then checks the original local timestamp before issuing a stop. `StopRepairModule` sends the remote stop and clears the local state/event itself. `ProcessSessionChange` stops its outstanding module repairs when processing the new session.

`eve/client/script/ui/inflight/shipModuleButton/shipmodulebutton.pyj`, `ModuleButton.RepairModule`, sends separate calls for the master and each slave. It catches the insufficient-material error while continuing other members. The server therefore repairs only the explicitly requested item; expanding the bank again would risk duplicate requests and payment. Automated tests exercise the master/slave calls separately. Inspection output is retained locally in `artifacts/overheating/paste-inspection.txt` and `artifacts/overheating/repair-lifecycle-inspection.txt`.

Carbon Destiny's [ordered ticker at 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072/python/destiny/net/server/_baseticker.py) was consulted for update ordering. Its ticker does not specify dogma repair pricing or payment/refund behavior. The preceding paid-runtime record remains the implemented payment contract; these client call shapes do not independently establish CCP server refund or offline-repair semantics.

## Paid repair-family overload cycles, November 7, 2026

The paid overload producer now extends from shield boosters to local armor/hull repairs and targeted remote shield/armor/hull repairs. Internal `StartOverloaded` paths select the actual module's overload program in the earned fitting graph, reuse that calculation for ship and module values, pay capacitor/fuel, and register the exact funded deadline with rack heat in the same transaction. Heat is published only after commit. Normal fitting snapshots retain their ordinary values; production protocol calls still select normal cycles. This checkpoint enables no player overload controls, damage approximation, new hull cohort or content-coverage flag.

Local repairs retain their paid completion amount and modified deadline in `PendingLocalRepair` records in the existing `WorldArmorRepair` table. Ancillary local armor repair and ancillary remote armor/shield repair use their existing native fuel rules after the overload modifiers: charged armor multiplies the modified base amount, and charged shield cycles preserve their zero capacitor cost. A failed heat write rolls back repair state, fuel, capacitor, cooldown changes and remote events together, preserving the previous reload deadline. A reopened local checkpoint retains its paid amount/deadline and heals once without another payment.

Mutadaptive repair uses the actual Rodiva fitting graph and completed paid chain. Overload amount and ramp multiply once; switching to a normal cycle preserves the chain while dropping the overload modifier, then a subsequent overloaded cycle uses its next ramp step. Remote cancellation now removes its matching paid heat source in the repair transaction at the host clock, preserving heat already generated and other funded sources. A failed cancellation preserves both the repair row and heat source. Ordinary shield assistance remains immediate; armor/hull assistance remains deferred to its paid completion. Cancelled deferred work cannot heal at its former deadline.

The calculation evidence remains the build-3662168 catalog programs and pinned client temperature function recorded in the [overheating foundation](#overheating-calculation-foundation-november-7-2026). Carbon Destiny's [ordered ticker at 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072/python/destiny/net/server/_baseticker.py) remains the update-order reference. The [original EOS heat research](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/issues/12) was reviewed for the remaining damage/spread work; no stochastic damage formula or approximation is enabled by this producer checkpoint.

## Paid propulsion and tackle overload cycles, November 8, 2026

Milestone 6's internal paid producer paths now include admitted afterburners, microwarpdrives, webifiers, warp scramblers and warp disruptors. The active pilot's earned module graph supplies the selected overload program. Propulsion retains the existing navigation-skill calculations, pays for the modified cycle and returns its effective speed/mass inputs; MWD signature bloom still follows the ordinary active marker. Tackle pays for the modified range and persists that same profile for pilot/NPC consumers, retaining ordinary strength, web percent and MWD blocking. Heat, payment, cooldowns, effects and aggression commit together, and heat is published only after commit. Production calls still select ordinary cycles; no player overload toggle, new admission or overheating coverage flag is enabled.

Propulsion stop now removes its current paid source with the active marker. Tackle stop, source disconnect, source/target travel and invalid-source/target sweep remove their matching heat source with the effect row. The host clock is captured once for bulk cancellation. Already-generated heat and paid cooldowns remain; stopped sources cool rather than continuing to heat. Incoming target travel can remove another pilot's outgoing source through the source row's actual ownership. An unrelated pilot's explicit stop cannot remove an owned source. Shared `ShipHeatPersistence.CancelSource` supports current-effect removal and exact paid-key removal; stale expected keys leave the current source intact. The preceding remote-repair stop uses that same exact-key helper without changing its payment contract.

Rejected heat insertion rolls back capacitor, active/tackle effects, cooldowns and aggression. Rejected effect removal preserves both the effect and its heat source. Nanite work on another fitted module excludes a mobility overload cycle for the ship, and missing Thermodynamics, a second active propulsion module and a non-heatable core stabilizer cannot pay for heat. Detached snapshots retain effect, cooldown and heat from the same generation even after a later live cancellation. Reopening clears connection-owned effects, retains the paid cooldown, and bounds heat by the captured paid deadline under the existing restore policy.

The build-3662168 catalog programs and pinned temperature function remain the [calculation evidence](#overheating-calculation-foundation-november-7-2026). Carbon Destiny's [ordered ticker at 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072/python/destiny/net/server/_baseticker.py) was consulted for ordered authoritative updates. No movement packet, heat-damage approximation or client overload command is introduced.

## Paid electronic and remote support overload cycles, November 8, 2026

Milestone 6's internal paid producers now include player ECM, dampeners, tracking/guidance disruption and target painting; remote tracking/sensor assistance; and capacitor transfers, neutralizers and nosferatu. Each selected module uses its earned overload graph for the funded cycle. EWAR and assistance persist that profile in their recipient contact, including compatible scripts. Capacitor modules use the modified duration while retaining their established payment, percentage, impedance and pool rules. Payment, effects, cooldowns, aggression and rack heat commit together; heat and capacitor events publish after commit. Normal calls still select ordinary cycles. No player overload control, new admission or production overheating flag is enabled.

Stop and travel remove stored effects and paid heat together, preserving spent resources, generated temperature and cooldowns. EWAR incoming cancellation and invalid-target sweep now also remove the corresponding source activation marker. Remote assistance retains its existing mutual deletion triggers between effect and marker; the store deletes the effect once rather than scheduling both deletions through EF. The heat owner observes assistance membership changes and validates its exact live source key against the remaining projected effect. Recipient docking/system transfer and source burnout therefore stop heat at the physical cleanup boundary, even when the next read arrives later. Ordinary coordinate changes do not add polling work. The existing cold restore policy for bounded paid heat remains unchanged.

The recipient receives the actual overloaded bonuses: sensor assistance changes targeting attributes, and a scripted tracking link changes the recipient's fitted weapon. Stop restores the weapon's baseline. Normal/overloaded repeat cycles select their respective profiles without accumulating an overlay. Capacitor transfers/drains update both pilot pools once; rejected duplicate starts cannot repeat the transfer or publish another event. Rejected heat insertion rolls back pools, effects and cooldowns; rejected removal preserves the effect and heat source. Foreign stops and missing Thermodynamics leave paid state unchanged.

The [overheating foundation](#overheating-calculation-foundation-november-7-2026) remains the build-3662168 catalog and pinned temperature evidence. Carbon Destiny's [ordered ticker at 114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/114e89fa584f0e1a81eb0a679aabf41af8870072/python/destiny/net/server/_baseticker.py) remains the authoritative-update ordering reference. No packet shape, movement arithmetic or stochastic damage approximation changes.

## Burnout and charged ship resources, November 8, 2026

When damage takes an online module offline, the owning transaction advances shield or capacitor charge to the burnout time using the old fitting and recharge duration. It then carries the charged fraction to the new capacity. A recharge-duration change alone also starts a new interval. Other resource timestamps and raw armor and structure damage survive the transition. An absent balance represents a full resource under both fits. Rolling back restores condition, online state and charge together. Reopening an older save with a burned-out module still marked online applies the same transition at reconciliation time.

The staged build-3662168 archive matched SHA-256 `37fb2af4fa0ec5c8fa320f34cd28c8b7dbf4917418336b38cba20a7860db0368`. Inert inspection of `dogma/attributes/chargedAttribute.pyj` confirmed that `ChargedAttribute.Update` rescales the stored charge by its previous capacity and uses the exponential recharge curve. Eden uses its existing fitting mutation boundary to settle elapsed time. [Pinned Carbon Destiny movement ticker](https://github.com/carbonengine/destiny/blob/27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f/src/Ballpark.cpp)

A scan of all 12,809 archive bytecode entries found the heat attenuation attribute name in dogma constants and QA type validation. It supplied no server damage roll or simultaneous-burnout ordering. The historical experiments describe end-of-cycle heat, physical slot distance and rack occupancy, with uncertainty around offline slots. Damage propagation remains evidence-blocked. The next independent work in milestone 6 is cancelling active controllers for the affected module or weapon bank and sending native overload notifications. [Historical heat experiments](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/issues/12)

The coverage ledger now credits Nanite Repair Paste for both ancillary armor fuel and cargo-funded module repairs. The paid repair check requires the production paste type and its valid catalog price. Module overheating remains partial.

## Complete overheating and native controls, November 8, 2026

Milestone 6 is implemented for the existing admitted content. All **1,826 admitted heatable profiles** now connect native module/rack selection to the previously verified paid producers, earned overload modifiers, resident rack heat, adjacent damage, burnout, native notifications and saved condition. Station repair and cargo-funded nanite repair complete the condition lifecycle. This closes the earlier checkpoint limitations in this topic. Other milestones and hull admission remain separately tracked.

### Evidence and explicit ordering policy

The user supplied [EVE University's Overheating guide](https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Overheating). Its controls, end-of-cycle damage model, occupancy, slot distance and repair behavior provide the remaining gameplay reference. The [original heat experiments mirrored by EOS](https://github.com/pyfa-org/eos/issues/12) independently describe the probability factors. The implementation uses those measured rules with the pinned catalog's values. It does not claim exact CCP random draws or undocumented event ordering.

For each completed paid source cycle, the target damage probability is `rackHeat / 100 * onlineModules / totalSlots * attenuation^distance`. The numerator counts online, unburnt high/medium/low modules across the ship. The denominator includes all high/medium/low slots and rig slots. Distance is the absolute physical slot difference in the same rack, with no wrapping. Offline modules remain damage targets and are excluded from the numerator. Each successful trial applies the source's effective heat damage from the earned fitting graph, so Thermodynamics is applied once. Raw rack temperature retains the pinned native calculation and is not rounded for display or shared observation.

Sources at a shared completion deadline resolve in ascending module item ID, with targets in physical slot order. A source burnt by an earlier completion cannot contribute later damage. This deterministic ordering is an Eden policy where the evidence is silent. A persisted random seed and paid cycle/target identity derive stable SplitMix64 trials. Duplicate observers, late frames and retries cannot reroll a paid interval. The persisted trial identity makes stochastic gameplay reproducible.

The native evidence remains build **3662168**, archive SHA-256 **37fb2af4fa0ec5c8fa320f34cd28c8b7dbf4917418336b38cba20a7860db0368**. Inert inspection of `slotsContainer.pyj.CheckOverloadRackBtnState` confirms that a rack command's anchor is its first module, which can be passive. `PilotOverloadStore` therefore validates the anchor's physical rack, then selects its eligible heatable members. `StateManager.Overload` ignores the single-call return and tracks a local pending flag. Rack replies preserve the native Python list of module IDs. Category-5 `OnGodmaShipEffect` events clear pending start/stop flags. `ProcessPendingOverloadUpdate` remains a local client event and is not invented as a network notification. The prior [native overload record](#scoped-burnout-cancellation-and-overload-notifications-november-8-2026) retains the source files and field evidence. The Carbon organization was consulted again and supplies no authoritative server heat-damage implementation. The existing Destiny reference remains revision `27ee40b38a563d29054b32f64c3ebc87fabaec1f`.

### Native controls and authoritative settlement

`Overload`, `StopOverload`, `OverloadRack` and `StopOverloadRack` validate active ship ownership, fitted membership, native effect identity, online/condition state, Thermodynamics and paste-repair exclusion. Single selection expands an existing weapon bank. Rack selection handles each eligible physical member, including a passive anchor. Invalid commands preserve requests, resources and inventory.

Preheating affects the next paid activation. A running ordinary cycle retains its original payment and bonuses until its next cycle. Stop-overload retains an already funded hot cycle through its deadline. If an ordinary cycle ends without repeating, a pending hot selection becomes inactive preheat and receives the native acknowledgement. Ship primes include effective online state, current module damage, selected overload effects and unrounded rack heat/absorption. Repeated commands and observation preserve funded cycle identity.

The world owner advances damage without a connected observer. Nonfatal trials remain resident and warm observation incurs zero SQL commands, connection opens and fitting calculations. Burnout atomically commits damage, offline state, heat and activity removal, then clears the overload request. Controller cancellation stops the affected active module or the whole turret/missile bank before repetition. Paid capacitor, fuel, cooldown and launched projectiles retain their established ownership. Shield/capacitor capacity changes settle at the exact burnout deadline and preserve charge fractions. Native damage, offline and overload-stop notices carry the resulting state and funded stop time.

Ordinary position updates preserve preheat, including warp movement. Docking, system transitions, developer translocation and session cleanup clear selection. Travel clears paid sources in its own transaction, with registry cleanup after commit. A rejected travel transaction preserves preheat. Moving or repairing an item invalidates the resident profile without erasing condition.

Heat JSON now includes module profiles and the damage seed, with compatible defaults for old records. Checkpoints capture temperature and condition from one generation. A detached earlier checkpoint retains the earlier online/undamaged state even if the running world later burns out a module. Cold reopen settles retained temperature and paid deadlines without retroactively running combat controllers or drawing damage for offline elapsed time. Saved damage survives independently of charge/crystal wear. A rejected burnout write rolls back condition, online state and heat together, leaving the same paid trial available for retry.

### Partial nanite material settlement

Paste is reserved from owned cargo at repair start. Cancellation repairs the earned HP and charges the ceiling of earned HP times the saved unrounded material rate, returning the unused reservation once. New material rates are frozen with the repair, so later catalog/skill changes cannot alter that reservation. Completion consumes the quoted total. Returned cargo uses existing stack capacity or creates owned stacks, and emits native inventory changes. Travel, disconnect and restart use the same settlement. The source ship receives the return while owned, with the pilot's active ship as fallback after ownership loss. Returning reserved assets can exceed the current cargo capacity.

World schema **112** adds `PasteUnitsPerDamage` to paid repair rows. Legacy rows default to zero and retain their original prepaid contract because their unrounded start-time rate cannot be reconstructed reliably. This avoids a migration-created grant. A failed paste return rolls back repair progress, material changes and schedule removal, and retry settles once. Stale generation keys cannot stop replacement repairs.

The regenerated production ledger preserves the source fingerprint, all **6,079 entries**, all admitted IDs and every capability other than heating. Exactly **1,826 overheating checks** become implemented and **1,762 records** become Complete. The other heatable entries retain separate missing capabilities. Totals remain **235 admitted hulls**, **3,408 modules/rigs**, **801 charges** and **76 combat drones**.

## CPU and power-grid skill budgets, November 9, 2026

The reported Catalyst fit showed **49.5 / 222.5 CPU remaining**, while the online rejection used **5 / 178** and required **15 CPU** for a 1MN Afterburner II. Both views imply 173 CPU already used. The missing 44.5 CPU is precisely CPU Management V's 25% bonus on 178. This screenshot is user-provided evidence, not agent live acceptance.

Eden applied CPU Management and Power Grid Management in its authoritative `FittingRules.Apply` and sent the resulting absolute output attributes to Godma, but omitted those skills from `PilotBrain` ship effects. Static inspection confirms that `clientDogmaLocation.OnlineModule` calls local `Activate` before remote `SetModuleOnline`. The local graph therefore rejected a legal fit before the request reached Eden. `ClientDogmaInstanceManager.GetFittingDogmaLocation` supplies `_GetBrainForGhostFitting` to the simulator; that method reuses the current character brain or reads `GetAllInfo.charInfo`. Simulation had the same missing budgets.

The two resource-output skills now share named Gameplay definitions between authoritative calculations and native brain generation. Earned levels emit one ship item modifier (`M`, post-percent operation 6, no extras, source skill ID) per trained skill, at 5% per level. Zero-level skills emit no bonus. Existing `GetAllInfo` and versioned `OnServerBrainUpdated` paths carry these effects. No saved skills, module costs or resource limits are changed, and genuine overload remains rejected.

Pinned catalog types **3426 / CPU Management** and **3413 / Power Grid Management** supply 5 through attributes **424 / 313**; effects **368 / 218** multiply those sources by skill level, and effects **397 / 490** apply ship item post-percent modifiers to output attributes **48 / 11**.

Sources: inert inspection of staged build-3662168 `code.ccp`, SHA-256 **37FB2AF4FA0EC5C8FA320F34CD28C8B7DBF4917418336B38CBA20A7860DB0368**, specifically `clientDogmaLocation.OnlineModule` / `Activate`, `baseDogmaLocation._StartModuleOnlineEffect`, and `clientDogmaIM.GetFittingDogmaLocation` / `_GetBrainForGhostFitting`. The [EVE University fitting guide](https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Fitting_ships) and [support skills reference](https://wiki.eveuniversity.org/Support_skills) confirm whole-ship CPU and power-grid bonuses; the pinned implementation remains the build-specific reference. Consulted [Carbon Blue Marshal.cpp at af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997](https://github.com/carbonengine/blue/blob/af0d65b29f951260ea09140ca11adf5d40566997/src/Marshal.cpp) for the existing NEWOBJ/dictionary representation. This fix reuses that adapter and changes no codec or movement behavior.

**Automated validation.** The exact-method filter `FullyQualifiedName=Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.PilotBrainTests.EarnedFittingOutputSkillsMatchTheAuthoritativeBudget|FullyQualifiedName=Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.PilotFittingTests.NativeFittingBudgetAllowsEarnedCapacityAndRejectsRealOverload` passed all **12 cases** (4.23 seconds build, 9.17 seconds test command). Coverage includes earned levels 0 through V, encoded gray-matter round trips, real-world GetAllInfo budgets, successful online notifications, true CPU/power-grid rejection without state changes, and reopened online state. An initial test assertion used CLR integer/string types for decoded Blue values; it was corrected to assert their actual wire representations before the passing run. Final affected validation passed **74 cases** in 32.33 seconds. The OR-combined filter selected `FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.Persistence.` with each of `PilotBrainTests`, `PilotFittingTests`, `PassiveFittingTests`, `EquipmentBonusTests`, `ModuleFittingTests`, `CatalogFittingReadTests`, `ShipRigTests`, and `FittingConstraintTests`, plus `FullyQualifiedName~Eden.Protocol.Tests.PilotSessions.FreeSkillPointServiceTests`. Tool restore (0.25 seconds), solution restore (0.68 seconds), build (5.26 seconds, zero warnings/errors), catalog policy (8 cases, 1.42 seconds), formatting policy (9 cases, 0.19 seconds), and direct `Format.ps1 -Check` (28.35 seconds) passed. The first formatting wrapper passed its switch as a positional string and did not execute verification; the direct invocation above completed it. The full .NET suite was not run.

**Live acceptance.** Not performed; agent client testing is disabled. Native fitting and simulation presentation remain unverified.

**Optional maintainer checks.** After updating the server and reconnecting, online the reported afterburner and reopen the same simulated fit to compare CPU/power-grid budgets.
